# Quarry v2 and the Verevon in-app browser: audit of Codex, Aside, Firecrawl and Apify

*Audit T2, as of 2026-10-14. Scope: Quarry v2 (Ingestion Plane web acquisition and browser execution), integration-corev2 where it overlaps, the Model Plane browser control path and the Verevon v3 browser UI, compared with the Codex desktop app in-app browser, the Aside browser, the Firecrawl repository and the Apify platform. Status: read-only audit; nothing in the product or the reference apps was changed.*

## How to read this document

**Tiers.** Every recommendation sits in one of three tiers.

- **PRIORITY** = do first: gaps that block or visibly undercut the vision, table-stakes capabilities every comparable reference has and Verevon lacks or half has, real safety, security or data-governance risks, and high-leverage unique capabilities with acceptable effort.
- **RECOMMENDED** = clear evidenced value that supports the vision but does not block it, larger efforts that need sequencing, and differentiators worth adopting after the priority set.
- **NICE TO HAVE** = polish, niche, speculative or modest value.

**Provenance tags.** Every factual sentence about a reference app, repo or product ends with a tag: [REA] observed through REA (reverse-engineering analysis) of the shipped app; [SRC] read directly in source code or shipped files, including beautified copies of shipped bundles; [DOC] documentation only, internal or public; [INF] inference, with the basis stated. A claim that rests only on public docs or on inference says so.

**Verevon status vocabulary.** Absent (nothing exists), Partial (some code exists but the capability does not work end to end or is unwired), Stub (a type, module or route exists with no real behaviour behind it), Planned (a dated plan exists in the repo and no code). "Shipped" appears only in the baseline counts, the strengths section and the common-ground tables. Every Verevon status in this document was verified by reading the CoresSystem code at commit afb83928 (2026-10-10). Verevon claims cite paths relative to the repo root, for example `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:446-451`.

**Effort vocabulary.** S, M, L and XL are relative sizes for one team; XL items span several planes and usually need an ADR first. Efforts are the verified values, which replaced the first estimates for 33 items. Where an item is split into slices, the item text gives the size of each slice.

**Ids and anchors.** Each recommendation has a stable id of the form F-t2-NNN. Its heading carries an HTML anchor with that id, so a link such as [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) jumps to it from anywhere in the document. Prevalence is written "n of 4 references", except in the section 2 tables for the two browser categories (2.8 and 2.9), which count out of 3 because Apify, documented only, has no browser shell to compare.

**Waves.** W1 to W5 are delivery waves, not dates. W1 unblocks and makes safe; W2 reaches table-stakes parity; W3 adds governed depth; W4 is platform and leverage; W5 is polish and long-tail bets.

**Verified.** Every item passed a code-verification pass on the Verevon side (status, gap, effort and approach on each card come from it), and the 24 Priority items also passed a reference-side check of the cited reference behaviour and prevalence; section 1.6 summarises both. Appendix A gives the method and limits, Appendix B indexes all 77 items, Appendix C logs the reference-side corrections and Appendix D lists what code verification changed.

## 1. Executive summary

### 1.1 Goal

Verevon's web layer has two halves that must become one product: an acquisition API (scrape, crawl, batch, search, monitors, schedules) modelled on Firecrawl and Apify, and a remote, leased Chromium that users and the Model share, modelled on the Codex in-app browser and Aside. This audit reads the four references and the Verevon code at commit afb83928 to answer three questions: what every comparable product has that Verevon lacks or half has, what each reference does uniquely well and is worth adopting, and which Verevon strengths must be protected while the gaps close. The result is 77 recommendations in three tiers, five waves, and ten decisions that need an architecture decision record (ADR) before building.

### 1.2 Where Verevon stands today

The code-verified baseline holds 260 Verevon capabilities across ten categories: 142 shipped, 52 partial, 22 stub and 44 absent [SRC]. The picture is uneven. Integration-plane overlap is the strongest category (14 of 18 shipped), followed by output formats and content processing (16 of 26) and AI-browser control (22 of 38). The weakest are rendering and browser engines (9 of 24 shipped, 8 partial, 3 stub, 4 absent), orchestration and scale (8 of 20) and platform and ecosystem (10 of 19, with 5 absent). The user-facing browser shell is in the middle (19 of 34 shipped, 7 absent) [SRC].

Two readings follow. First, Verevon is ahead of every reference on search routing with Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) gating, SSRF and egress containment, governed one-action-per-call execution, and evidence (step receipts, checkpoints, proof bundles, replay) [SRC]. Second, most of what is missing already exists as code that nothing calls: crawl scoping and robots enforcement, scrape actions, the DOCX parser, the retry helper, the secret redactor, the Temporal client and the LLM planner are all built and unwired [SRC]. After verification, 61 of the 77 recommendations are Partial, 11 Absent, 3 Stub and 2 Planned; none is already done.

### 1.3 Headline findings

1. **No browser path works in production mode.** The gateway starts sessions without a grant and sends `session:{id}` as the grant id, which production Quarry rejects with 403; BrowserBroker implements grants only on an in-memory store and no production code calls AcquireGrant (`apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:483-506`, `:1235`; `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:1377-1381`) [SRC]. See [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001).
2. **The gateway search cache is a confirmed cross-tenant and ZDR hole.** Its key carries no org and entries are stored without a ZDR check, while the Quarry search it fronts uses org-scoped private-corpus providers (`apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/search.rs:195-231`, `:285`; `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/search_routes.rs:495-530`) [SRC]. See [F-t2-003](#F-t2-003).
3. **Human takeover is enforced by the gateway, not by Quarry.** The Quarry step handler never reads `control_mode`, so a Model Plane run that calls Quarry directly keeps acting after the user takes over (`apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:2222-2310`) [SRC]. See [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013).
4. **Budgets are a contract, not an enforcement.** Only `max_steps` is enforced on the step path; cost and runtime are unbounded, browser time is not metered, and a crawl with `max_pages` 0 is unlimited (`agent_routes.rs:433-451`; `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-orchestrator/internal/workflows/workflows.go:456`) [SRC]. See [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008) and [F-t2-006](#F-t2-006).
5. **Crawl has no scope and no robots check.** The live crawl workflow enqueues every href after a depth check; CrawlFrontier, CrawlDenialReason and RobotsCache have no production caller (`workflows.go:385-620`; `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:817-840`) [SRC]. See [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016).
6. **The scrape contract is thin and the ZDR scrape path returns nothing.** `/v1/scrape` has no `formats[]`, no main-content controls and no unknown-field rejection; output is artifact references only, and under ZDR the pipeline writes no artifacts and returns html and markdown as None (`routes.rs:345-375`; `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/pipeline.rs:597-601`) [SRC]. See [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005).
7. **Raw script evaluation is ungated inside Quarry.** The sensitive-action gate covers only dialogs, uploads and downloads, and `evaluate` runs in the page's main world; the Model Plane side already refuses to offer it, so exposure is direct service callers (`agent_routes.rs:276-310`; `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:3003-3017`) [SRC]. See [F-t2-002](#F-t2-002).
8. **The shared user-plus-AI browser is half built.** Every user session is pinned to its first host with 12 steps and 120 seconds, the chat-side panel is read-only with no live frame, and no Model Plane run can attach to a user's session (`browser.rs:483-490`; `apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/lib/chat-run-watch.ts:402-452`) [SRC]. See [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011), [F-t2-012](#F-t2-012) and [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013).
9. **Secrets reach receipts, snapshots and screenshots unmasked, and the model would see any credential it types.** AX values are copied verbatim, typed text is persisted in step receipts, `redact_sensitive` has no caller, and there is no `fill_secret` action or secret store reachable from Quarry (`chromiumoxide.rs:1096-1130`; `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/step_receipts.rs:185-200`) [SRC]. See [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021) and [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024).
10. **One shared Chromium serves every session and restarts on a viewport mismatch.** Any viewport change closes the browser and clears pages, egress receipts and dialogs for all sessions; the agent path has no pool, cap, idle reaper or absolute TTL (`chromiumoxide.rs:185-270`; `agent_routes.rs:1425-1505`) [SRC]. See [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007) and [F-t2-006](#F-t2-006).

All 24 Priority items. Status and effort are the verified values.

[F-t2-024](#F-t2-024) is Priority but lands in W3 because it depends on [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001), [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013), [F-t2-015](#F-t2-015) and [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021).

### 1.5 Decisions leadership must make

Ten topics need an ADR before the items that depend on them start. Each must respect the plane rules: no cross-plane database access, the Model proposes and Quarry executes or rejects, ZDR propagates, and Space access never widens an owner-plane ACL.

1. **Web policy of record and tighten-only claims.** Whether Control Plane org-core or Model Plane capability-core owns disabled, allowed and forced capability policy, delivered to Quarry only as signed claims. Items: [F-t2-022](#F-t2-022), [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032), [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008), [F-t2-025](#F-t2-025).
2. **Secret custody for browser credential use.** integration-corev2 holds secrets, Quarry resolves and fills them, and the Model sees only opaque references; no secret material may enter receipts or telemetry. Items: [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024), [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021).
3. **Shared human-plus-agent sessions.** How a grant binds a shared session, the control lease, and a broker-defined navigation policy for free human browsing that is not a single-host allowlist. Items: [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001), [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011), [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013), [F-t2-054](#F-t2-054).
4. **ZDR semantics per feature.** For caches, monitors, schedules, durable crawls and telemetry: refused, inline-only or hash-only, published as a ZDR capability matrix. Items: [F-t2-003](#F-t2-003), [F-t2-004](#F-t2-004), [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016), [F-t2-018](#F-t2-018), [F-t2-030](#F-t2-030), [F-t2-048](#F-t2-048).
5. **Anti-bot posture.** Confirm the honest-identity stance, decide whether to delete the stealth code, and define what policy-gated proxy escalation and robots override are allowed. Items: [F-t2-027](#F-t2-027), [F-t2-066](#F-t2-066), [F-t2-067](#F-t2-067), [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016), [F-t2-058](#F-t2-058).
6. **Script execution model.** Whether evaluate, scrape actions, code mode and generated extractors are acceptable when every binding call is still validated and executed by Quarry under grants and caps. Items: [F-t2-002](#F-t2-002), [F-t2-023](#F-t2-023), [F-t2-063](#F-t2-063), [F-t2-065](#F-t2-065).
7. **External agent exposure over MCP (Model Context Protocol).** Reverses the documented deferral; needs audience-bound OAuth and a ZDR-aware tool-call log. Items: [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064), [F-t2-042](#F-t2-042).
8. **Run trace envelope and sharing.** Model Plane trace versus Application Plane projections and Space grants, without letting a Space member see more than the owning plane allows. Items: [F-t2-014](#F-t2-014), [F-t2-038](#F-t2-038), [F-t2-072](#F-t2-072).
9. **Automation owner.** Application Plane versus Model Plane for completion-triggered follow-ups and routines, with per-fire browser grants for unattended runs. Items: [F-t2-031](#F-t2-031), [F-t2-061](#F-t2-061).
10. **Browser execution substrate.** Shared Chromium pool, per-session containers or VMs, governed remote providers, or a user-browser extension that gives up containment and ZDR evidence. Items: [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007), [F-t2-059](#F-t2-059), [F-t2-060](#F-t2-060), [F-t2-077](#F-t2-077).

The open questions that matter most, beyond the ADRs: was any org-private search result ever served across orgs (verification found that the Quarry search behind the gateway cache uses org-scoped private-corpus providers, so the cache is a cross-tenant exposure today); what idle timeout and navigation policy a human browsing session gets; whether enterprise tenants need a container or VM per browser session; and which claims need runtime confirmation first, namely the 401/403 failures on the AI paths, the viewport relaunch frequency, password masking in the accessibility tree, and the iframe sandbox flags in the search results viewer. A separate housekeeping decision is whether stale repo documents that claim robots handling, format parity, DOCX and budget enforcement are done should be retired wave by wave through the stale-document register.

### 1.6 What changed during verification

Tier calibration produced 24 Priority, 41 Recommended and 12 Nice to have. Two independent verification passes then ran: a Verevon-side pass that read the code behind all 77 items, and a reference-side pass that re-read the evidence behind 24 items. The Verevon pass kept 8 items as written, reworded 61 because the code showed a more precise gap, a better place to build or reusable parts, and recommended demotion for 8; six of those moved from Recommended to Nice to have ([F-t2-054](#F-t2-054), [F-t2-057](#F-t2-057), [F-t2-061](#F-t2-061), [F-t2-063](#F-t2-063), [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064), [F-t2-065](#F-t2-065)) and two were already Nice to have. The Priority count is unchanged at 24; the final split is 24, 35 and 18. Effort changed for 33 items, almost always upward, because items that looked like one-plane work turned out to span three or four services. The reference pass confirmed 2 items outright and corrected 22 in part; 14 prevalence counts were wrong and have been replaced or removed. No item remains unverified or low confidence, and no item was dropped as already built.

## 2. What every reference shares

The four references split into two pairs: Firecrawl and Apify are acquisition platforms (scrape, crawl, search, jobs), Codex and Aside are browsers that a person and an agent share. 61 capabilities count as table-stakes: present in both members of a pair, or in 3 of the 4 references. A few rows fall below that bar, either because verification corrected the count or because the comparison classed a single-reference browser capability as table-stakes for a browser product; they are kept and the count shown is the corrected one. Verevon already ships 14 of the 61 at parity or better, mostly in the agent-control core (accessibility-tree observation, stale-target protection, locators, dialogs, approvals, risk tiers, evidence) and in platform plumbing (fetch engine, proxies, politeness, durable queue). The rest are mostly Partial, and the pattern is consistent: the code exists in Quarry v2 but is not wired into the live path, or is enforced in the Verevon gateway rather than in Quarry.

Prevalence is written as "n of N references". N is 4 for the acquisition categories (sections 2.1 to 2.7). The data counts the two browser categories (2.8 and 2.9) out of 3, because Apify, whose evidence is documentation only, has no browser shell to compare [INF]. Where verification corrected a count, the corrected statement is used and the original number is not repeated. "Verevon today" is the verified status of the recommendation that covers the capability; where one recommendation covers several capabilities, the status of the specific capability is given with the item status in brackets.

### 2.1 Acquisition API surface

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Synchronous single-URL scrape returning the document inline | 2 of 4 (Firecrawl, Apify) | Partial | [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005) |
| Site crawl with scoping controls | 2 of 4 (Firecrawl, Apify) | Stub | [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016) |
| Web search API with filters, categories and provider fallback | 3 of 4 (Firecrawl, Aside, Codex) | Shipped | none needed |
| MCP server exposure for external agents | 3 of 4 (Apify, Aside, Firecrawl) | Absent (item Partial, because an OpenAPI spec exists) | [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064) |
| CLI and installable agent skills | 3 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl, Aside) | Absent (item Partial) | [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064) |

The shared baseline for an acquisition API is one scrape call that returns the requested formats inline, a crawl with include/exclude scope, and a search endpoint [SRC for Firecrawl, DOC for Apify]. Verevon's search is ahead of the references (multi-provider router with Zero-Data-Retention, ZDR, gating), but its scrape returns artifact references instead of inline text, so a ZDR scrape returns no text at all (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/pipeline.rs:597-601), and POST /v1/crawl accepts only url, max_pages, max_depth, ingest and zdr (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:533-552). Model Context Protocol (MCP) exposure is the one capability three references ship and Verevon has deferred by decision (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/docs/OSS_PARITY_BACKLOG.md:30).

### 2.2 Output formats & content processing

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main-content extraction with selector filters and selectable extractors | 2 of 4 (Firecrawl, Apify) | Partial | [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005) |
| Screenshot and page-print (PDF) output on scrape | 3 of 4 (Firecrawl, Codex, Aside) | Partial | [F-t2-023](#F-t2-023) |
| Office and e-book document conversion and file capture during crawl | 2 of 4 (Firecrawl, Apify) | Partial (DOCX extractor has no caller) | [F-t2-037](#F-t2-037) |

Both acquisition platforms let the caller choose how much of the page comes back (main content only, include or exclude selectors) and both convert office documents; Apify also falls back to the full HTML when a page is not a clear article [DOC]. Firecrawl runs a fixed transformer order and strips unrequested formats at the end [SRC]. Verevon runs one hard-coded Readability pass (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/pipeline.rs:506), hard-codes screenshot and pdf to None on the scrape path (pipeline.rs:702-703), and has no content-type router, so the DOCX and PDF extractors in quarry-transform are reached only from the answer fetcher (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/answer.rs:835-839).

### 2.3 Rendering & browser engines

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Multi-tier fetch engine core (HTTP, TLS-impersonating HTTP, headless browser behind one interface) | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Shipped (Chromium only) | none needed |
| TLS/HTTP2 impersonation tier and browser-consistent headers | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Stub | [F-t2-066](#F-t2-066) |
| Browser pool lifecycle and per-request context isolation | 1 of 4 (Apify Crawlee; the Firecrawl count was corrected: it runs one Chromium behind a page semaphore) | Partial | [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007) |
| Persistent sessions and browser profiles across runs | Recorded for Firecrawl and Apify; the count is an undercount, Codex also keeps persistent storage partitions [SRC] | Partial | [F-t2-020](#F-t2-020) |
| Declarative page actions on scrape (wait, click, write, press, scroll, screenshot, JS) | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Partial | [F-t2-023](#F-t2-023) |

The engine baseline is a waterfall of cheap HTTP, TLS-impersonating HTTP and a real browser behind one interface, which Verevon has [SRC]. Where Verevon is behind is browser process management: one shared Chromium is relaunched, and every session's pages are dropped, whenever a lease asks for a different viewport (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:194-270). Profile persistence works end to end, but the snapshot captures only cookies for the current URL without expiry and no IndexedDB (chromiumoxide.rs:1771-1832), and the ActionRuntime that would run scrape-time actions has no production caller (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/browser_driver.rs:128-240).

### 2.4 Anti-bot, proxies & reliability

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Proxy pool plumbing and egress self-check | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Shipped | none needed |
| Tiered, residential and geo-targeted proxies with escalation on block | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Partial | [F-t2-058](#F-t2-058) |
| Retries, error hooks and resilient internal clients | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Partial | [F-t2-047](#F-t2-047) |
| robots.txt compliance with governed override | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Stub | [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016) |
| Politeness and throughput throttling | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Shipped | none needed |

Both acquisition platforms respect robots.txt by default, throttle per host and retry with a per-request hook [SRC for Firecrawl, DOC for Apify]. Verevon's per-host politeness and proxy plumbing are at parity, and its egress containment (DNS pinning, per-hop redirect checks, deny-by-default network guard) is stronger than either reference. The robots gap is a compliance risk: robots rules are applied only in /v1/map, and RobotsCache itself has no production caller (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/map_routes.rs:87-109), and the Go crawl workflow has no robots check (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-orchestrator/internal/workflows/workflows.go:396-516). The retry helper is exported but only its own tests call it (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/lib.rs:164).

### 2.5 Orchestration & scale

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Durable request queue / URL frontier with lease-based claims | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Shipped | none needed |
| Crawl budgets and guardrails (page, depth, rate, cost caps, result TTL) | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Partial | [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008) |
| Crawl scoping and link filtering with actionable denial reasons | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Stub | [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016) |
| Sitemap, robots and index-assisted URL discovery | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Partial | [F-t2-041](#F-t2-041) |
| Executable schedules with timezone, jitter, overlap skip and stale-run recovery | 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl) | Partial | [F-t2-018](#F-t2-018) |

The baseline is a durable queue, a scoped frontier, sitemap seeding, budgets and schedules that run. Verevon's Postgres SKIP LOCKED queue with Temporal is at parity. Everything above it is scaffolding without a live caller: CrawlFrontier and CrawlDenialReason are never used by the Go workflow (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/crawl_frontier.rs, crawl_denial.rs), only max_steps is enforced on the agent path and crawls are bounded only by max_pages where 0 means unlimited (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:446-451; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-orchestrator/internal/workflows/workflows.go:456), and schedules map only change_monitor to a workflow, so scrape, crawl and batch schedules never fire (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-control/internal/resources/schedule_wire.go:39-51).

### 2.6 Platform & ecosystem

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Usage pricing function and metering (credits, failures free, feature surcharges) | 2 of 4 (Firecrawl, Apify) | Partial | [F-t2-046](#F-t2-046) |
| Plans, limits and plan-gated browser features | 3 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl, Aside) | Partial | [F-t2-046](#F-t2-046) |

Every commercial reference has one pricing function and plan keys that gate web features [SRC for Firecrawl and Aside, DOC for Apify]. Verevon publishes five idempotent usage metrics to billing-core, but agent routes emit no usage event, so browser steps and minutes are unmetered, and the plan-key lists in org-core and billing-core contain no browser or Quarry keys (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/usage.rs:88-94; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:700-761).

### 2.7 Observability & operations

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Request correlation ids and run origin attribution | 3 of 4 (Firecrawl, Codex, Apify) | Partial | [F-t2-014](#F-t2-014) |
| Run history with retention, TTL access and ZDR-scrubbed records | Firecrawl and Apify; the Aside count was corrected (its only evidenced retention is a 15-minute purge of ephemeral sessions) | Partial | [F-t2-003](#F-t2-003) |
| Least-privilege permissions for agents and run tokens | 3 of 4 (Apify, Aside, Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-036](#F-t2-036) |

The references agree on a correlation id per request, a run origin, a run history with a retention rule, and scoped, expiring run authority. Verevon has the stronger evidence model (immutable step receipts, checkpoints, proof bundles) but the operational layer around it is thin: quarry-edge mints its own request id per handler with no x-request-id intake (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:1739), ZDR runs leave no durable record, and BrowserBroker grants live in an in-memory map with no production issuer (apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/internal/grant/grant.go:62-71; apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/cmd/main.go:63).

### 2.8 In-app browser shell (user-facing)

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Tab strip operations and detached windows | 1 of 3 (Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-070](#F-t2-070) |
| Chat beside the browser: side panel, docked browser, floating composer | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-012](#F-t2-012) |
| Navigation controls and free cross-site browsing | 1 of 3 recorded (Codex; free browsing is inferred from the absence of a human allowlist [INF]) | Partial | [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011) |
| Standard page utilities: find, zoom, print, mute, DevTools, media indicators | 2 of 3 (Codex, Aside) | Partial | [F-t2-069](#F-t2-069) |
| Browser profiles and persistent login state | 2 of 3 (Codex, Aside) | Shipped | none needed |
| Agent-control indicator, live view and human take-over | 3 of 3 (Aside, Codex, Firecrawl) | Shipped (takeover is enforced in the gateway, not in Quarry) | [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) phase 1 |
| Screenshots and page capture (viewport, area, full page) | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-069](#F-t2-069) |
| Browsing history and bookmarks with agent/user source | 2 of 3 (Codex, Aside) | Partial | [F-t2-068](#F-t2-068) |
| Privacy controls and clear browsing data | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-068](#F-t2-068) |
| Browser and agent-permission settings hub | 2 of 3 (Codex, Aside) | Absent (item Partial) | [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032) |

Both browser references are ordinary browsers first: the user navigates anywhere, keeps profiles, sees history, and the agent is layered on top with a visible control indicator and a chat beside the page [REA/SRC]. Verevon has the agent layer (profiles, live view, AI/Manual chip, DevTools drawer) but not the ordinary browser underneath: every user session is an agent run with allowed_domains set to the first host, max_steps 12 and max_runtime_s 120 (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:483-490), the interactive BrowserChrome is mounted only in the dashboard Crawl composer (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/KnowledgeScrapePreview.tsx:253), and the only history is a browser-local list capped at 10 entries (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/web-history.ts:32). Takeover is recorded server-side, but Quarry's step handler never reads control_mode (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:2227-2294).

### 2.9 AI-browser control

| Capability | References that have it | Verevon today | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Authenticated agent-to-browser control channel (agent loop outside the browser) | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) |
| Browser-level command catalogue with audited tab/window/bookmark management | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial | none needed (Quarry sessions are server-side; no user-browser state to manage) |
| Code-mode browser control (model writes a script against a typed runtime) | 3 of 3 (Aside, Codex, Firecrawl) | Planned | [F-t2-063](#F-t2-063) |
| Sandbox hardening for model-written browser code and approval inputs | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Planned (no present gap: typed actions are plain data) | [F-t2-063](#F-t2-063) |
| Capability- and environment-conditional tool docs and policy delivered as data | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Not assessed (no baseline record; item Partial) | [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032) |
| Browsing operating procedure in the system prompt | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial (one-paragraph prompt; LLM planner off by default) | [F-t2-010](#F-t2-010) |
| Accessibility-tree observation with stable element refs and diffs | 3 of 3 (Aside, Codex, Firecrawl) | Shipped | none needed |
| Stale-target protection and ref re-resolution | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Shipped | none needed |
| Actionability checks, hit-target verification and trusted input events | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-015](#F-t2-015) |
| Post-action settle logic and ambient page-event notes | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-034](#F-t2-034) |
| Multi-path input with visual fallback and set-of-marks screenshots | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-034](#F-t2-034) |
| Playwright-compatible semantic locators across frames | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Shipped | none needed |
| JavaScript dialog handling as controllable objects | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Shipped | none needed |
| Agent tab ownership and turn-scoped tab lifecycle | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013), [F-t2-054](#F-t2-054) |
| Co-browsing: agent acts in the tab the user is looking at | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Absent (a Model Plane run never attaches; item Partial) | [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) |
| User takeover, manual handoff and interruption | Codex and Aside show takeover UI; verification found no reference that enforces a took-over state in code, so no count is given | Partial | [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) |
| Durable human-in-the-loop approvals with fail-closed semantics | 3 of 3 (Aside, Codex, Firecrawl) | Shipped | none needed |
| Risk-tier confirmation taxonomy and final-confirm mode | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Shipped | none needed |
| Session permission modes and rule engine with action classes | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial | [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032) |
| Gated uploads and downloads with one-time grants | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Partial (code exists; driver capability flag is false) | [F-t2-039](#F-t2-039) |
| Secure credential handoff: the model never sees secrets | 2 of 4 (Aside; Codex cloud browser only), corrected to a differentiator | Absent | [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024) |
| Prompt-injection guard for web content | 3 of 3 (Aside, Codex, Firecrawl) | Partial (Model Plane screens; Quarry does not label) | [F-t2-049](#F-t2-049) |
| Visual proof and recording of agent work | 2 of 3 (Aside, Codex) | Shipped (no video) | none needed |

This is the category where Verevon is closest to the references and in places ahead: fail-closed target repair, cross-process frame actions, dialogs as objects, risk gates enforced in code, and immutable receipts are all shipped. The gaps are in the loop around those primitives. Ref-targeted clicks are synthetic element.click() calls with no visibility, stability or hit-test check (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:1232-1330), observation is captured immediately after an action with no settle (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/observation.rs:576-640), the LLM planner is off unless QUARRY_BROWSER_AGENT_LLM is set (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/llm_planner.rs:130-148), and no secret-reference action exists, so a login today needs the model to type the secret or the user to take over (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-core/src/contracts.rs:638-700). The downloads path is the clearest "built but not wired" case: quarantine, caps and admission checks exist, but capabilities() reports downloads_to_artifacts=false, so every download_ref is rejected (chromiumoxide.rs:1877; agent_routes.rs:1191-1193).

### 2.10 Integration-plane overlap

No capability in this category is table-stakes across the references; its clusters are differentiators (provider-API routing, connector hints by domain, OTP via mail connectors) and are covered by [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024), [F-t2-043](#F-t2-043), [F-t2-056](#F-t2-056) and [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064) in the differentials section.

## 3. Best of breed: who does each thing best

The table names the reference whose mechanism Verevon should copy for each capability area, using the verified reference data. Provenance: Firecrawl is read in source, but its hosted parts (fire-engine, Hangar) are visible only through client contracts; Apify is public documentation only; Codex and Aside are static analysis of the installed apps.

| Capability area | Best reference | Why it is best (one sentence) | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|
| Inline scrape and strict options | Firecrawl | One shared strict options schema is reused by every endpoint and the requested formats come back inline under a per-team concurrency permit [SRC]. | [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005) |
| Main-content extractor choice | Apify | The caller picks the extractor and the platform falls back to full HTML when the page is not a clear article [DOC]. | [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005) |
| File-type routing on scrape | Firecrawl | A specialty check routes PDFs, Office files and images to the right parser by content type before the HTML pipeline runs [SRC]. | [F-t2-009](#F-t2-009) |
| Async job lifecycle | Firecrawl | Cursor-paged status, per-URL errors with a requiresAction flag, cancel, and a "processing continues" timeout contract [SRC]. | [F-t2-017](#F-t2-017) |
| Crawl scoping and robots.txt | Firecrawl | Typed denial codes from a native filter, remediation messages built in TypeScript, robots on by default and overriding it gated as an enterprise entitlement [SRC]. | [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016) |
| Sitemap discovery and URL de-duplication | Firecrawl | Parallel sitemap kickoff from robots lines and common paths, plus permutation-based dedupe that locks the redirect target [SRC]. | [F-t2-041](#F-t2-041) |
| Schedules | Firecrawl, with Apify second | Lease-claimed ticks, deterministic jitter, skipped_overlap and skipped_no_credits states and stale-run recovery [SRC]; both have timezone-aware cron [SRC, DOC]. | [F-t2-018](#F-t2-018) |
| Structured extraction | Firecrawl | Schema normalisation with strict mode, model chosen by schema complexity, a repair pass on parse failure, and summary, question and highlights as one call each [SRC]. | [F-t2-019](#F-t2-019) |
| Document parsing depth | Firecrawl | Per-page classification sends only the pages that need OCR to the OCR service and converts text PDFs natively [SRC]. | [F-t2-037](#F-t2-037) |
| Screenshot and PDF capture | Aside | Scroll-and-stitch tiling for tall pages, element shots and print-to-PDF [SRC]. | [F-t2-023](#F-t2-023) |
| Scrape-time page actions | Firecrawl | One capped action list on the scrape request with per-type output arrays, though execution happens only in the hosted engine [SRC]. | [F-t2-023](#F-t2-023) |
| Browser pool lifecycle | Apify Crawlee | maxOpenPagesPerBrowser placement, retire-after-N pages and a 300-second inactivity sweeper [DOC]. | [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007) |
| Persistent browser profiles | Firecrawl | Profiles refused under ZDR, monotonic saves and a key-value tombstone so a late save cannot resurrect a deleted profile [SRC]; the snapshot spec and encryption are Verevon requirements, not reference parity. | [F-t2-020](#F-t2-020) |
| Cost and runtime budgets | Firecrawl | Admission check at full session TTL, actual duration settled exactly once under a row lock, with a reconciliation sweep [SRC]; Apify aborts the run at the max total charge [DOC]. | [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008) |
| Metering and plans | Firecrawl | One pricing function per job, failed requests cost nothing, and two clear limit units [SRC]. | [F-t2-046](#F-t2-046) |
| Per-org capacity | Firecrawl | Tenant backlog with expiry, an in-flight priority penalty, and 429 only on backlog overflow [SRC]. | [F-t2-025](#F-t2-025) |
| Proxy tiers and geo egress | Firecrawl | Country-validated egress, locale and mobile emulation keyed into the cache, and escalation only on 401, 403 or 429 [SRC]. | [F-t2-058](#F-t2-058) |
| Fetch-path reliability | Firecrawl and Apify Crawlee | Composed cancellation tokens give each engine its own deadline inside one scrape budget, with a transportable error vocabulary [SRC]; Crawlee defines the retry contract with an error hook [DOC]. | [F-t2-047](#F-t2-047) |
| Engine planning | Firecrawl | Each engine declares a capability set, the waterfall hedges on a timer, and typed re-plan signals re-run planning under a retry budget [SRC]; Apify learns the per-site mode [DOC]. | [F-t2-057](#F-t2-057) |
| ZDR cache admission and run records | Firecrawl | An inline admission check refuses to cache anything with custom headers, actions, a profile or low-fidelity engine output, and ZDR requests are scrubbed rather than dropped [SRC]. | [F-t2-003](#F-t2-003) |
| ZDR-safe telemetry | Firecrawl | A sampler drops spans for ZDR requests and a bounded registry catches traces flagged ZDR after start [SRC]; Codex's safe/sensitive log split is the complementary pattern [SRC]. | [F-t2-004](#F-t2-004) |
| Org web policy | Firecrawl Safe Mode | Policy is resolved in the API layer and recomputed in the worker for every URL as a backstop; it is not strictly tighten-only, since an admin bypass flag exists [SRC]. | [F-t2-022](#F-t2-022) |
| Agent-to-browser control channel | Aside | A signed-challenge, short-lived control token and a routed, cancellable command bridge [SRC]; no reference implements a durable org-scoped grant issuer, which is a Verevon requirement. | [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) |
| Script execution safety | Codex | Evaluate is made structurally safe in a read-only isolated world, and raw devtools-protocol access sits behind a default-off preference, an enterprise requirement and per-origin consent [SRC]. | [F-t2-002](#F-t2-002) |
| Trusted input and actionability | Codex | An in-page hit-test probe across nested and cross-process iframes, settle polling, and typed "covered by" errors before trusted input events [SRC]. | [F-t2-015](#F-t2-015) |
| Post-action settle and observation | Codex, with Aside | Settle waits for navigation commit and first paint, then for accessibility and DOM mutation quiet capped at 750 ms, and the accessibility snapshot stitches per-frame trees under per-frame timeouts [SRC]; Aside adds a 350 ms mutation-ratio quiet window and ambient page notes flushed into the next tool result [SRC]. | [F-t2-034](#F-t2-034) |
| Browsing procedure prompt | Aside | Snapshot ladder, "unconfirmed until re-snapshot", and a rule to switch strategy after two or three failures [SRC]. | [F-t2-010](#F-t2-010) |
| Co-browsing and tab ownership | Codex | Agent tabs marked handoff or deliverable with turn-scoped release and hidden agent tabs [SRC]; Aside's owned-versus-borrowed tab rows with orphan reconciliation are the second model [SRC]. | [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) |
| Chat beside the browser | Codex | The browser docks left, right, bottom, pinned or in a new window, with a floating composer [REA/SRC]. | [F-t2-012](#F-t2-012) |
| Free browsing and omnibox | Codex | Navigation commands including hard reload, and a token-based omnibox suggestion service [SRC]. | [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011) |
| Credential handoff | Codex Cloud Browser | The request is validated against the live page, values are delivered in an isolated world, observation is gated after delivery, and email OTP is a variant; this is wired to the cloud browser, not the desktop in-app browser [SRC]. | [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024) |
| Secret redaction at capture | Aside | Text and ARIA snapshots and log arguments are redacted by input type, autocomplete token and value shape before storage [SRC]; no reference redacts screenshots or receipts. | [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021) |
| Per-origin consent and typed denials | Codex | Consent with turn, conversation and global scopes resolved deny-first, one policy choke point and a 16-reason denial catalogue [SRC]. | [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032) |
| Permission decision audit | Codex, with Apify | One structured record per permission decision with who decided and why [SRC]; Apify's limited-permission default with scoped, expiring tokens [DOC]. | [F-t2-036](#F-t2-036) |
| Downloads and uploads | Codex | One-time download grants consumed in the will-download handler with redirect-chain matching, and uploads through an intercepted file chooser [SRC]. | [F-t2-039](#F-t2-039) |
| Agent presence and run control | Codex, with Aside | A host-drawn cursor that the click waits for and structured annotation payloads [SRC]; Aside's steer and queue commands with sequence resume [SRC]. | [F-t2-038](#F-t2-038) |
| Run reaping and caps | Aside | A global run semaphore and an idle sweeper that closes agent-owned tabs of idle sessions [SRC]. | [F-t2-006](#F-t2-006) |
| Honest bot-block handling | Codex | A classifier with explicit non-signals, at most one recovery, no fingerprint changes, and a closed reason report [SRC]. | [F-t2-027](#F-t2-027) |
| Run trace envelope | Firecrawl, with Codex | A versioned event envelope with per-agent sequence and structured errors, observed as a contract with the implementation upstream and beta-gated [SRC]; Codex supplies the UI-side pieces [SRC]. | [F-t2-014](#F-t2-014) |
| Durable hosted sessions | Firecrawl Hangar | Idle and absolute TTLs, view versus control scopes and scrape-to-session replay, seen through client contracts [SRC]. | [F-t2-035](#F-t2-035) |
| Monitor change quality | Firecrawl | Normalised compare, unified and JSON field diffs, and a goal-aware judge [SRC]. | [F-t2-030](#F-t2-030) |
| Outbound webhooks | Apify | Rich event vocabulary, payload and header templates and ad-hoc per-run webhooks [DOC]; Verevon's signed delivery with retry and dead-letter queue is already stronger. | [F-t2-031](#F-t2-031) |
| External contract and MCP | Apify | Hosted Streamable-HTTP MCP behind OAuth with category tool loading and a full OpenAPI spec [DOC]. | [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064) |
| Code-mode control | Aside | One persistent QuickJS scope per session with a small typed surface and runtime lints [SRC]; Codex freezes approval inputs to close the time-of-check gap [SRC]. | [F-t2-063](#F-t2-063) |
| Session isolation | Apify | Isolated per-run containers under SOC 2 Type II, runtime technology not disclosed [DOC]. | [F-t2-060](#F-t2-060) |
| URL reputation gate | Firecrawl | Local lists, optional customer gateway rules and a reputation provider, run before the first engine and on every redirect [SRC]. | [F-t2-049](#F-t2-049) |

Taken together: Firecrawl is the model for the acquisition half (scrape contract, crawl scoping, jobs, schedules, extraction, budgets, ZDR handling), and its code can be read, so its mechanisms are the safest to copy. Codex is the model for governance of a shared browser (script gating, trusted input, consent, decision audit, downloads, co-browsing marks, docked chat), with the caveat that its credential handoff ships only in its cloud browser. Aside is the model for the agent loop's ergonomics (control channel, settle, procedure prompt, capture-time redaction, code mode, run reaping). Apify is the model for platform shape (browser pool, containers, webhooks, hosted MCP), but every Apify claim rests on documentation only and should be confirmed before it drives a design.

## 4. Priority recommendations

Priority items are the changes that unblock production use of the in-app browser and the Quarry acquisition API, or that close a breach of a Verevon architecture rule (Zero-Data-Retention, ZDR, must propagate; no tenant may read another tenant's data; the Model proposes and Quarry executes or rejects). They are listed in recommended build order within the tier, so an item generally assumes the ones above it. Every card shows the verified status of Verevon at commit afb83928 (2026-10-10): status, gap, effort and approach come from the code-verification pass and supersede the original comparison notes. Where verification corrected a claim about a reference product, the corrected claim is the one stated here. Effort is S, M, L or XL for the verified scope on the card.

<a id="F-t2-001"></a>
### F-t2-001 · Production BrowserBroker grants and delegated bearer forwarding (unblocks every browser path)
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (BrowserBroker) + Frontend Plane gateway; Quarry validates only | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Give the Model Plane BrowserBroker a durable (Postgres) grant store and an issuance path the Frontend gateway can reach. The gateway obtains a user-session grant when it creates a browser session, sends `grant_id` on `POST /v1/agent/runs`, forwards `x-browser-authorization`, and derives a narrower run grant for AI runs instead of the interim `session:{id}` string. Add one prod-mode contract test across gateway, model-gateway, execution-core and quarry-edge that fails on 401 or 403.

**Why it matters.** Prod-mode Quarry requires a broker grant on every run and step and refuses to boot without a validator, yet nothing in production issues a grant and the broker store is in memory [SRC]. Until this lands, the in-app browser, the AI step loop, co-browsing, the credential bridge, grant-carried budgets and per-origin consent cannot work in production [INF from the code paths below; not runtime-tested]. It is the highest-leverage item in the audit.

**What the references do.** No reference implements a durable, org-scoped grant issuer; it is a Verevon design requirement, not a parity feature [INF]. The closest mechanisms are single-user, local-device trust checks. Aside's browser-control endpoint refuses unauthenticated clients: a client fetches a challenge, signs it with an installation key and receives a short-lived session token, and commands travel over a bridge routed per account, profile and mode that rejects stale connections [SRC, from a scratch copy of the daemon blob]. Codex runs a signed, nonce-bound per-turn browser-access check tied to a "Work settings" toggle [SRC; enterprise gating INF from an error string]. Codex also hardens every guest attach in its Electron main process, forcing partition, session and preload and deleting dangerous flags; that is 1 of 4 references (Codex) and Electron-specific, so it says nothing about Aside [SRC]. Firecrawl's hosted sessions take `ttl` and `activityTtl`, enforced by the external, closed Hangar service; Firecrawl's own 15 s reconciler settles billing and is not a reaper [SRC; enforcement INF].

**Verevon today.** The broker implements AcquireGrant, ValidateGrant and RevokeGrant with org and owner scoping, a host list of at most 32, a 15-minute TTL and sensitive child grants, but the store is in memory (apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/internal/grant/grant.go:61-71) and no production code calls AcquireGrant. create_session posts to Quarry with no grant_id (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:483-506), which prod Quarry rejects with 403 (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:1377-1412), and start_ai_run sends `session:{id}` (browser.rs:1235) without the browser bearer that model-gateway's VerifiedBrowserBearer extractor requires (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/auth.rs:427-437). Reuse the grant package, browser.proto, resolve_browser_grant, the declared BrowserBroker audience (audience_tokens.rs:27-42) and Quarry's GrpcGrantValidator.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Slice it: issuance, gateway wiring and the contract test on the in-memory store first, then Postgres. Prefer a model-gateway HTTP endpoint wrapping AcquireGrant over teaching the gateway gRPC. Only `x-browser-authorization` is missing from the bearer set; add it to proxy_model_json_with_delegations (chat/shared.rs:629-690). Both create_session and start_ai_run need the grant; deriving a run grant from a session grant is a new broker API. The Model Plane owns issuance and the gateway must never mint authority; domains come from the broker, never the client; ZDR grants are never persisted; a Space must never widen a grant. The 15-minute TTL caps session length until [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011) adds renewal. Risks: scope creep, restart semantics for in-flight sessions, and user-session issuance bypassing the cross-domain approval gate.

<a id="F-t2-002"></a>
### F-t2-002 · Gate Evaluate and raw script actions behind a grant capability and one-shot approval
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | S | Ingestion Plane (plus a BrowserBroker allowlist change) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Make the Quarry `/step` path deny `AgentAction::Evaluate` by default on agent runs. Either add `evaluate` to the broker's allowed sensitive actions and to Quarry's sensitive-approval path, binding the script hash into the one-shot approval, or reject Evaluate outright until a consumer exists. Remove `evaluate` and `pdf` from the unwired ModelPlanePlanner prompt. Build the isolated-world read-only evaluator only with [F-t2-023](#F-t2-023), where a return value is consumed.

**Why it matters.** Quarry otherwise fails closed, but Evaluate has no approval branch, so any risky action (submit, delete, read a sensitive field) can be expressed as script and bypass the risk-classified approval model. The executor must be sound regardless of which caller reaches `/step`. Severity is defense-in-depth: the exposure is internal service callers, not an open user-facing hole.

**What the references do.** Codex does not gate `playwright.evaluate` behind a capability or approval; it makes evaluate structurally safe. Scripts run in a dedicated isolated world with universal access off, against read-only wrappers that block assignment, `defineProperty` and `delete`; credential fields are masked, `import()` is blocked, and argument and result sizes are capped [SRC, from the beautified shipped plugin bundle]. This read-only evaluate is 1 of 4 references (Codex). Codex's consent gates apply only to raw Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP): a `full_cdp_access_enabled` preference (default false), an enterprise requirement and per-origin consent; "domain allowlist" means CDP protocol domains plus a per-method denylist, not website domains [SRC]. Firecrawl's Safe Mode strips `executeJavascript` and rejects interactive sessions [SRC]; Aside isolates its REPL guest from the raw CDP client, though its `page.evaluate` may mutate the page [SRC]. A grant capability and one-shot approval are Verevon proposals [INF]. Caveat: the source record says Codex docs mark raw CDP development-only [DOC, not re-opened].

**Verevon today.** `sensitive_approval_for_action` covers only respond_dialog, upload_ref and download_ref and falls through at apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:280; the broker allowlist has no evaluate (apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/internal/grant/grant.go:30-36); ChromiumoxideDriver::evaluate runs in the main world (quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:3003-3017). The Model Plane half is done: execution-core's browser.act schema (runtime_loop/agent.rs:3479-3512) and ACTION_SCHEMA (llm_planner.rs:53-65) never offer evaluate, and the gateway's sanitize_action denies it (browser.rs:3751). `/step` discards the script's return value (quarry-runtime/src/observation.rs:542-543). Reuse the one-shot `used_sensitive_approval_grants` set, `authorizes_exact_sensitive_action` (grant_validator.rs:98-109) and the typed `browser_action_denied` shape.

**How to build it in Verevon.** The gate lives in quarry-edge and quarry-runtime, not only in the planner schema; denials are typed Quarry errors. A new sensitive action class needs the broker allowlist and proto alignment. Sensitive actions require a parent run grant, and UI runs carry `session:{id}` today, so the gate must fail closed until [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) lands. Drop the "hide it from the planner" sub-task; it is already true. Risk: an extraction flow may rely on mutating evaluate, so audit callers first.

**Quick win.** A default-deny branch plus one allowlist entry in a path that already has the approval machinery.

<a id="F-t2-003"></a>
### F-t2-003 · Close the gateway search-cache ZDR and tenant hole, then publish a per-feature ZDR capability matrix
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Frontend Plane gateway (phase 1); Ingestion Plane (matrix, tombstone) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Phase 1 (S): promote the gateway's private CacheAdmission to a shared module, call it in search_web before lookup and before store, add the authorized org id to the cache key, and key the scrape-preview cache by org and request variant (formats, headers, actions). Phase 2 (M): generate or test a per-feature ZDR capability matrix from route attributes in quarry-core with explicit refusal codes, and add a metadata-only ZDR run tombstone with a retention janitor after a contract review.

**Why it matters.** Two architecture rules are broken at once. The gateway `/search` cache has no org in its key, so an answer built from one org's private corpus can be served to another org, and it stores with no ZDR check. This is source-verified cross-tenant exposure, and the gateway is the one boundary that must hold neither. Phase 1 is a small gateway-only change plus a test.

**What the references do.** Firecrawl has the only explicit cache-admission rule, 1 of 4 references: `shouldCache` is an inline boolean inside `sendDocumentToIndex`. It refuses to cache when the request carries custom headers, actions or a profile, when the result came from a low-fidelity engine, from Exchange or from the index itself, and for page-aware or marker PDF parsers [SRC]. Firecrawl also has the only per-feature ZDR list, 1 of 4 references: team modes are disabled, allowed or forced; agent, extract and scrape-status refuse under forced ZDR, and change tracking degrades with a warning [SRC]. Deletion is a per-request ZDR cleanup queue plus access-row expiry (24 h default, `crawlTtlHours` for crawls), with URL and options redacted in the request row while outcome, timing and cost remain [SRC]. Plan-based retention comes from Apify and is documentation only [DOC]. Aside shows local session storage and a 15-minute purge of terminal ephemeral sessions, not a retention policy [SRC]. Caveat: the ZDR team modes are enterprise flags, and not every ZDR sink's retention was traced.

**Verevon today.** search_web keys on query, limit, include_answer and filters only (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/search.rs:209-226), stores with no ZDR check (search.rs:285) and keeps entries 4 h fresh and 24 h stale (cache.rs:20-25). The Quarry `/v1/search` it fronts uses org-scoped private-corpus providers (quarry-edge/src/search_routes.rs:503-517). Quarry itself is correct: its SearchCache is org-keyed and skips reads and writes under ZDR (search_routes.rs:762-780). A ZDR-aware CacheAdmission exists privately for the scrape preview (knowledge/quarry.rs:213-280), though its key (line 104) is target-only. Reuse `crate::zdr::{request_zdr, org_zdr_enabled}` (fails closed), `authorized_org_id`, the existing refusal codes (quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:563-588) and the quarry-control janitor loop.

**How to build it in Verevon.** The gateway owns phase 1; Quarry owns the matrix and tombstone. Fail closed on a ZDR lookup failure, as zdr.rs does. The tombstone must be metadata only. Do not weaken Verevon's stricter refusals when aligning with Firecrawl. ZDR semantics for caches, monitors and schedules need the ADR shared with [F-t2-004](#F-t2-004), [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016) and [F-t2-030](#F-t2-030). Risks: lower cross-org hit rate (acceptable) and matrix drift (generate or test it).

**Quick win.** The highest-value small fix in the audit: a small gateway-only change, existing helpers, one test that a ZDR org's search leaves no cache entry.

<a id="F-t2-004"></a>
### F-t2-004 · ZDR-safe telemetry: span sampler, late-ZDR registry, log filter and safe/sensitive log fields in Quarry and the gateway
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Ingestion Plane (adopted by Model Plane and Frontend Plane gateway) | W1 | Medium |

**What to build.** Phase 1 (S): remove the URL field logged inside the ZDR-active branch of change_routes.rs, sweep the roughly 250 tracing call sites for url, query and error-text fields (reqwest errors embed URLs), carry effective ZDR in a task-local or span field so a filter on the JSON fmt layer can drop sensitive fields, and add a lint. Phase 2 (M), delivered together with span emission in [F-t2-029](#F-t2-029): an OpenTelemetry sampler that drops ZDR spans, a bounded late-ZDR trace registry with a span processor, a documented safe/sensitive log-field contract mirrored in Rust and the Go `quarryotel` package, and an explicit exporter-disable flag.

**Why it matters.** ZDR must propagate, and today it stops at the observability boundary. A leak into logs or an OTLP exporter is invisible to users, breaches ZDR contracts, and blocks safe SIEM export and support diagnostics. The live exposure is log fields, since almost no spans are produced at the default filter.

**What the references do.** ZDR-aware telemetry is 1 of 4 references (Firecrawl). Its TypeScript OpenTelemetry setup carries a ZDR flag in context; a sampler returns NOT_RECORD for flagged requests, a registry of trace ids flagged ZDR after the span started (a Set with oldest-first eviction, capped at 100,000) is checked by a span processor on end so descendants are dropped before export, and a winston filter drops ZDR-flagged log lines [SRC]. One helper service, the Go html-to-markdown service, honours an `X-Zero-Data-Retention` header by omitting the request id, and only that client sends it [SRC]. Privacy-classified log fields are 1 of 4 references (Codex): every log call app-wide takes `{safe, sensitive}` tags, but how sinks treat `sensitive` was not found, so "sinks drop or hash sensitive" is a Verevon proposal [SRC; sink behaviour INF]. Codex's `BROWSER_USE_DISABLE_AMBIENT_NETWORK` is a sandbox kill switch for gates, Sentry and analytics, not a ZDR mechanism [SRC]. The Rust sampler shape is a port, not observed. Caveat: Firecrawl tracing is off unless an OTLP endpoint is configured.

**Verevon today.** apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/telemetry.rs:25-64 installs a JSON fmt layer and an optional OTLP layer under one EnvFilter with no ZDR awareness; pkg/quarryotel/quarryotel.go:39-93 uses ParentBased(AlwaysSample). change_routes.rs:632-637 logs source_url inside the ZDR-active branch at debug level. The edge creates only tower-http spans (routes.rs:303) and the Go services call quarryotel.Init but no tracer.Start [INF from grep]. Posture already propagates through the token floor plus body flag (auth.rs:121-139), emit_for_zdr (quarry-runtime/src/events.rs:137) and the NATS envelope zdr key (apps/Model Plane/go/pkg/natsx/publisher.go:57). Reuse `Claims::effective_zdr_mode` as the single posture source and page_renderer.rs's URL-free ZDR log as the field-discipline pattern.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Do not add a ZDR header or NATS header: Quarry derives posture from the token floor or body flag and ignores a service-principal zdr claim (auth.rs:104-120), so a third carrier would go unread. Each plane owns its telemetry init (quarry-edge, mp-telemetry, gateway zdr.rs), so the helper is a documented contract, not shared code. Keep content-free counters available to ZDR tenants, noting the edge metrics crates are inert today (telemetry.rs:6-18). Risks: partial adoption gives false confidence, so enforce with a lint; over-suppression hides incidents.

**Quick win.** Phase 1 is a field sweep plus one test and removes the one known debug-level leak.

<a id="F-t2-005"></a>
### F-t2-005 · One strict ScrapeOptions contract: formats[], inline return (the ZDR path) and main-content controls
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Ingestion Plane (quarry-core, quarry-transform, quarry-edge); thin clients elsewhere | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Define ScrapeOptions once in quarry-core and scope the first slice to `POST /v1/scrape`: `formats[]` with inline markdown, html, links and metadata under a size cap with FormatRef fallback; `onlyMainContent` and include/exclude selectors via a clean_html stage ahead of readability; a selectable raw or clean_full extractor; `effectiveOptions` and a scrape_id in the response; `GET /v1/scrape/:id` for non-ZDR runs. Switch model-gateway web_fetch to the inline path. Embedding in crawl, batch, search hydration and schedules is a follow-on.

**Why it matters.** Every agent web_fetch pays two calls (scrape, then artifact GET), and a ZDR scrape returns no text because outputs exist only as artifact refs never written under ZDR. Inline return is the natural ZDR path. One validated contract lets the Model propose options and Quarry reject bad ones with field-level 400s.

**What the references do.** Inline scrape is 2 of 4 references (Firecrawl, Apify), table stakes for a scrape API; a shared strict options contract and scrape-by-id are each 1 of 4 (Firecrawl). Firecrawl validates every request against one zod strictObject, takes a per-team concurrency permit with a bounded wait (two thirds of the timeout), runs the pipeline in-process and returns one Document with per-format fields and metadata; `waitFor` is capped at half the timeout [SRC]. Every transformer runs and checks its own option, redaction runs early, and a final coercion strips unrequested fields [SRC]. The job-access row is written even for ZDR requests; `GET /scrape/:id` is refused only for teams forced into ZDR, so writing nothing under ZDR is a Verevon choice [SRC]. Its native cleaner removes 42 chrome selectors unless a node holds one of 13 force-include regions [SRC]. The extractor choice with fallback comes from Apify's Website Content Crawler and keys on whether the page is a clear article [DOC]; Apify Web Fetch offers only markdown, html, text, links and raw with a 10 MB cap [DOC]. Caveats: Firecrawl silently defaults `skipTlsVerification` to true unless headers or actions are set, never copy that; its openapi.json lags the code.

**Verevon today.** ScrapeRequest takes url, prev_fingerprint, cache (with max_age_s), zdr, privacy, signals, ingest, org_id, render hints and prefer_http3 (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:345-375): no formats, timeout, selectors or deny_unknown_fields, so the gateway's formats and maxPages keys (gateway search.rs:112-140) are silently ignored. Only POST is routed (routes.rs:131). Output is FormatRef only (quarry-core/src/output.rs:129-150); under ZDR html and markdown become None (quarry-runtime/src/pipeline.rs:597-690), and model-gateway makes a second artifact call (model-gateway/src/quarry.rs:755-775). One readability pass runs (pipeline.rs:506) with a whole-HTML fallback only on failure (readability.rs:235-240). docs/OSS_PARITY_BACKLOG.md:18-34 claims parity; the code contradicts it. Reuse readability.rs, links.rs, metadata.rs, images.rs, attributes.rs, CachePolicy.max_age_s and `zdr::guard` with WriteKind.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Ingestion Plane owns the contract. ZDR: inline return writes no artifacts and no cache, no scrape-by-id for ZDR, and any URL-plus-options cache is org-keyed and bypassed under ZDR. Keep a lenient unknown-field period because gateway callers already send extra keys. Adopting schemars is a new dependency decision; openapi.yaml is hand-maintained; verification moved the OpenAPI-generation slice with a CI route-coverage check from [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064) into this item. Crawl and batch are refused under ZDR and handed to the Go orchestrator, so embedding there is L. Risks: large payloads, selector lists deleting real content (golden-page fixtures), and output changes shifting monitor fingerprints.

**Quick win.** The scrape-only slice reuses existing transformers and removes a round trip from every agent fetch.

<a id="F-t2-006"></a>
### F-t2-006 · Reap abandoned agent runs (idle and absolute TTL), cap concurrent runs, and make the fleet console real
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Ingestion Plane (small Frontend change) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Add a sweeper in quarry-edge that closes agent runs past their lease `ttl_s` or an idle timeout, refreshes last-activity on steps, attached viewers and takeover, and calls the existing close_run routine so the lease is released and a run.expired event is emitted. Add org-scoped and global caps on concurrent runs at start_run with a typed 429 plus retry-after, counting UI-created sessions too. Checkpoint non-ZDR runs for resume with run-id affinity routing rather than a shared registry. As a separate S, proxy `/v1/fleets` through the gateway and add the missing cancel route, or hide the fleet route until then.

**Why it matters.** An abandoned user or AI session holds a Chromium page and a usable grant with no user present: a resource leak on a shared host that grows with every run, and there is no cap on agent runs at all. The SPA has no beforeunload or sendBeacon close, so a closed tab or crash leaves the session open.

**What the references do.** Idle plus absolute TTLs for a multi-tenant server are best shown by Firecrawl; TTL handling appears in 2 of 4 references (Aside, Firecrawl). Firecrawl's hosted sessions carry an absolute `ttl` (default 600 s, max 3600) and an `activityTtl` (default 300 s), passed to the closed Hangar service as max lifetime and idle timeout [SRC; enforcement INF]. Aside, a local single-user daemon, gates run start with a global semaphore of 200, limits active sub-agents to 8 per run, and appends a text warning to tool output at 5 open tabs [SRC, from the daemon blob]. Its `flushIdleTabs` sweeper closes agent-owned tabs only for sessions in idle, interrupted or aborted status whose `updatedAt` is older than `closeAfterIdleMinutes` (default 15); it does not reap running runs and no event emission was seen [SRC]. Terminal ephemeral sessions are purged after 15 minutes; Aside has no absolute run TTL [SRC]. No reference evidence supports a fleet console. Caveat: Firecrawl's `maxBrowserSessions` is declared in team flags but no enforcement was found.

**Verevon today.** start_run computes ttl_s (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:1446, 1502) but nothing reads it; enforce_step_budget checks max_steps only (446-451); runs leave the registry only through close_run (3623-3626). The agent ChromiumoxideDriver is one shared browser whose acquire never blocks (quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:1914-1942; main.rs:1284-1288); the RuntimeLeasePool of 2 and 4 (main.rs:323, 337) gates only scrape rendering. The 15-minute grant TTL (browser-broker/internal/server/server.go:22) bounds grant use, not leaked pages. FleetRunConsole.tsx fetches /v1/fleets directly, the gateway has no such route, and its Pause button PATCHes an unregistered route (fleet_routes.rs:216-222). Reuse close_run, AgentRunCheckpoint and resume_checkpoint, control_mode as the takeover signal, and the quarry-control janitor pattern.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Ingestion Plane owns the browser lifecycle; grant revocation is a BrowserBroker call and optional given the grant TTL. A RunEntry owns a process-local Chromium session, so durable state only enables resume and cross-instance steps need sticky routing. ZDR runs are never checkpointed and simply expire. Never reap a run a user is watching or has taken over. Shares admission code with [F-t2-025](#F-t2-025), gains visibility from [F-t2-029](#F-t2-029), and gives [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008) its runtime enforcement.

**Quick win.** The sweeper reuses the existing close routine and the ttl_s already stored on every lease.

<a id="F-t2-007"></a>
### F-t2-007 · Stop viewport-triggered browser relaunches: per-page emulation, a small Chromium process pool and page hygiene
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Ingestion Plane (quarry-browser, quarry-edge) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** Part A (S): remove the viewport from browser identity and apply `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` per page or context at attach, together with `Page.setWebLifecycleState(active)`, `Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled(true)` and a fixed per-lease viewport. Part B (M): add an admission cap, idle reaper and health probe for the agent driver path, then optionally a pool of K Chromium processes with max open pages and retire-after-N, moving only the browser handle, pages and the single-slot live_frame_cache into per-slot state. Put the page-opening probe behind `/ready`, not the unauthenticated `/health`.

**Why it matters.** ensure_browser closes the shared Chromium and clears every session's pages, receipts, dialogs and telemetry whenever a lease's viewport differs, so a Model Plane run (no viewport) and a UI session (always 1280x800) on one edge destroy each other's live sessions; ensure_browser also runs on every goto, so mismatched sessions ping-pong. The in-app browser and AI runs need predictable capacity and crash containment, and pages that check focus misbehave without focus emulation.

**What the references do.** A browser process pool is 1 of 4 references (Apify's Crawlee BrowserPool, public source and docs): a new page goes to the first active browser below `maxOpenPagesPerBrowser` (default 20), a browser retires after 100 pages and closes when empty or via a 300 s inactivity sweeper, with pre and post hooks for launch, page create and page close [DOC, plus public source]. Firecrawl's Playwright service launches one Chromium and bounds concurrent pages with a semaphore (default 10); its `/health` creates a fresh context and blank page and closes them, with no navigation and no deadline [SRC]. Page hygiene is 2 of 4 references (Aside, Codex). Aside on attach calls Page.enable, DOM, Network and Inspector enable, `Page.setWebLifecycleState` active, `Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled` and `Target.setAutoAttach` flatten, guarded by an internal flag, with a stable 1440-wide AI tab viewport [SRC, from the daemon blob]. Codex disables Electron background throttling only while a tab is visible, driven or captured, a desktop-shell mechanism [SRC]. The Rust slot-vector plus semaphore shape is a Verevon proposal [INF].

**Verevon today.** ChromiumoxideDriver keeps one Browser plus one active_viewport (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:114-126); ensure_browser (194-270) runs from acquire (1914-1926), goto (1991) and open_tab_page (378) and relaunches on any mismatch because the viewport is applied only at launch (244-246); no Emulation, focus or lifecycle call exists in the crate. The agent path acquires with no pool, semaphore or reaper (quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:1512-1513); `/health` is a static ok (routes.rs:309). The gateway always sends 1280x800 (gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:481-489). Production frequency is unverified. Reuse the per-session isolated CDP contexts and the session-keyed maps for receipts, policies, dialogs and telemetry; the lease contract (quarry-core/src/lease.rs:19-22) already allows per-page emulation.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Pure Ingestion Plane change; gateway and SPA need none. The per-session pinned egress proxy and CDP Fetch boundary must stay bound to each session if sessions move between processes, and `--disable-quic` must remain when the pinned proxy is on (chromiumoxide.rs:236-243). ZDR sessions stay non-persistent. Acceptance test: a 1280x800 lease and a None-viewport lease concurrent on one driver, neither losing its page. The substrate choice needs the ADR shared with [F-t2-059](#F-t2-059) and [F-t2-060](#F-t2-060). Risks: memory pressure from more processes and receipt ordering when state is re-keyed.

**Quick win.** Part A alone removes the cross-session kill with a handful of CDP calls.

<a id="F-t2-008"></a>
### F-t2-008 · Enforce cost and runtime budgets in the Quarry executor, meter browser time, and clamp crawls to the remaining entitlement
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Ingestion Plane (enforcement) + Model Plane cost-core (ledger) + Control Plane (entitlements) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Slice 1 (S): add a spend accumulator and start time to RunEntry, add the per-step cost already computed for receipts, and replace enforce_step_budget with an enforce_budget covering steps, cost and wall clock that returns a typed budget-exhausted error. Apply it only to agent-initiated runs or steps, or stop the gateway passing 12 steps and 120 s for manual sessions and use an idle TTL instead. Slice 2 (M): emit a browser-second UsageEvent on close_run, clamp crawl and batch page limits and request rate to the remaining entitlement and return the clamped value, and have cost-core carry the remaining budget in the BrowserBroker grant and reconcile once per run id.

**Why it matters.** Agents with live browsers and paid providers can run up unbounded cost. docs/WEB_AGENT_ROADMAP_EXECUTION.md:50-53 says max_cost_usd is enforced; the HTTP step path does not enforce it [SRC]. Both crawl platforms enforce in the executor, which is where Quarry must do it.

**What the references do.** Executor-side budget enforcement is 2 of 4 references (Firecrawl, Apify). Firecrawl's browser-session create runs a credit check for the full TTL (402 if short) and reserves only a concurrency slot (429); a debit-ahead exists only for keyless use. On stop it locks the session row with SELECT ... FOR UPDATE, settles the actual duration exactly once, and a periodic sweep settles sessions whose stop was missed [SRC]. Rates are plain browser versus a session that used a prompt, plus a ZDR surcharge. Its CostTracking class can throw on a limit, but no open-source caller sets one, so it is dormant [SRC; hosted enforcement INF]. At crawl submit, limit becomes min(requested, remaining credits) and only the clamped value is stored; maxConcurrency is capped to the team limit; results expire after `crawlTtlHours` (default 24) [SRC]. Apify lets an Actor call charge(event, count) within a caller-set maximum total charge and aborts the run automatically when reached; "finish the step and persist partial results" is a Verevon proposal [DOC; proposal INF]. Caveats: the Apify half is documentation only; Firecrawl sessions need the hosted Hangar service.

**Verevon today.** enforce_step_budget checks max_steps only (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:446-451, called at 2294); default_constraints (433-440) leaves cost and runtime unbounded; max_runtime_s only becomes the lease ttl_s (1446). Cost and runtime logic exists in the library AgentLoop (quarry-runtime/src/agent_loop.rs:154-163), which has no HTTP caller. Per-step cost_usd is computed for receipts (agent_routes.rs:2346-2362) but never accumulated; usage.rs has no browser metric. Crawls are bounded only by max_pages, where absent means unlimited (quarry-orchestrator/internal/workflows/workflows.go:456), plus a 30-minute Temporal timeout (jobs/dispatcher.go:254). execution-core enforces steps and runtime on its own clock but never checks max_cost_usd (browser_agent.rs:320-330, 666). Reuse the receipt cost functions, AgentLoop over_budget, UsageEvent and UsageMeter, and the quarry-control credit-usage read model.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Enforcement belongs in the Quarry step admission path; cost-core owns model spend only; Control Plane owns entitlements, which quarry-control does not model as a ceiling today. The gateway creates every manual session with max_steps 12 and max_runtime_s 120 and human actions share `/step` (gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:481-488, 663-732), so naive enforcement would cap human browsing [INF]. Resolve caps as min(request, grant claim, org default) and fail closed. Under ZDR, receipts are skipped, so accumulate in memory and keep usage events content-free. Depends on [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001), [F-t2-006](#F-t2-006), [F-t2-022](#F-t2-022), [F-t2-046](#F-t2-046) and [F-t2-014](#F-t2-014). Risks: double counting between Quarry and cost-core (one ledger key per run), flat-table estimates stopping runs early (label estimated versus provider-billed), and silent clamping (always return the clamped value).

<a id="F-t2-009"></a>
### F-t2-009 · Route PDFs and Office files to the right parser in /v1/scrape (Content-Type plus magic bytes)
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | S | Ingestion Plane (quarry-runtime pipeline, quarry-transform) | W1 | High |

**What to build.** After a driver returns a body, decide the content kind from the Content-Type header and then from the first bytes (`%PDF-`, a zip local-file header for Office Open XML). If the kind is PDF or DOCX, hand the already-fetched bytes to the matching quarry-transform parser instead of running charset decoding and readability over binary data, and record the detected kind in output metadata. Legacy OLE `.doc`, images, archives and media get a typed `unsupported_media` skip reason.

**Why it matters.** PDFs and Office files linked from public sites are a common knowledge-base input. Today a PDF URL goes through readability and produces markdown that users see is wrong at once, even though a PDF text extractor already ships in the answer path.

**What the references do.** Firecrawl is the only reference that routes files to parsers: 1 of 4 references, and table-stakes for a Firecrawl-class scraper [SRC].
