Its `specialtyScrapeCheck` checks a cloud-storage PDF reference first, then the Content-Type header, then magic bytes from a signature table (`%PDF-`, `PK 03 04` for OOXML/ODF, OLE2 for legacy Office); the sniff window is derived from the table, and the zip check excludes genuine ZIP media types and does not look for `[Content_Types].xml` [SRC]. A non-HTML kind raises a typed handoff that carries the downloaded bytes, so the parser never re-downloads [SRC]. Archives, audio, video and executables are rejected explicitly; image routing needs a deployment switch plus a per-request parsers option, otherwise images are rejected [SRC]. Apify's Website Content Crawler selects files by content type but only downloads them and does not extract text [DOC]. Caveat: Firecrawl's PDF quality depends on the external FirePDF service in the hosted product [SRC].

**Verevon today.** `PageRunner` reads Content-Type only for the `PageFetched` event and charset decode, then runs readability over every body (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/pipeline.rs:441-506). `is_pdf` (header or `%PDF-` in the first 1 KiB) lives in quarry-runtime/src/answer.rs:679-753, and answer.rs:839 is the only caller of `pdf::extract_text` (25 MB cap, quarry-transform/src/pdf.rs:40, 202-213). `docx::extract_text` exists with the same cap but has no non-test caller (quarry-transform/src/docx.rs:1-40). `images.rs` holds only HTML lazy-image and srcset helpers (images.rs:1-40), so there is no image parser to route to. Reuse `is_pdf`, the two parsers with their caps, `charset::decode`, the `PageFetched` content_type metadata and `zdr::guard`.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Ingestion Plane only. Add the sniff after the driver fetch in pipeline.rs, reuse the existing PDF sniffer, add the zip-header check confirmed by a `[Content_Types].xml` entry so plain zip archives are not sent to the DOCX parser, and route PDF and DOCX only. Keep the byte caps (16 MB pipeline body, 25 MB per parser), parser timeouts and zip decompression-bomb limits. Under Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) extracted text stays inline with no artifact write. Record the kind in metadata because changed output for non-HTML bodies changes the fingerprints that change monitors compare. Image bodies need OCR or a vision call, a separate M-sized item; `services/quarry-vision-sidecar` is the likely home. Risk: large or malicious files, so enforce caps and sandbox parsers.

**Quick win.** The sniffer and both parsers exist; the work is wiring plus a typed skip reason.

<a id="F-t2-010"></a>
### F-t2-010 · Turn on the LLM browser planner by default, with budgets, a browsing procedure prompt and connector-first routing
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Model Plane | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Move the durable browser planner in execution-core from the `QUARRY_BROWSER_AGENT_LLM` environment gate to an org-policy flag, once per-run step, time and token budgets exist. Add a versioned browsing procedure to the planner prompt (observe, act, re-observe to confirm; escalation ladder; stop rules) and widen the planner's action set to the Quarry verbs it cannot reach today (press, select, back, wait_for). Put connector-first routing guidance in the parent chat runtime loop so the model prefers Integration Plane provider actions or Quarry search and scrape before opening a browser, unless the user asked for the browser.

**Why it matters.** The durable run and chat autopilot are the paths users rely on for long tasks, and today they fall back to a deterministic planner unless an operator sets an environment variable. A procedure prompt raises task success at near-zero cost, and connector-first routing is cheaper, more reliable and honours provider OAuth scopes.

**What the references do.** A multi-step model loop over browser tools is table-stakes: 3 of 4 references (Firecrawl, Aside, Codex; Apify has no agent loop) [SRC]. Firecrawl's interact loop is the shape to copy: a capped interactive snapshot (about 40k characters) plus URL goes to the model with a single tool, the model re-runs snapshot after each command (no diff), an action-log reminder is injected each step, and the run stops at 25 steps or a per-command timeout [SRC]. Its only guardrail is a regex denylist for opening tabs, with no allowlist and no token or cost budget, so cite it for loop shape, not guardrails [SRC]. A procedure prompt is present in 2 of 4 references. Aside's prompt gives the real ladder: interactive snapshot, full snapshot, wait and re-snapshot, then annotated or raw screenshot; an action is unconfirmed until a fresh snapshot, strategy switches after two or three failures, progress is posted about every 30 seconds [SRC]. The "AX ref, semantic locator, set-of-marks, coordinates" ladder is a Verevon synthesis [INF]. Connector-first routing is unique to Codex (1 of 4) and is skill text the model is told to follow, not a code-level router [DOC]. Caveat: Codex procedure and efficiency caps are enforced only by model compliance [DOC].

**Verevon today.** `LlmPlanner::from_env` returns `None` unless `QUARRY_BROWSER_AGENT_LLM` is set (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/llm_planner.rs:128-148) and nothing under apps/Model Plane sets it, so the deterministic planner runs by default (tool_bridge/mod.rs:170-232). The in-app browser is not agent-less: the SPA's suggest-action loop works without the flag (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:2015-2100). The durable planner's `ActionType` is goto, click, type, extract, observe, scroll and wait (browser_agent.rs:91-99) with a one-paragraph prompt and a 512-token cap (llm_planner.rs:170-263); press, select and back exist on Quarry's wire (quarry_agent.rs:125-175) but are unreachable, and get_content is already reachable through extract and observe. No per-run token budget or routing policy exists. Reuse `planner_infer_request` and `ACTION_SCHEMA`, `check_limits`, `stop_criteria` and the browser_risk HITL gates (browser_agent.rs:225-345), and the suggest-action prompt as the richer base.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Model Plane execution-core, in order: budgets (a per-run token budget is new work, since `max_cost_usd` feeds Quarry's flat action-cost table and per-org spend lives in cost-core), then prompt and schema, then the org-policy flag. Every widened action must map onto Quarry's governed `AgentAction` set and keep per-step grant, domain and risk gates: the Model proposes, Quarry executes or rejects. Default-on depends on [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) (the durable run needs a broker grant and bearers the gateway cannot supply in production) and on [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021) (raw observation text enters the prompt unscrubbed). ZDR: model-gateway refuses durable browser runs under ZDR (browser_run.rs:297-310), so the flag cannot apply there. Risks: runaway cost without budgets, over-long prompts, eager routing that ignores an explicit browser request.

<a id="F-t2-011"></a>
### F-t2-011 · Let users browse freely: a human-session policy plus an omnibox that routes free text to Verevon search
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Ingestion Plane (Quarry) with Model Plane broker and Frontend Plane omnibox | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Two deliverables. (a) Frontend slice (S to M): URL-like omnibox input navigates, free text opens Verevon search through the existing gateway search route or Ask into chat, a suggestion dropdown is fed by the gateway suggest route plus local history, and hard reload with error-page retry is added. (b) Backend slice (L): a human-browsing grant kind in the Model Plane BrowserBroker that Quarry validates instead of exact domain-list equality, with renewal for idle sessions, and Quarry human-session semantics that exempt human steps from `max_steps` and use an idle timeout instead of a 120 s lease. Egress and SSRF (server-side request forgery) policy stay unchanged.

**Why it matters.** A browser locked to its first host for 120 seconds is not a browser, and today free text in the address bar becomes a google.com URL that the domain lock rejects. This is the largest day-one usability gap for people.

**What the references do.** Codex's in-app browser is the model. It ships back, forward, reload and hard reload (ignore cache) as commands, its preload captures mouse buttons 3 and 4, and reload from an error page retries the failed URL [SRC]. Its omnibox is a token-based suggestion service: `start(query)` streams matches with contents, description, destination URL and accept and delete tokens; `accept(token)` navigates and records; `deleteMatch` removes a history entry [SRC]. Navigation controls are recorded in 1 of 4 references (Codex) and inferred for Aside as a Chromium browser [INF]; omnibox suggestions are in 2 of 4 (Codex, Aside). Aside wraps the upstream Chromium omnibox in a private extension API with an AI mode that attaches tabs [SRC]. Free cross-site human browsing in Codex is inferred from the absence of a URL allowlist for human navigation [INF]. Caveats: the Codex browser pane and browser-use are remote-config gated and default to false; its autocomplete uses an API stock Electron lacks, so a patched Chromium omnibox and its suggestion sources are unverified [INF].

**Verevon today.** Back, forward, soft reload, new tab and a human-takeover control mode ship (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/BrowserChrome.tsx:1143-1215; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:255-300, 690-790), but every user session is created with `allowed_domains` = first host, `max_steps` 12 and `max_runtime_s` 120 (browser.rs:483-490), and Quarry applies the same budget and domain list to human steps (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:446-451, 2258-2300). In governed mode the broker list must be non-empty, at most 32 hosts and equal on every step (quarry-browser/src/lib.rs:55-77; agent_routes.rs:314-339), and grants expire after 15 minutes (apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/internal/server/server.go:22). The omnibox turns free text into a google.com URL (browser-omnibox.ts:14-15) with no suggestions. Reuse `BrowserControlMode` and timeline initiator tagging, the gateway search/web, suggest and suggestions routes (search.rs:36-70, 100-140), the CrossDomainNavigation approval gate, the egress private-IP blocks and the `PersistentSessionRegistry` idle-reaper pattern (persistent_session.rs:55-110).

**How to build it in Verevon.** Host authority is broker-only in governed mode, so the policy is a Model Plane BrowserBroker feature validated by Quarry; neither the gateway nor Quarry may widen the list alone, and the item depends on [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) and shares idle and absolute TTL work with [F-t2-035](#F-t2-035). Agent-initiated cross-domain navigation keeps its HITL gate, and human-versus-agent origin comes from Quarry's control mode, never a client flag. ZDR: no local or server history or suggestions. The gateway search cache needs org and user keys and ZDR gating before it feeds suggestions ([F-t2-003](#F-t2-003)). Space access must not widen a user's host policy. Risks: SSRF and egress abuse, unbounded host growth, cost of long-lived Chromium leases without idle reaping.

<a id="F-t2-012"></a>
### F-t2-012 · Chat beside the browser: a dockable browser panel bound to the chat run
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Frontend Plane | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Two slices. (a) Frontend (M): give the existing chat live-run panel dock positions (right, bottom, maximised, hidden) persisted per user, a floating composer over the page, and a "new chat per tab or keep the chat" rule. (b) Backend (L): bind the panel to a live, steerable session, either through a gateway projection of execution-core-owned Quarry runs (owner-gated, exposing Quarry's frame stream and control routes) or by letting a chat turn act on a user-created session, plus real BrowserBroker grant issuance for UI-driven runs.

**Why it matters.** Both reference browsers converge on chat and page side by side: the user watches, takes over and asks about the visible page in one view. Verevon has the pieces but the chat view is read-only and shows the agent's separate page.

**What the references do.** Present in 2 of 4 references (Codex, Aside); table-stakes for a chat-plus-browser product. Codex hosts the browser pane with destinations new-window, left, right, bottom, pin, chat and new-chat, plus a hidden browser-use host; commands toggle the panel and step through workspace layouts, and a floating composer is revealed when the pointer nears the bottom of the page, with a thread-scoped option to hide it [SRC]. That the composer posts into the active chat is inferred; the send path was not traced [INF]. Aside runs a per-tab side panel: `sidePanel.setOptions` per tab with a session id, session resolution when a tab activates, a new-session or keep-session setting (default new-session), and a "sidecar to this page" framing block injected on the first user message for the first attached tab [SRC]. Caveats: the Codex pane sits behind a server-delivered gate that defaults to false [SRC]; Aside is a local single-user browser [SRC].

**Verevon today.** A right-docked, collapsible chat split exists: ChatPage mounts `ChatLiveRunPanel` (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/components/ChatPage.tsx:957-984), which reuses `BrowserChrome` in compact mode (ChatLiveRunPanel.tsx:608) with pause and resume. It is read-only, fed by per-step artifact screenshots, and only for planned and deep-research turns: `browserSessionFromChatRun` leaves every live-frame URL null and the session id undefined, so manual controls stay disabled (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/lib/chat-run-watch.ts:402-452). The interactive BrowserChrome with takeover, approvals and frame streaming is mounted only in the Crawl composer (KnowledgeScrapePreview.tsx:253). There is no dock position, floating composer or new-chat rule, only a collapsed flag (chat-run-watch.ts:45). Reuse the panel and its compact mount, the chat-run-watch adapter, the gateway frame, control, timeline and artifact routes (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:371-444), the `owned_orchestration_run` ownership proof (browser.rs:3310-3347) and the ChatWorkspaceCanvas layout as dock host.

**How to build it in Verevon.** The Frontend Plane owns presentation only; page context reaches the Model Plane only through governed `browser.observe` results or attachments, never raw DOM from the SPA. A chat turn's browser runs inside execution-core with its own Quarry lease and grant (browser.rs:3320-3345), while the dashboard's `start_ai_run` acquires a separate lease and only re-navigates to the tab's URL (browser.rs:1237, 1311-1318), so the user's page and the agent's page differ today; closing that is the L part. Takeover goes through the gateway control route and Quarry control mode, which depends on [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013); UI-driven runs still use an interim `session:{id}` grant id (browser.rs:1258-1262), so [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) is a prerequisite. ZDR chat runs have no frames: do not persist dock state server-side and keep the panel's honest ZDR copy. Risks: small-screen layout and frame bandwidth beside streaming chat.

<a id="F-t2-013"></a>
### F-t2-013 · Co-browsing: the agent works in the user's visible session in agent-flagged tabs, with takeover enforced inside Quarry
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Ingestion Plane (control lease, tabs) + Model Plane (attach mode) + Frontend Plane (tab UI) | W2 (phase 1 in W1) | High |

**What to build.** Phase 1 (S, ships alone in W1): Quarry's `/step` derives the initiator from the verified principal (service principal means agent, user token means human), rejects agent steps while `control_mode` is human takeover with a typed `USER_TOOK_OVER`, stamps a monotonic control epoch on control events and receipts, and records the initiator in step receipts and tab operations. Phase 2: an attach mode so a Model Plane run binds to the user's Quarry session through a session-bound child grant and works in an agent-flagged tab that opens without stealing focus, with Reveal, Take over and Give back. Phase 3: deliverable and handoff tab marks so finished pages stay open.

**Why it matters.** Co-browsing is the core of a browser that users and AI share. Today the AI run drives a separate hidden lease, and takeover is enforced only in the Frontend gateway, so a Model Plane run that calls Quarry directly keeps acting after the user takes over. This corrects an earlier strength claim that takeover is authoritative in Quarry.

**What the references do.** No reference implements server-side control-mode arbitration of agent steps; per-step takeover enforcement is a Verevon design [INF]. The confirmed pieces come from Codex: `markTab` with handoff or deliverable marks scoped to a turn id, where turn end releases marked tabs and keeps a handoff tab open; Interrupt, SubagentStop and Stop hooks that end the turn; and hidden agent tabs hosted in a webview that stays at about:blank until the snapshot URL is known and opens without focus [SRC]. Codex's `user_took_over` is only the decline reason of its secure sign-in elicitation; in its in-app browser the user can interact at any time with no input lock found [SRC]. Its fail-closed tab-mention rule (match provider tab id, title and URL, never guess) is prompt instruction, not runtime enforcement, and manual handoff works only on the cloud backend [DOC]. Aside shows a "controlling the tab / Take over" strip and keeps owned-versus-borrowed session tabs with idle sweep and orphan reconciliation, but no daemon-side takeover handling was found [SRC][INF]. Firecrawl exposes an interactive live-view URL on its hosted browser sessions, so a human can act in the agent's session [SRC].

**Verevon today.** `BrowserControlMode` is persisted on the run record (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/step_receipts.rs:55-90) and set by `set_browser_session_control`, which hard-codes `initiated_by` Human (agent_routes.rs:1733-1785). Enforcement lives only in the gateway from a caller-supplied actor field (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:696-723, 2065-2075); the Quarry step handler (agent_routes.rs:2227-2294) never reads `control_mode`, receipts carry only the run owner, tab events have no owner field and there is no control epoch. A Model Plane run never attaches: `start_run` leases its own session with profile_id, start_url and an interim grant string (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/browser_agent.rs:674; browser.rs:1235). Reuse the persisted control mode, the Control and Tab timeline events, `Claims::is_service` and `actor_id` (quarry-edge/src/auth.rs:60-139), and the broker's `SessionKey`, `ParentGrantID` and scoped-approval helpers (apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/internal/grant/grant.go:44-130).

**How to build it in Verevon.** Enforcement moves into Quarry `/step`; the gateway check stays as UX-level early rejection. Phase 2 is blocked by ownership: `caller_owns_browser_run` (agent_routes.rs:474-482) requires the caller's actor id to equal the run actor, execution-core calls Quarry with an org-scoped service token, and Claims has no on-behalf-of field, so a Model Plane run gets 404 on a user-owned session. Attach needs an explicit delegation design (Auth Core claim or session-bound child grant) that never widens the user's ACL or Space authority, scheduled after [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) and treated as L to XL. [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011) covers the human navigation policy, [F-t2-038](#F-t2-038) the agent-activity presentation. ZDR sessions stay non-persistent (control timeline writes are skipped, agent_routes.rs:493-495). Risks: races between an in-flight step and takeover (hence the epoch), tab leaks without an idle sweeper.

**Quick win.** Phase 1 only: small, Ingestion Plane only, and it closes a real safety gap.

<a id="F-t2-014"></a>
### F-t2-014 · Surface one canonical agent run trace in the UI: typed events, receipts, live view, correlation envelope
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Model Plane (trace contract); Ingestion Plane and Frontend Plane deliver their halves | W2 | Medium |

**What to build.** First slice (S to M): gateway proxies for Quarry's receipts, egress-receipts and procedure routes, level and substring filters on the devtools route, and a read-only `browser.console` model tool. Later slice (L): extend the existing Model Plane `Event` envelope with `producer_seq` and agent or origin fields, publish Quarry browser-session, step-receipt, egress-decision and artifact events in it on NATS (persisted unless ZDR), add `x-request-id` intake as a tower layer in quarry-edge, a run origin enum, and real `from_seq` replay for Quarry's agent-event SSE (server-sent events). The existing single-run surfaces gain egress-receipt and procedure panels.

**Why it matters.** This is what users watch while the agent browses. Without it, "the Model proposes, Quarry executes or rejects" is invisible, approvals lack context and support cannot follow one run across gateway, Model Plane, Quarry and audit.

**What the references do.** A full versioned run-trace envelope is in 1 of 4 references (Firecrawl, beta), with Codex contributing UI-side pieces. Firecrawl's contract carries schemaVersion, eventId, runId, occurredAt, producerSequence and agent; producerSequence is monotonic per emitting agent, so consumers group by agent id and then order [SRC]. Only the OpenAPI contract and a proxy controller are in the repo; the events come from an upstream beta service, so persistence and ordering are not inspectable [SRC]. Firecrawl has no edge middleware for `x-request-id`: provider-tool routes adopt or generate and echo it and use it for safe retries, proxies forward it, and billing is keyed by it; the tower-layer design is Verevon's own [SRC]. Correlation ids and run origin appear in 3 of 4 references; Apify runs carry a `meta.origin` enum (WEB, API, CLI, SCHEDULER, WEBHOOK, ACTOR, MCP and others) [DOC]. Agent-readable console logs are in 2 of 4: Codex's `tab_dev_logs` takes levels (debug, info, log, warn, error), a filter and a limit; every result carries surface and tab metadata, and async notifications are drained into the next result but suppressed after native credential use [SRC].

**Verevon today.** A canonical envelope exists (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/events.proto:8-60: event_id, schema_version, producer, correlation_id, causation_id, org_id, user_id, zdr, fields 14-20 reserved), with EventLog query and subscribe on an opaque cursor (eventlog.proto:26-37, 50-90) and an OrchestrationEvent run stream carrying browser action, observation and approval events (orchestration.proto:990-1040). The SPA renders these in ChatLiveRunPanel (ChatLiveRunPanel.tsx:360-380) and a per-session timeline in BrowserTimelineDetail. Missing: Quarry's receipts, egress-receipts, procedure and agent-events routes (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:3660-3730) are not exposed by the gateway router (browser.rs:368-470); the agent SSE accepts `from_seq` but replay is a no-op (agent_routes.rs:2650-2706); `DevtoolsQuery` has only after_sequence and limit (agent_routes.rs:1055-1072); Quarry handlers mint their own request id with no `x-request-id` intake. FleetRunConsole is a multi-run console against an unrouted `/v1/fleets`, not the target. Reuse the Event and EventType, the EventLog cursor, the run event stream proxy, the UI surfaces, Quarry's owner timeline and StepReceipt store, and execution-core scrub.rs.

**How to build it in Verevon.** The Model Plane owns run and session events, so extend `model_plane.v1.Event` rather than define a second envelope; Quarry publishes in it and never writes Model Plane storage; the Application Plane projects for realtime; the gateway only proxies. ZDR runs stay live-only with no persisted trace, and the immutable egress receipt is not retained under ZDR ([F-t2-004](#F-t2-004)). Request ids are never an authorization or tenancy key; cap length and charset. All Quarry routes stay owner-scoped. AI-run start depends on [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001). Risks: event volume, cross-plane ordering, and prompt injection or secrets in console and page text, which need the same redaction and untrusted-content labelling as observations.

<a id="F-t2-015"></a>
### F-t2-015 · Actionability checks and trusted input for every ref-targeted action, then rich input actions
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Ingestion Plane | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Change Quarry's ref-targeted click, type and select path from in-page `element.click()` to a pipeline: hit-test the element centre, scroll into view only if it is not hittable, wait until attached, visible, enabled and stable, then dispatch trusted `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` and `Input.dispatchKeyEvent`. Return typed failures (covered by a named tag, not visible, disabled, unstable) and keep the synthetic path only as an explicit fallback. Split out as follow-ups: cross-process iframe hit-testing, and rich input (press with modifiers, drag, select_text, paste with a per-session virtual clipboard).

**Why it matters.** Every AI click passes through this executor. Untrusted synthetic clicks silently do nothing on sites that ignore them or sit behind overlays; an honest "covered by cookie banner" error lets the model recover instead of looping.

**What the references do.** Custom actionability pipelines are in 2 of 4 references (Codex, Aside); Playwright's auto-wait gives Firecrawl's playwright service and Apify's Crawlee similar behaviour by inheritance [INF]. Codex is the best implementation: it hit-tests with a shadow-root-aware in-page `elementFromPoint` probe injected per frame hop through `DOM.resolveNode` and `Runtime.callFunctionOn`, using `getContentQuads` and `getBoxModel` for coordinate transforms [SRC]. Order: hit-test first, `DOM.scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` only if not hittable, then a 25 ms poll with a 500 ms deadline and 0.5 px tolerance until two consecutive samples match [SRC]. Errors read "covered by <tag> or otherwise not hittable", "did not settle after scrolling" and "couldn't be clicked" for unpositioned nodes; input is then dispatched as trusted mouse and key events [SRC]. Aside's `waitForReady` checks attached, visible, enabled and stable with a 32 ms sample and a 5 s timeout, and falls back to DOM activation with checked-state verification when the target is obscured [SRC]. Rich input is not unique to Codex: Aside also has trusted CDP input with modifiers, insertText, file-chooser interception and download events; only the virtual clipboard and held clicks or drag are Codex-specific [SRC for Aside, from the daemon blob]; Codex's held-click, drag and paste action set is doc-backed [DOC], while its clipboard bridge is [SRC].

**Verevon today.** `execute_native_target_action` checks only `isConnected`, then calls `element.click()` or sets the value and dispatches synthetic events (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:1232-1330); the non-native verified-target path does the same in a page script (chromiumoxide.rs:2026-2093). There is no scroll-into-view, visibility, enabled, stability or covered-element check and no hit-test on the ref path. Only `click_point` (chromiumoxide.rs:2841) dispatches trusted mouse events, Press carries only a key string (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-core/src/contracts.rs:699), and the only typed target error is `TargetRepairRequired` (quarry-core/src/error.rs:30). Reuse the backend-node resolution and per-frame context lookup (chromiumoxide.rs:1232-1290), `click_point`'s moved, pressed, released sequence (2841-2896), the `BrowserDriverCapabilities` flag pattern (lib.rs:269-286) and `ActionOutcome::unknown(postcondition_required)` in observation.rs.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Ingestion Plane quarry-browser only. Express failures as `ErrorCode::TargetRepairRequired` (409) with `details.reason`, because `ErrorCode` is a wire contract shared with the Go, TypeScript and Model Plane clients. Gate the pipeline behind a new driver capability flag so the Browserbase, Browserless and Kernel adapters are unaffected, and leave the synthetic path for governed download clicks until trusted-click download behaviour is verified. Quarry keeps returning an unverified postcondition rather than claiming success, and upload and download gates must not weaken. The Model Plane `browser.act` schema is a closed list, so modifiers, drag and paste need a separate Model Plane contract change. Risks: cross-origin iframe transforms and regressions in existing flows, hence the flag.

<a id="F-t2-016"></a>
### F-t2-016 · Scoped, robots-compliant crawl: wire CrawlFrontier scoping, robots enforcement and typed denials into the live crawl path
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Stub | L | Ingestion Plane (quarry-control workflow + quarry-runtime frontier); Control Plane (override entitlement, audit) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** In risk order: (1) S: a default same-host (optionally subdomain) scope in both enqueue loops of the Go crawl workflow, since a crawl today follows external links up to `max_pages`. (2) M: a robots.txt preflight at enqueue and for batch through the guarded fetch path, with typed per-URL denial events returned by status and errors routes. (3) The scoping vocabulary on a ScrapeOptions-backed CrawlRequest (include and exclude patterns with ReDoS caps, subdomain and external-link switches, discovery depth, sitemap mode), an audited ignore-robots entitlement in the Control Plane, and a ZDR decision for the durable orchestrator path. Correct the GAP.md rows that call robots enforcement closed.

**Why it matters.** Scoping and robots compliance are table-stakes in both crawl references. Repo docs claim robots handling is closed while the code does not enforce it. Unscoped crawls waste budget and expose every org to compliance risk.

**What the references do.** Present in 2 of 4 references (Firecrawl, Apify); Codex and Aside have no crawler. Firecrawl's native Rust `filter_links` returns allowed links plus a denial map of typed codes, checking in order parse, protocol, depth, file type, section scope, backward links, exclude, include, robots, social media and external links [SRC]. Remediation sentences are built in TypeScript from the denial enum, several still name v1 options, and none names allowSubdomains [SRC]. Pattern caps are 1000 patterns and 100k characters in total across include and exclude, 2000 characters each [SRC]. robots.txt is fetched at crawl start through a forced engine list with an 8 s timeout and about one day of cache; fetch failure fails open and Crawl-delay is ignored [SRC]. Enforcement is on by default for crawl only; scrape checks robots only under Safe Mode or a team flag, and overrides are enterprise entitlements blocked under Safe Mode [SRC]. Apify's `enqueueLinks` offers a strategy, globs and a transformRequest hook, and robots respect is opt-in in Crawlee [DOC].

**Verevon today.** The Go `CrawlJobWF` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-orchestrator/internal/workflows/workflows.go:396-516) and its durable-frontier variant (518-610) enqueue every extracted href after only a depth check; `links::extract` keeps external links (quarry-transform/src/links.rs:1-23; quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:817-840). `CrawlRequest` has only url, max_pages, max_depth, ingest and zdr (routes.rs:533-552), and 0 means unlimited. `CrawlFrontier`, `CrawlDenialReason` and `RobotsCache` have no production caller; the only live scope and robots logic is `/v1/map` (quarry-edge/src/map_routes.rs:87-109, 160-260); batch accepts any URL list unchecked; crawl and batch are refused under ZDR (routes.rs:553-590). Reuse `merge_and_scope` and `host_in_scope`, `RobotsTxt` and `sitemap` from quarry-transform, the frontier and denial types, the RobotsCache TTL logic, the per-page `page_failed` and `page_blocked` events (workflows.go:174-202) and the `knowledge.crawl_site` action schema (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/shared/actions/action-registry.ts:568-571).

**How to build it in Verevon.** The Ingestion Plane owns crawl; frontier logic stays in Rust per ROADMAP Phase 5, so the Go workflow calls an edge endpoint or activity helper. `ReqwestRobotsFetcher` (quarry-runtime/src/robots_cache.rs:165-205) is a plain reqwest client without the SSRF guard or DNS pinning, so robots must be fetched through the guarded driver chain that `/v1/map` uses (map_routes.rs:118-128). A robots override is an audited Control Plane entitlement shared with [F-t2-022](#F-t2-022); the Model can propose a crawl but never request a bypass. The vocabulary rides on [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005), denials surface through [F-t2-017](#F-t2-017), sitemap seeding follows in [F-t2-041](#F-t2-041), and the user-agent token is unified in [F-t2-067](#F-t2-067). ZDR: the durable path refuses ZDR because `run_page` carries no zdr field and Temporal history stores URLs; lifting it is a design decision. Risks: changed defaults alter existing crawl results, so make them explicit with a legacy opt-out and show denial summaries in the UI; cap and linear-time-compile patterns against regex DoS.

<a id="F-t2-017"></a>
### F-t2-017 · Public async job lifecycle: status, paginated results, per-URL errors, cancel/pause/resume, origin and live tail
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Ingestion Plane (quarry-edge, quarry-control, quarry-orchestrator) + Frontend gateway | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Add authenticated, org-checked routes on the Quarry edge for crawl and batch jobs: `GET /v1/crawl/:id` (status, completed/total, warnings, expiry), `GET /v1/crawl/:id/errors` (per-URL failures including robots-blocked URLs), `GET /v1/crawl/ongoing`, `DELETE /v1/crawl/:id` and `POST /v1/crawl/:id/pause|resume`, with `/v1/batch` aliases. Turn `GET /v1/jobs/:id/events` into a live tail that sends a catch-up snapshot first and honours `Last-Event-ID`. Record a run origin (user, agent, schedule, webhook, api) on the job record and surface the controls in the crawl UI and as proposable Model Plane actions. Treat cursor-paged result data as a separate sub-item.

**Why it matters.** A user or agent who starts a crawl of up to 5000 pages has no way to stop, pause or inspect it from the product. Agents need one poll call and a cancel to honour budgets, and the audit model needs to know who started a run. Status and cancel are also the dependency for webhooks, waiters and monitors.

**What the references do.** Job status and cancel exist in 2 of 4 references (Firecrawl, Apify); a live stream with catch-up exists in 1 of 4 (Firecrawl). Firecrawl's `GET /crawl/:jobId` returns status, counts, credits, `expiresAt` and a page of documents that ends at 100 documents or 10 MiB, with a `next` cursor that advances by what was actually returned; `/errors` returns `[{id, timestamp, url, code, error, requiresAction}]` plus `robotsBlocked[]`; `DELETE` marks the crawl cancelled, cancels the queue group and answers 409 when already complete; results expire by a per-team TTL (time to live), 24 h by default [SRC]. Its WebSocket sends a `catchup` status message, then each finished document, then `done`, but it has no resume cursor, polls once a second server-side and there is no SSE (server-sent events) route in the source [SRC]. Apify runs have a persisted status, a graceful abort with a 30 s grace period, resurrection of a finished run with the same storage ids and a recorded `meta.origin`, but no pause [DOC]. Pause and resume are not a reference capability: Firecrawl has them only for monitors, not crawls, so they come from Quarry's own Temporal signals [SRC]. `Last-Event-ID` resume over an event history is a Verevon recommendation, not a reference mechanism [INF]. The step timeline idea comes from Firecrawl's legacy multi-page extract, not from crawl jobs [SRC, medium confidence].

**Verevon today.** The edge exposes only `POST /v1/crawl` and `/v1/batch` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:133-134), a generic jobs list and an events route that drains once and ends with a done frame (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/resource_routes.rs:1057-1127). Pause, resume, cancel and state queries exist only in `CrawlJobWF` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-orchestrator/internal/workflows/signals.go:87-146); `BatchJobWF` has no handlers (workflows.go:359-383), and nothing sends the signals: quarry-control's Temporal client is a `NoopClient` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-control/internal/temporal/client.go:91-130) and only the orchestrator dials Temporal (cmd/orchestrator/main.go:66), starting workflows as `quarry-job-<jobID>` (internal/jobs/dispatcher.go:246). The gateway proxies start, discover, list and events only (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/knowledge.rs:179-196). Reuse the control `GET`/`PUT /v1/jobs/{id}` record (resources.go:24-31, 486-530), the `page_failed`/`page_blocked` events (workflows.go:174-202), the gateway `crawl_run_events` proxy that already forwards `Last-Event-ID` (knowledge/quarry.rs:1098-1120) and the `ingestions.*` action registry (src/shared/actions/action-registry.ts:1320, 2563-2590).

**How to build it in Verevon.** Do not wire a Temporal client into quarry-control. Follow the repo's reconciler pattern: the edge writes a desired state (cancel, pause, resume) to the control job record via the existing `PUT`, and the orchestrator `jobs.Manager`, which already holds the client and the deterministic workflow id, sends the signal. Add control-signal handlers to `BatchJobWF` or document batch as non-cancellable. Build `/errors` from existing `page_failed` and `page_blocked` events, and implement the live tail in the edge handler by polling control with `after_seq`, with a per-org cap on open streams. Every route needs an org check from verified claims; the Model may propose cancel or pause as registry actions but Quarry executes. Crawl and batch are refused under Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) today, so no ZDR content handling is needed now; any future ZDR crawl must be status-only. Surface the fixed 30-minute `WorkflowExecutionTimeout` (dispatcher.go:254) as a typed terminal state. Result pagination needs a data-source decision first: crawl output is not stored per job today (events carry excerpts; pages go to artifacts or the Data Plane when ingest is on). Cancel must be idempotent against finished workflows and must not leave a half-written ingest. Shares the signal path with [F-t2-018](#F-t2-018).

<a id="F-t2-018"></a>
### F-t2-018 · Make scrape, crawl and batch schedules actually execute (Temporal schedules)
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Ingestion Plane (quarry-control, quarry-orchestrator, quarry-edge); Frontend gateway trims its UI | W2 | Medium |

**What to build.** Persist the full schedule definition (name, config such as url/urls, max_pages and schema, overlap policy, timezone), map the crawl, scrape and batch target kinds to the already-registered Temporal workflows, and make trigger and backfill work. Write skipped and failed reasons as job events and send failure notices through the Application Plane. Until this ships, stop the gateway UI offering overlap, trigger and backfill controls the backend cannot honour.

**Why it matters.** Scheduled re-crawls keep tracked sources fresh, and the UI already lets users create such schedules. Today only `change_monitor` schedules run, so a user-created scrape, crawl or batch schedule either fails at creation or never fires, which erodes trust and leaves knowledge stale.

**What the references do.** Scheduling is in 2 of 4 references (Firecrawl, Apify) and is table-stakes for crawl platforms. Firecrawl's scheduler tick claims due monitors through a database RPC with a worker id, limit and 60 s lease, defers each fire by a deterministic jitter of `sha256(id) mod min(interval/2, 5 min)`, records `skipped_overlap` when the previous check is still active, marks checks stale after 60 min (10 min for search-only) and releases them, and supports IANA timezones and a plain-English phrase parser with a 5-minute minimum [SRC]. The credit check happens at run time in the runner through a credit lock, recorded as `skipped_no_credits`, not before dispatch [SRC]. Note that this scheduler drives change-tracking monitors, not generic scheduled scrape or crawl jobs, and the claim SQL is not in the open repo, so `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` is a Verevon design choice rather than an observed mechanism [SRC; INF]. Apify schedules are timezone-aware cron with per-run input overrides, a 10 s minimum and a default email when a scheduled run fails to start through misconfiguration, not on every failure [DOC]. Timezone-aware cron exists in both references [SRC; DOC]. Firecrawl monitoring is recent and partly behind a billing rollout, and requires the DB-backed deployment [SRC].

**Verevon today.** `workflowFor` maps only `change_monitor` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-control/internal/resources/schedule_wire.go:39-51); the orchestrator skips workflowless schedules (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-orchestrator/internal/schedules/schedules.go:158-163); `MountScheduleAliases` receives a nil Temporal client so trigger and backfill return 501 (quarry-control/cmd/control/main.go:177-192). `CrawlJobWF`, `BatchJobWF` and `ScrapeJobWF` are registered (quarry-orchestrator/cmd/orchestrator/main.go:94-116) and `Manager.Trigger`/`Backfill` exist unused (schedules.go:365-398). The gap is larger than "skipped": the edge's non-preset create path forwards keys (kind, config, overlap_policy, name) that do not exist on `store.Schedule` (quarry-control/internal/store/store.go:333-355), validation requires `org_id` and `target_ref` (store/schedule_validate.go:39-55) while the create handler never reads the org parameter (resources/resources.go:113-130), so creation most likely fails with 400 [INF from reading the code, not executed]. Reuse the `ChangeMonitorWF` replay-safe `run_id` derivation (workflows.go:296-320), the orchestrator reconcile loop as the single writer of Temporal schedules, native Temporal `ScheduleSpec` overlap, jitter and `TimeZone`, and the preset create path in schedule_routes.rs that stamps org and `created_by` from the claim.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Persistence first: extend `store.Schedule` and the control schema, map kind to `target_kind` and config to `target_ref`/args in the edge, and require the org from the signed query in the control create handler. Then extend `workflowFor` for the three workflows, derive `run_id` from the workflow execution as `ChangeMonitorWF` does, and set overlap policy and `TimeZone` in schedules.go create/update. For trigger and backfill, prefer reaching the orchestrator's existing `Manager.Trigger`/`Backfill` through a pull-model request row or an internal orchestrator endpoint over a second Temporal client in quarry-control; record the choice in an ADR (architecture decision record) because the control-side interface was designed for an SDK client. The Ingestion Plane owns schedules end to end; failure notices go through Application Plane notification-core by API, and entitlement is re-checked at fire time against the Control Plane with no cross-plane DB reads ([F-t2-008](#F-t2-008)). Durable jobs are refused under ZDR (quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:563-588), so refuse schedule creation for ZDR orgs and re-check posture at fire time. Existing rows must not all start firing after the fix: require an explicit enable step. Sequence after crawl scoping so recurring crawls do not repeat off-site walks.

<a id="F-t2-019"></a>
### F-t2-019 · Structured extraction that works in production: json, summary, question and highlights formats over a real Model Plane structured-output grant
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | M | Ingestion Plane (formats) + Model Plane (invoke) + Control Plane (grant decision) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Add `json` (schema plus prompt), `summary`, `question` and `highlights` as scrape and crawl formats, executed by sending the schema and the ZDR flag to the Model Plane's existing `/v1/invoke` over an org-bound, metered service token. Add schema normalisation and one repair pass. Resolve the Control Plane grant that currently denies every Quarry-to-Model-Plane call, and make the gateway forward the schema as well as the prompt.

**Why it matters.** Schema-to-JSON extraction is the most used model feature of scrape APIs and the basis for monitors, research jobs and extraction profiles. The Model Plane already centralises ZDR-aware routing and cost, so Quarry should call it rather than hold model keys. Today extraction works only under a dev bypass [SRC].

**What the references do.** A built-in `json` scrape format and `question`/`highlights` formats are in 1 of 4 references (Firecrawl); a per-page summary is in at least 2 of 4 (Firecrawl, and Apify's Website Content Crawler offers an optional per-page AI summary) [SRC; DOC]. Firecrawl's `json` path normalises the schema (recursing into `$defs` and `anyOf`, making every key required and setting `additionalProperties:false`; it does not resolve `$ref`), routes schemas with `$ref`/`$defs` to a larger model, trims the markdown to 80 percent of the model's input tokens, calls the provider with a schema-constrained response format, runs a text and LLM repair pass on parse failure, and falls back to a second model of the same provider only on quota or rate-limit errors [SRC]. `question` and `highlights` run on a Firecrawl-hosted fine-tuned model or Gemini with token-based page trimming and a "page too long" warning; the 10,000-character limit applies to the prompt text, not the page [SRC]. Each of `json`, `question` and `highlights` adds 4 credits per page, and `/extract` is deprecated in favour of the `json` scrape format [SRC]. Models are hard-coded to specific vendors, so Verevon must route through the Model Plane instead [SRC].

**Verevon today.** `AiFormatRunner` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/ai_formats.rs:60-300) implements summary, json and query as a library: json is reachable only through `POST /v1/extract` with a schema (quarry-edge/src/extract_routes.rs:195-300; sequential, markdown capped at 20k, prompt field marked `dead_code` at line 48), query only through `/v1/answer`, summary has no caller, and there is no highlights format. The schema is pasted into the prompt and checked after the call; `ModelPlaneInvokeRequest` (quarry-runtime/src/mp_client.rs:31-40) carries neither `structured_output_schema` nor `zdr`, and ai_formats.rs:302-310 discards the ZDR argument. The `model-invoke` and `model-stream` rows are `granted:false` (quarry-runtime/contracts/cross-plane-token-requests.json:41-65; quarry-edge/src/model_plane_gate.rs), and `StructuredExtractClient` targets a `/v1/structured/extract` route that exists nowhere in the Model Plane. What exists to reuse: model-gateway `/v1/invoke` already accepts `structured_output_schema` and `zdr` (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:6693, 8987) with an OpenAI `response_format` mapping in inference-core (provider/openai.rs:463-490); the `ExtractStructured` RPC (model-gateway/src/grpc.rs:2516-2655) already does Quarry scrape plus schema-bound inference with an 80k character cap, ZDR-aware; and the model-free structured harvest (JSON-LD, hydration data) is a first pass.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Do not design a new Model Plane endpoint. Extend `ModelPlaneInvokeRequest` to send `structured_output_schema` and `zdr` to `/v1/invoke`, add normalisation and one repair pass in Quarry or as an inference-core option, and run the structured harvest first so the model fills only missing fields. Treat the Control Plane registry grant (D16, QB-T17, DEF-QB-T17-PART2, owner and date unassigned) as the gating decision, not an engineering detail. As an interim path that needs no grant, extend `ExtractStructured` (multi-URL, repair, highlights, summary) as the Model-proposed route and keep Quarry formats for the user-facing API once the grant lands. Provider strictness differs: OpenAI gets non-strict `json_schema`, Anthropic gets the schema only through the prompt (provider/anthropic.rs:725-737), so validate after every call. Page text is untrusted input; require highlights to be verbatim substrings. Per-page model cost multiplies in crawls, so this depends on the budgets in [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008) and the formats contract in [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005); multi-URL fan-out is [F-t2-040](#F-t2-040).

<a id="F-t2-020"></a>
### F-t2-020 · Harden persistent browser profiles: complete snapshots, per-org envelope encryption at rest, ordered saves, ZDR enforced inside Quarry
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Ingestion Plane (quarry-browser, quarry-runtime, quarry-edge) + Control Plane (key API) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Two independent slices. Snapshot completeness: capture all cookies of the browser context with expiry and SameSite, local and session storage for every visited origin, and an IndexedDB export, and re-apply them on restore. Encryption and ordering: envelope-encrypt the snapshot row and the cached payload with a per-org data key obtained through a Control Plane key API (environment master key first, KMS later), add a monotonic `saved_at` upsert and deletion tombstones, extend the purge consumer to profiles, and apply the ZDR and owner checks to the `/v1/profiles` routes.

**Why it matters.** Persistent profiles hold live session cookies for users' signed-in sites. Today snapshots miss most of the state and are stored as plaintext, so restores fail silently and a database read exposes credentials [SRC]. Encryption is specified in Quarry's own contract (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/docs/CONTRACTS.md:185-199) but not implemented [DOC].

**What the references do.** Some form of persistent browser state exists in 3 of 4 references (Firecrawl through Hangar, Codex, and Aside [INF]), with only a weak analogue in Apify, but no reference encrypts browser profiles per org: Firecrawl keeps only a profile listing while the profile contents live in the closed, hosted Hangar service, and no encryption code exists in its repo [SRC]; Codex keeps browser storage in persistent `persist:codex-browser-*` partitions with a storage id and a profile importer [SRC]; Aside is a full Chromium browser with profiles [INF]; Apify's SessionPool pins cookies to a proxy session, which is only a weak analogue of a named profile [DOC]. The reusable mechanism is Firecrawl's save ordering: a save is ignored unless its `savedAt` is newer than a deletion tombstone, `saved_at` is set to the greater of the stored and new values, and delete removes rows saved at or before the tombstone; the tombstone is a key-value entry keyed by team hash and name, not a column [SRC]. Firecrawl rejects profiles under lockdown and ZDR and answers 409 while a session is still saving [SRC]. Firecrawl seeds caller cookies through Playwright `context.addCookies` with a "last two host labels" domain heuristic, which is wrong for `co.uk`-style suffixes; do not copy it [SRC]. The envelope-encryption pattern (random AES-256-GCM data key wrapped by a per-(user, tool) RSA key, ciphertext only in logs) is Apify's secret Actor input mechanism, not profile encryption, so it is borrowed as a security baseline, not parity [DOC]. The full snapshot specification is Verevon's own intent from CONTRACTS.md [DOC].

**Verevon today.** `capture_snapshot` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:1771-1832) calls `Page::get_cookies`, which in chromiumoxide 0.9.1 returns only cookies matching the tab's current URL, sets `expires` to `None`, reads storage for the current origin only and hard-codes `indexed_db` to empty. Snapshots are plaintext JSON through a last-write-wins upsert with no ordering (quarry-runtime/src/postgres_profile_store.rs:30-92), cached unencrypted in Redis (quarry-edge/src/main.rs:597-600), and `S3ProfileStore` is never constructed (quarry-runtime/src/lib.rs:166). The purge consumer handles page images only (quarry-runtime/src/page_image.rs:43-61). ZDR refusal is already enforced inside Quarry at run creation (quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:527-533, 1466-1476), but the `/v1/profiles` routes check org id only, with no ZDR or owner check, and `GET` returns raw cookie values (quarry-edge/src/profile_routes.rs:102-125, 268-296). Reuse the `ProfileStore` trait as the single serialization boundary, `zdr_forbids_persistent_profile` and `Claims::zdr_floor`, the restore probe and `ProfileScope`, and the migrations directory.

**How to build it in Verevon.** In quarry-browser, use `Storage.getCookies` or `Network.getAllCookies` for the whole context, iterate visited origins for DOMStorage, add IndexedDB through the CDP (Chrome DevTools Protocol) domain, and restore emulation overrides with the hooks from [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007). In quarry-runtime, add the encryption layer below the Redis cache so the cache holds ciphertext, with envelope `{enc_v, key_id, nonce, aad = org|profile|snapshot, ciphertext}`, lazy re-encryption on next save plus a one-off backfill. Keys come from a Control Plane key API; Quarry never reads Control Plane tables, and erasure is crypto-shredding driven by a Control Plane erasure event. Skip the S3 store in the first slice since production already requires Postgres. The store has no owner column, so owner and Space isolation stay enforced above it and a Space must never widen a profile's owner ACL; do not widen the routes' audience before an owner check exists. Decide whether `GET /v1/profiles/:id` should ever return raw cookie values. The Model Plane must never see cookie values. Main risks: master-key loss destroys all profiles; nonce handling in a new Rust AES-GCM implementation needs review; stale restores can look like hijacks to the origin. Profile pinning for scrape is [F-t2-058](#F-t2-058).

<a id="F-t2-021"></a>
### F-t2-021 · Secret redaction at capture time: AX/DOM snapshots, screenshots, OCR, receipts and logs, before storage or model exposure
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Ingestion Plane (quarry-browser, quarry-runtime) + Model Plane (execution-core planner and persistence) | W2 | Medium |

**What to build.** A deterministic, versioned redaction module inside Quarry's snapshot builder that masks secrets before artifact write, event emission and the response to the Model Plane. Snapshots always mask password, tel, one-time-code and card fields by input type, autocomplete token, label pattern and Luhn check; durable captures also redact by value shape (JWTs, API-key prefixes, private-key blocks, numeric OTPs). Redact typed text in step receipts and the planner prompt. Screenshot masking by element rectangle is a follow-on slice.

**Why it matters.** Verevon records more than any reference (receipts, timeline, proof bundles, live frames), so it captures more secrets. The AX (accessibility) projection copies node values verbatim and receipts store typed text, so a password typed by the agent lands in storage and in the model prompt [SRC]. Redaction is a precondition for the credential handoff in [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024).

**What the references do.** Capture-time page redaction is in 1 of 4 references (Aside); some redaction is in 3 of 4 (Aside capture and logs, Firecrawl PII on output, Apify secret env vars in logs). Aside's capture redaction replaces values with `[redacted:capture]` using layered rules: sensitive input types (`password`, `tel`), sensitive autocomplete tokens, a field-label regex (cvv, otp, pin, ssn, secret), value patterns (JWT, AWS access key, API key, Slack token, private-key block, labelled secret, numeric OTP) and a card-number candidate regex with a Luhn check, applied to text and ARIA snapshots for durable capture, plus redaction of tool arguments and error bodies in logs [SRC, from a scratch copy of the daemon blob, no runtime confirmation]. The interactive agent snapshot path redacts only non-empty password inputs, so other secrets on a page still reach the model [SRC]. No reference redacts screenshots, OCR (optical character recognition) output or receipts [SRC]. Aside's origin-checked password fill sets the value through the native `HTMLInputElement` setter with input and change events, and its only caller unlocks an external password-manager extension page, not a website login; website fills go through the Vault extension with session, policy and URL checks [SRC]. Firecrawl offers opt-in `redactPII` on markdown through an external privacy service that fails closed to an empty string [SRC]; Apify encrypts secret env vars at rest and redacts them from run logs [DOC].

**Verevon today.** `native_projection_from_cdp` copies AX name and value verbatim (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:1096-1130); snapshot target text falls back to the AX value (quarry-runtime/src/observation.rs:1573-1640); `StepReceipt` stores the whole `AgentAction`, including `Type` text, plus the post-action observation (quarry-runtime/src/step_receipts.rs:190-206); OCR and screenshot artifacts are written unmasked behind only a ZDR gate (observation.rs:700-790). `redact_sensitive` (quarry-runtime/src/agent_memory.rs:52) has no callers at all because `AgentScratchpad` is itself unused. Only egress URLs are redacted (chromiumoxide.rs:1053-1066). The Model Plane scrubs token patterns from step output and checkpoints before persistence (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/scrub.rs:1-60; grpc.rs:1015-1042), but the `LlmPlanner` prompt receives raw observation text (execution-core/src/llm_planner.rs:170-263). Reuse scrub.rs as the richer pattern set (Bearer, JWT, `sk-`, AWS, GitHub, PEM, DSN, key=value), `redact_sensitive` as a seed, and `put_artifact_if_allowed` plus `build_active_snapshot` as the choke points.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Port the scrub.rs pattern set into a Quarry module and apply it in `native_projection_from_cdp`, `build_active_snapshot`, receipt persistence (mask `AgentAction::Type` text and the stored observation) and before artifact writes; scrub `extracted_text` in the planner prompt before inference. Field-semantic masking needs input type and autocomplete, which an AX node does not carry, so the snapshot builder must fetch them per input target (DOM describe or page evaluate); whether Chromium already masks password values in its AX tree is not verified. Rules must be deterministic and local with no model call, and must run on the ZDR path too, because live observations are returned even when nothing is persisted. Quarry and the Model Plane are separate workspaces, so share patterns by duplication with a conformance test, or record a shared crate in an ADR. Main risks: over-redaction breaks extraction, so keep rules narrow and typed; regex cost on large snapshots; screenshot masking depends on accurate rectangles.

<a id="F-t2-022"></a>
### F-t2-022 · Org-pinned, tighten-only web policy: tri-state capability gates delivered as signed claims and enforced by Quarry
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Control Plane (policy of record) + Model Plane (grants) + Ingestion Plane (enforcement) | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Make the Control Plane the policy of record for web and browser capabilities (robots mode, maximum proxy tier, credential and cookie stripping, login or form-write actions in autonomous runs, raw evaluate, uploads and downloads, recording, persistent profiles, paid providers, domain allow and deny lists, ZDR) as `disabled`, `allowed` or `forced`. Deliver it as a signed claim to user-token callers and as action-class fields in BrowserBroker grants for Model-Plane-driven runs. Quarry resolves one `ResolvedPolicy`, rejects at the edge with typed errors and re-resolves per URL in `PageRunner` and the step path as a backstop. Requests, Spaces and the Model can only tighten.

**Why it matters.** This implements three Verevon rules directly: Quarry executes or rejects, ZDR must propagate, and Space access never widens an owner-plane ACL. Today no org or request web policy is applied at all; only ZDR is org-pinned [SRC].

**What the references do.** A Safe Mode is in 1 of 4 references (Firecrawl); tri-state team flags plus default-off browser gates are in 2 of 4 (Firecrawl, Codex). Firecrawl computes `ResolvedPolicy = merge(orgPolicy, defaults)` in the API layer, rejects requests that ask for forbidden options with `403 SAFE_MODE_BLOCKED`, and recomputes it in the worker for every URL including crawl children, where it silently strips `Authorization`, `Cookie` and `Proxy-Authorization` headers, write and press actions, profiles and URL userinfo [SRC]. Team flags hold `disabled|allowed|forced` per capability (ZDR, ignoreRobots, threatProtection, ipWhitelist); effective option = forced ? on : (allowed ? request : typed rejection). API keys additionally carry allowed endpoints and formats, cached for about 60 s [SRC]. It is not strictly tighten-only: an admin can set `allowBypassSafeMode` so a request may send `safeMode:false`, and allow-listed domains switch off robots, stealth and authentication controls [SRC]. Audit coverage is partial: rejection events are emitted only from the scrape, crawl, batch, extract and agent controllers and only when the team's SIEM flag is on; worker downgrades, map and search emit nothing [SRC]. `maxBrowserSessions` is declared but no enforcement was found [SRC]. Codex's browser gates (browserUse, full CDP access, WebMCP, record-and-replay, auto-auth, multi-window) are vendor rollout flags with client defaults of false and a Dev-only env override, not org-admin policy; the exception is the enterprise requirement `browser_use_full_cdp_access` [SRC]. Signed claims are Verevon's own design; Firecrawl reads flags from a cached account record [SRC; INF].

**Verevon today.** org-core's `RetentionPosture` (apps/Control Plane/org-core/internal/org/types.go:255-315) feeds an auth-core claim that `Claims::zdr_floor` raises over request flags (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/auth.rs:56-140), but only for user tokens. The edge hard-wires `RunPolicy::default()` (quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:461; main.rs:1485-1493), two unrelated `RunPolicy` types exist (quarry-core/src/policy.rs:1-17; quarry-runtime/src/policy.rs:55-80), control's presets (`fast` = RobotsIgnore) are never consumed (quarry-control/internal/resources/presets.go:45-80), and `/v1/blocklists` entries have no org column and are not read by the edge. The Model Plane gate is one coarse capability, `cap.browser.open` (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/capability_policy.rs:793-794), plus capability-core org-scope grants (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/policy/engine.go:158-215) and a sensitive-action allowlist in the broker (browser-broker/internal/grant/grant.go:30-36). Reuse the retention-posture delivery pattern, `effective_zdr`/`zdr_floor` merge semantics, broker `allowed_domains` and `BrowserEgressPolicy` deny-all defaults.

**How to build it in Verevon.** Run the ADR first: org-core claims versus capability-core grants, because capability-core already holds tenant-bound org-scope grants and is a competing policy of record. The claim channel reaches only user-token callers: execution-core and model-gateway call Quarry with service tokens whose ZDR claim is ignored by design, so for Model-Plane-driven runs the resolved policy must ride in the BrowserBroker grant or the start-run body. Ship a Quarry-only thin slice before any org-core storage or admin UI: one `ResolvedPolicy` replacing the `RunPolicy::default()` sites, tighten-only merge with the existing ZDR floor, typed denials for evaluate, upload and download (ties to [F-t2-002](#F-t2-002)), and reconcile or delete the second `RunPolicy` type, the unused presets and `/v1/blocklists` instead of adding a third shape. The remaining work is org-core storage, auth-core claim minting and broker action classes. Depends on robots enforcement [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016), the proxy-tier cap [F-t2-058](#F-t2-058) and production grants [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001); per-origin consent builds on top in [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032). Risks: policy resolved in two places can drift, so test both paths; claim staleness mid-run, mitigated by the short grant TTL and per-action revalidation; strict defaults can break login-based research, so keep escalation through approvals and never block the human user's own manual browsing.

<a id="F-t2-023"></a>
### F-t2-023 · Scrape-time page actions plus screenshot and PDF formats, under one bounded, grant-gated action policy
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Partial | L | Ingestion Plane (quarry-core, quarry-runtime, quarry-browser, quarry-edge); Frontend renders outputs | W2 | High |

**What to build.** Two deliverables. Formats: make `screenshot{fullPage, clip, quality, viewport}` and `pdf` first-class scrape formats captured on the scrape session, stored as artifacts under the claim's org, inline-only under ZDR, with tile-and-stitch only past the single-capture height limit. Actions: carry a validated, capped `Vec<Action>` on the scrape request and run it on the same page before extraction, returning per-action outputs in order, with Firecrawl-style caps (about 50 actions, 60 s total wait). Evaluate inside actions follows the [F-t2-002](#F-t2-002) policy.

**Why it matters.** Declarative actions and screenshot or PDF output are table-stakes for scrape APIs, and the runtime pieces already exist in Quarry on the agent path, but scrape cannot reach them: the screenshot and pdf formats are always `None` [SRC]. Agents and users need a page after consent dismissal, a scroll or a click, and a visual artifact for review.

**What the references do.** Scrape-time actions are in 2 of 4 references (Firecrawl, Apify); screenshot capture is in 3 of 4 (Firecrawl, Codex, Aside), and Apify likely offers it too [INF, not re-checked]. Firecrawl's request schema allows nine action types (wait, click, write, press, scroll, screenshot, scrape, executeJavascript, pdf) with `MAX_ACTIONS=50`, a 60 s total wait, screenshot quality 1-100 and a 7680x4320 viewport cap; outputs are grouped into per-type arrays (`screenshots`, `scrapes`, `javascriptReturns`, `pdfs`), not one ordered list, so an ordered per-action result is a Verevon improvement [SRC; INF]. Execution happens only in the closed hosted fire-engine; the open-source Playwright engine declares `actions:false`, so self-hosted Firecrawl cannot run actions [SRC]. The scrape `executeJavascript` action has no script-size cap; the 100,000-byte and 1-300 s limits belong to the separate hosted-browser `/execute` endpoint [SRC]. Codex captures JPEG at quality 80 with `captureBeyondViewport` plus clip and uses a screencast timestamp to guarantee a fresh frame for hidden pages [SRC]. Aside always scroll-stitches: it plans vertical tiles with CSS-pixel overlap, scrolls to each, waits 150 ms plus two animation frames, captures PNG with `captureBeyondViewport:false` and composes the tiles; element shots use the box model as clip and default to webp; PDF goes through `Page.printToPDF` [SRC, scratch copy of the daemon blob]. Apify's page actions are DOC-only, declarative per-Actor options rather than a general action DSL [DOC]. Firecrawl's OpenAPI spec lists eight types without pdf, so the spec has drifted [SRC].

**Verevon today.** `ScrapeRequest` has only `render.waitFor*` hints (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:346-373); the pipeline hard-codes `screenshot: None` and `pdf: None` (quarry-runtime/src/pipeline.rs:702-703); `signals.actions`, `screenshot` and `pdf` are planning hints only (quarry-runtime/src/driver_plan.rs:13-20). Screenshot via `capture_beyond_viewport` and PDF via `PrintToPdfParams::default()` exist on the agent path (quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:2392-2445). `ActionRuntime` (quarry-runtime/src/action_runtime.rs:62-110) has no production caller and acquires its own session instead of the scrape session used by `BrowserDriverAdapter::do_fetch` (quarry-runtime/src/browser_driver.rs:128-240). docs/OSS_PARITY_BACKLOG.md:21-23 marks both as parity, which is overstated. Reuse `do_fetch`, the `Action` enum and `ActionScript` with `on_error` (quarry-browser/src/actions.rs:1-80; it includes Navigate and Evaluate, so both need explicit policy), `driver.screenshot`/`driver.pdf`, `put_artifact_if_allowed` (observation.rs:525-545), the unpopulated `OutputFormats.screenshot/pdf` fields (quarry-core/src/output.rs:140-147) and the tenant-bound artifact read (quarry-edge/src/resource_routes.rs:157).

**How to build it in Verevon.** Formats (M): capture inside `do_fetch` after goto and wait_for, return the bytes through the fetch response so the pipeline fills `OutputFormats.screenshot/pdf` as artifacts, with ZDR no-store, cache bypass and the clip, quality, viewport and fullPage options. The vision sidecar's tiles (quarry-runtime/src/vision.rs) are OCR preprocessing, not capture, so do not reuse them. Actions (M): carry the capped list on `RenderHints`/`FetchHints`, extend `ActionRuntime` to take an existing session, and run it in `do_fetch` on the same page so the scrape's host-pinned egress policy and cookie context are kept; a second `ActionRuntime` page would lose both. The Model proposes scrape actions and Quarry validates, caps and executes them; selectors and scripts from model output are untrusted. Evaluate stays denied until the [F-t2-002](#F-t2-002) gate exists. Cache admission must exclude requests with actions, headers or screenshot options ([F-t2-003](#F-t2-003)); the format registry is [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005). Frontend: render the artifacts in the timeline through the owner-gated gateway route and correct the stale tenant-scoping comment in browser.rs. Risks: memory on very tall pages, stitched seams on sticky or lazy content, and screenshots of authenticated pages, which must stay behind the owner gate and never persist under ZDR.

<a id="F-t2-024"></a>
### F-t2-024 · Credential handoff where the model never sees secrets: executor-side fill_secret and email OTP via integration-corev2
| Tier | Verevon today | Effort | Plane | Wave | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Priority | Absent | L | Ingestion Plane (Quarry executor + integration-corev2) + Model Plane (approval) | W3 | Medium |

**What to build.** OTP (one-time password) first: a `fill_otp` sensitive action beside `upload_ref` and `download_ref`, issued as a single-use child of the run grant bound to one origin and frame after user approval, plus an executor-only integration-corev2 (IC) `mail.otp.read` action that returns only the code. Quarry re-checks origin and field type, fills the value in the page, masks the field in observations and stores only a boolean in receipts. Password and vault-secret references (`secret://` handles) follow later, behind a secret-custody ADR.

**Why it matters.** Aside and the hosted Codex Cloud Browser (not the shipped desktop in-app browser) keep secrets out of the model. Verevon's only fallback today is human takeover into a scoped profile, which works for attended runs but blocks unattended agents and routines. Keeping the model blind to the value is what makes autonomous sign-in acceptable under prompt-injection risk.

**What the references do.** Secret handoff is in 2 of 4 references (Aside, Codex) and is a differentiator; Codex's variant is cloud-only. Codex's `browserAuth` flow lets the model request a handoff on a tab; the runtime proceeds only when the origin is https, the frame origin equals the top origin, exactly one required non-password input is present, there are no options or QR, the credential-observation gate is on and the conversation id is bound, otherwise it answers `unavailable` [SRC]. It then raises an elicitation of kind `browser_email_otp` that a guardian cannot auto-approve, stating the model cannot see the code and the user may type it instead; on approval the value is delivered natively and the model receives only a status, and AX, DOM and screenshot reads of that document are denied until navigation [SRC]. That the host fetches the code from a mail connector is inferred from the elicitation text, not observed [INF]. This flow is wired only to the hosted Codex Cloud Browser through a broker socket; the shipped desktop in-app browser does not advertise the capability, and the socket env is forwarded only in Dev builds [SRC]. Aside fills passwords from opaque password-manager references without a UI form, returning generated-password plaintext only to its trusted agent-manager extension, never to the model [SRC]. Firecrawl and Apify have no equivalent in this record set.

**Verevon today.** `AgentAction` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-core/src/contracts.rs:636-700) has only plaintext `Type`, `TypeRef`, `FrameTypeRef` and `TypeSemantic`; typed text is persisted verbatim for non-ZDR runs (quarry-runtime/src/step_receipts.rs:185-200; quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:2346-2362). No secret store with opaque references is reachable from Quarry: IC's vault is AES-GCM at-rest encryption for provider tokens (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/crypto/vault.go:1-40), `/internal/connectors/token` leases raw provider tokens to an allow-list that excludes quarry (internal/api/server.go:1315-1345; internal/config/config.go:335-350), IC has mail read operations but no OTP action (internal/actions/mail_read.go:100-175), and BrowserBroker sensitive approvals cover only `respond_dialog`, `upload_ref` and `download_ref` (apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/internal/grant/grant.go:20-130). Reuse Quarry's sensitive-approval machinery (agent_routes.rs:196-312), `CreateScopedSensitiveApproval` (grant.go:96-130), the `RiskCategory::Login` HITL (human-in-the-loop) gate (execution-core/src/browser_agent/browser_risk.rs:25-45), the orchestration approvals surface (gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:1190-1210) and IC's Gmail and Microsoft mail read capabilities (internal/providers/catalog.go:254, 431). The fill_secret plan exists only in a document (QUARRY_V2_BROWSER_AUTOMATION_IMPROVEMENTS_2026_OWNERSHIP_RECONCILED.md:1106-1127) [DOC].

**How to build it in Verevon.** Add the `fill_otp` variant to the existing sensitive-approval path and reuse the Login risk gate and orchestration approvals for the approval card, naming origin and connection and offering "I will type it myself", instead of a new elicitation channel. Add the IC `mail.otp.read` action with sender, age and pattern filters, audited through the existing outbox, kept off every Model-callable tool surface; give Quarry a distinct identity or manifest row toward IC, since IC internal auth is one shared API key (server.go:170-173), and do not add quarry to `INTEGRATION_TOKEN_LEASE_CONSUMERS`. The Ingestion Plane owns execution and secret resolution; the Model only sees opaque references and proposes, Quarry executes. Secrets are scoped to user or org and never widened by Space access. ZDR forbids persisting values or frames that show credentials, and non-ZDR receipts store typed text today, so [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021) redaction must land first; trusted input is [F-t2-015](#F-t2-015), takeover is [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013), and production grant issuance is [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001). Defer password references until the custody ADR and those items are in place; the full scope would be XL. Main risks: exfiltration through a phishing page or injected iframe, an OTP requested by a page the agent was lured to, and accidental logging; mitigate with https-only, top-origin equality, single-use approval that shows the origin, and receipts that carry a boolean only.

## 5. Recommended

Recommended items close the parity gaps that matter once the Priority tier has made the in-app browser and the Quarry acquisition API safe to run in production: capacity and observability, honest handling of blocked sites, page-to-chat context, consent and approval flows, monitors, webhooks and durable sessions. Each card states the status of Verevon at commit afb83928 (2026-10-10) as verified in code; effort and approach come from that verification pass, not from the original comparison notes. Cards appear in recommended build order within the tier.

<a id="F-t2-025"></a>
### F-t2-025 · Per-org capacity: concurrency caps with backlog and fair share, per-class rate limits and plan-aware quotas
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Ingestion Plane + Frontend gateway + Control Plane (entitlements) · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Give every org a concurrency cap with a bounded backlog and fair-share priority, class-aware rate limits on the gateway's browser, scrape, search and agent routes, and limits keyed to the org's plan. Today one tenant can take the whole browser lease pool and agent browser runs have no cap at all.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the best source, 1 of 4 references for the backlog mechanism: over-limit work waits in a per-tenant backlog with an expiry, the oldest entry is promoted when a job finishes, and 429 is returned only when the backlog is full [SRC]. Plan-scaled limits are 2 of 4 references: Firecrawl multiplies per-endpoint base limits by a plan factor with org overrides [SRC], and Apify documents plan concurrency limits but not the mechanism [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The gateway already limits every route with one 600 rpm class keyed by client IP (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/rate_limit.rs:50-58); Quarry has a process-global lease pool of 2 or 4 permits (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/main.rs:323,337) and the agent_runs map has no capacity check (agent_routes.rs:107,1639). Build four parts: org-keyed class limiters in the gateway using the mcp.rs pattern (S); per-org permits in RuntimeLeasePool plus a cap on agent runs, after threading org_id into the adapter acquire (M); backlog and fair share at the quarry-control jobs table, not the per-crawl postgres_queue (M); org-core quota keys with billing-core plan defaults read via a service credential (S-M).

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs a cross-plane grant for org-core and an ADR on fail-open versus fail-closed; backlog rows hold no URLs under ZDR (Zero-Data-Retention).

<a id="F-t2-026"></a>
### F-t2-026 · Per-operation browser deadlines, bounded tab attach and retry-once on a destroyed execution context
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: S · Plane: Ingestion Plane (quarry-browser) · Wave: W1 · Confidence: High*
