**What and why.** Give every Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP) call in quarry-browser an explicit deadline from one constants table, wrapped by a helper that returns a typed Timeout naming the operation, and retry idempotent read calls once when the execution context was destroyed. Hung steps are the first thing a user watching the live view notices, and they waste Model steps on transport errors.

**Reference practice.** Codex is the only reference with a per-operation deadline table, 1 of 4, though every reference bounds work somehow [SRC]. Codex wraps each CDP call with a constant deadline (about 20 s per command, 15 s page-ready, 5 s capture), rejects a tab attach after a 15 s handshake with distinct causes, and retries once on a context-destroyed error after dropping its cached isolated world, never retrying semantic errors [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Explicit deadlines exist only for downloads and the 5 s profile capture (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:76-77,683-693); the BrowserConfig builder sets no timeout (226-262), so other calls ride the library default of 30 s and fail as non-retryable DriverFailed strings (1988-2023). Set request_timeout and launch_timeout explicitly, add one cdp_call(op, fut) wrapper returning ErrorCode::Timeout with {op, ms}, wrap goto, and retry once only for content, AX snapshot and evaluate. Never retry ref or coordinate actions. Calibrate values from BrowserTelemetry, not Codex's numbers.

**Dependencies and risks.** Drop the Model Plane planner rule: execution-core already retries non-risky transient failures and deliberately excludes browser.act (permission/mod.rs:261-271). Per-run overrides may only tighten a Quarry-side maximum.

<a id="F-t2-027"></a>
### F-t2-027 · Honest bot-block handling: closed block taxonomy, non-signals, one recovery, a user-visible blocked state, and no stealth code
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane + Model Plane (planner rule) + Frontend Plane (UI state) · Wave: W1 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Separate strong bot-block signals (captcha widget, WAF interstitial, repeated challenge) from non-signals (bare 403 or 429, sign-in, consent, paywall, region block), enforce at most one low-risk recovery in Quarry, show a "site blocked automated access" state with take-over and try-another-source actions, and remove dead stealth code. Users get one truthful outcome and enterprise buyers a defensible no-evasion posture.

**Reference practice.** Codex is the best source, 2 of 4 references with Apify, whose academy supplies only a taxonomy because it recommends evasion [SRC; Apify DOC]. Codex classifies site-served evidence into {kind, confidence, evidence}, states that bare 403/429, timeouts, sign-in pages and paywalls are not bot detection, allows one short wait and one reload with no fingerprint or network change, then stops and tells the user, recording only the hostname [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The classifier at apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/observation.rs:154-214 substring-matches text and emits every hit at 0.98 confidence with requires_escalation=true; execution-core's BrowserObservation (browser_agent.rs:171-186) and both planner prompts never see the signal; the gateway labels any 403/429 "site_blocked" (knowledge/quarry.rs:283-304). stealth.rs is dead, but TLS-impersonation escalation, driver and proxy rotation on block statuses and a "stealth" preset are live. Order: add the signal to BrowserObservation and planner text; tiered classifier; single recovery enforced in Quarry; rewrite classify_scrape_failure; decide the live evasion-adjacent paths while deleting stealth.rs; build the blocked state on the takeover chip (BrowserChrome.tsx:1228-1236).

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs the anti-bot posture ADR; hostname aggregates stay in memory for ZDR orgs.

<a id="F-t2-028"></a>
### F-t2-028 · Page context to chat: Quarry hit-test, @tab mentions, selection and region attachments, frame context menu
*Verevon today: Absent · Effort: L · Plane: Frontend Plane with an Ingestion Plane endpoint and a Model Plane provenance path · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Let a user point at a tab, region or element in the in-app browser and ask about it: an @tab mention token, region crops, a Quarry hit-test endpoint that turns a frame point into an accessibility (AX) ref descriptor, and a right-click menu over the frame. The descriptors map onto the AX refs browser.act already uses.

**Reference practice.** Codex and Aside both ship page-to-chat context, 2 of 4 references [SRC]. Codex uses a tab mention {browserId, tabId, title, url} re-validated against the live tab on use and reported unavailable on mismatch [SRC]. Aside has the richest payload: text or region selections with element summaries, a preview image and per-element selector, role, text and href, kept per tab and cleared on navigation [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** None of it exists: the global composer deliberately has no "@" trigger (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/DashboardComposer.tsx:3873-3880) and Quarry has no hit-test endpoint or geometry in its contracts. The substrate is there: AX nodes carry backendDOMNodeId (quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:1165-1180), the tabs list returns {tab_id, title, url, active}, and snapshots carry a generation used to reject stale refs. Slices: a mention token resolved in the gateway via the Quarry tabs list, fail-closed, injected as a labelled untrusted page-context block (S-M, no Quarry change); region attach by cropping the streamed frame; a read-only hit-test endpoint using CDP DOM.getNodeForLocation (M); then the context menu.

**Dependencies and risks.** TrustClass derives from tool names only (model-gateway moderation.rs:204-240), so BrowserScraped provenance for attachments needs a new classification path; mentions never grant; attachments stay ephemeral under ZDR. Depends on [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) and [F-t2-012](#F-t2-012).

<a id="F-t2-029"></a>
### F-t2-029 · Quarry /metrics recorder, per-run statistics and graceful drain, then a span vocabulary, memory-aware admission and alert rules
*Verevon today: Stub · Effort: L · Plane: Ingestion Plane + Control Plane observability stack · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Install the Prometheus recorder in quarry-edge, serve /metrics on an internal listener with about 20 low-cardinality series, persist a RunStatistics object per run, drain browser leases on shutdown, then add memory-aware admission, a span vocabulary and alert rules. Quarry has no metrics today, so there are no SLOs, capacity numbers or alerts for the layer users and agents depend on.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the best source for metrics and per-run statistics, 2 of 4 references with Apify: prom-client registries on every worker with semaphore active, waiting and hold series [SRC], while Apify persists Crawlee Statistics per run and offers threshold alerts [DOC]. Apify's AutoscaledPool samples CPU, cgroup memory and event-loop lag and refuses work above 0.8 load [DOC]. Codex spans carry only url.origin and snapshot size, with approval wait subtracted [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The metrics crates are declared but inert and no /metrics route exists (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/telemetry.rs:6-14); no Prometheus job or rule covers Quarry (apps/Control Plane/observability/prometheus.yml:1-47). Graceful SIGTERM shutdown exists at the HTTP layer (main.rs:84-100) but drains no leases. Order: recorder and internal /metrics first (S), fixing the stale PLAN.md and GAP.md claims in the same change; RunStatistics in the Go run record (M); lease drain (S); memory-aware admission with a multi-Chromium pool, since a viewport change relaunches the single shared browser (M-L); alerting, which is new infrastructure because no Alertmanager exists (M).

**Dependencies and risks.** Span emission ships together with the ZDR span sampler in [F-t2-004](#F-t2-004) (its Phase 2, after the Phase 1 log filter), and spans are never emitted for ZDR runs without that sampler; labels never carry URLs or org ids; /metrics must not share the public listener.

<a id="F-t2-030"></a>
### F-t2-030 · Monitor change quality: normalised-content fingerprint, text/JSON diffs, tags and an optional goal-aware judge
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Ingestion Plane; Model Plane judge blocked · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Compare monitors on a fingerprint of the readable main-content markdown instead of raw response bytes, return a paragraph diff, accept a tag so one URL can keep separate histories, and later add a goal-aware judge. Monitoring already ships in the UI, so ad and timestamp churn flagged as change is visible to users now.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the only reference with this design, 1 of 4 [SRC]. It stores the last snapshot per (org, url, tag), normalises before compare, derives new, same, changed or removed, produces a unified diff and an optional schema-based JSON field diff, and runs a judge at temperature 0 that returns {meaningful, confidence, reason, changes[]} and fails open [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The compare basis is a blake3 hash of raw bytes (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/pipeline.rs:524,696); the normalised text_fingerprint only lands in the event payload. Baselines save artifact_id None and the diff artifact is a one-line string (quarry-edge/src/change_routes.rs:555,573); diff_paragraphs has no non-test caller; the monitor create body has only url and preset (gateway monitoring.rs:275-283). Phase 1 (M): add a text fingerprint to NormalizedOutput and the run_page response, switch both monitor paths, re-baseline existing monitors, persist the previous markdown as an artifact, add a diff route and tag column, and filter notifications in quarry-control notifyOnChange (resources.go:286-300). Phase 2 is blocked: every Model Plane switch is off and the model-invoke cross-plane grant is granted:false.

**Dependencies and risks.** Previous content is stored for non-ZDR orgs only; the JSON diff depends on /v1/extract, off by default.

<a id="F-t2-031"></a>
### F-t2-031 · Tenant-scoped, SSRF-guarded outbound webhooks with per-job events, then completion-triggered follow-ups and generalised monitors
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Make quarry-control webhooks tenant-safe before any customer can register one: org_id on registrations and deliveries, org-filtered fan-out, write-only secrets, a client guarded against server-side request forgery (SSRF), org-scoped edge routes and an optional per-job webhook on crawl and batch. Webhooks are how customers react to crawl and monitor results.

**Reference practice.** Apify is the best source for the contract, 2 of 4 references with Firecrawl: a rich event vocabulary, payload and header templates and ad-hoc per-run webhooks [DOC]. Firecrawl refuses private targets, supports wildcard event filters and metadata echo and keeps delivery logs, but delivers once with no retry [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Delivery is already stronger than Firecrawl: timestamped HMAC signing, a 5s/30s/5m/1h ladder and a DLQ (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-control/internal/dispatcher/dispatcher.go:25-33,107-123), and run_completed is emitted with an idempotency key by every workflow (quarry-orchestrator/internal/workflows/workflows.go:124-148). But store.Webhook has no org_id and a json-tagged Secret (store/store.go:376-394), fanoutWebhooks lists webhooks across all orgs (resources/resources.go:612-650), the edge exposes no webhook routes, and the client is a plain http.Client (dispatcher.go:40-42). Phase 1: org scoping, a SafeClient built from a shared Go egress package copied out of integration-corev2 (its guard sits under internal/ of another module), edge CRUD and a tenant-facing delivery list. Phase 2 (follow-up actions, multi-target monitors) is a separate ADR-gated item.

**Dependencies and risks.** The guard and org filter ship now; public CRUD is demand-gated. Cross-plane reactions use NATS events or the owning plane's API, with idempotency keys and a chain-depth cap.

<a id="F-t2-032"></a>
### F-t2-032 · Per-origin agent consent and action-class grants: child grants, run modes, typed denials, org origin policy and a browser settings hub
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane + Ingestion Plane + Control Plane + Frontend Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add typed browser denials with a decision source, per-origin consent with turn, conversation and always scopes where any deny beats any approval, run modes and action classes on grants, a versioned org origin policy and a Settings > Browser hub. Verevon has the hard part (fail-closed risk gates, one-shot sensitive grants), but agents retry around generic refusals and users re-approve the same site repeatedly.

**Reference practice.** Codex is the best source: per-origin consent and typed denials are each 1 of 4 references [SRC]. Codex checks denies before approvals per origin and class, merges admin and user policy field by field with deny precedence, and wraps dispatch in one choke point returning {code, decisionSource, retryable, message} [SRC]. Action classes appear in Aside, where every Page method declares its class, 2 of 4 with Codex [SRC]; org-level origin policy also appears in Firecrawl, 2 of 4 [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The broker host allowlist is fail-closed at run start, every step and in the CDP Fetch guard, but the grant store is in memory (apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/cmd/main.go:63), child grants cover three sensitive actions only (grant.go:32-34), status_err wraps a 403 as BAD_REQUEST (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:1106-1122), and no origin policy or browser settings section exists. Split: typed DenialReason with decision_source modelled on CrawlDenialReason (S-M); per-origin consent via a new parent-bound origin-extension grant kind, since validate_run_grant demands run domains equal the parent at every step, with decisions persisted in Control Plane user settings (L); org policy and the hub, intersected by the broker at issue time (L).

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) and the web-policy-of-record ADR; no wildcard patterns until isCanonicalDomain and BrowserEgressPolicy change together; ZDR orgs limited to turn scope.

<a id="F-t2-033"></a>
### F-t2-033 · Approval loop UX: keyboard-first approval card with redirect, persisted site rules and attention notifications
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane (schema and rules) with Frontend Plane and Application Plane delivery · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Turn the browser approval gate into a loop: one shared keyboard-first card in chat and the browser panel, a "do this instead" text delivered to the planner as a redirect, persisted org-level site rules, and approval_requested notifications that pull a user back to a background run. Today the card is click-only, repeats the same question and cannot be answered outside the browser panel.

**Reference practice.** Approval prompts and attention notifications each appear in 2 of 4 references (Codex, Aside) [SRC]. Codex defines the contract: display-friendly params, risk level, persist session or always, origin and page URL, with free text that rejects as "user asked to do X instead" and denials deep-linked to the setting [SRC]. Aside reports surface focus with a heartbeat and skips notifying for visible, disabled or ephemeral sessions [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** BrowserApprovalCard is approve or reject only (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/BrowserChrome.tsx:322-395), chat has a separate ApprovalRequests deck, a denial aborts the run by design in gate_risky_action (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/browser_agent.rs:870-945), and notification-core authorizes only run_completed and run_failed (apps/Application Plane/notification-core/internal/http/handlers.go:199-212). Slices: one shared card with shortcuts via use-chat-shortcuts.ts and redirect text in the existing decision reason field (S-M); richer approval creation on pause_for_approval and a planner path that takes redirect text instead of aborting; org-level rules in capability-core policy, not BrowserBroker, whose grants are in-memory (M); an approval_requested type on the runwatch path and notification-core allowlist (M).

**Dependencies and risks.** Depends on [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) and [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032); sensitive categories stay single-use; Escape is already globally bound; notification bodies are title-only under ZDR.

<a id="F-t2-034"></a>
### F-t2-034 · Agent observation quality and budget: post-action settle, frame-aware AX snapshot with warnings, set-of-marks screenshots, capped projection and image budget
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane + Model Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** After each agent action, wait for commit, first paint and a short quiet window with a hard cap; add per-frame deadlines and a warnings list to the accessibility (AX) snapshot; prioritise truncation by viewport and interactivity; forward console and network summaries to the planner. UI sessions are capped at 12 steps, so every wasted observation is expensive.

**Reference practice.** Settle and frame-aware snapshots are table-stakes in both browser references, 2 of 4 (Codex, Aside) [SRC]. Codex awaits navigation commit and first paint (5 s caps) then AX and DOM mutation silence for 50-100 ms within 750 ms, and stitches per-frame AX trees under their IFRAME owner with per-frame timeouts inside a global deadline, collecting {frameId, error} warnings [SRC]. Aside samples mutation ratios over 350 ms windows, tracks same-site requests up to 1.2 s and queues page events into the next tool result [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Quarry already returns a bounded AX and frame projection (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:1086-1128) with console and network summaries, but observation.rs:576-640 captures state immediately after the action with no settle, the AX fetch is one root-frame call with no per-frame deadline (chromiumoxide.rs:2681-2722), and the only settle is scrape-only (quarry-runtime/src/browser_driver.rs:28-70). On the Model Plane, observation_from_wire drops the summaries (execution-core/src/quarry_agent.rs:405-474) and the planner is text-only. Build the agent-action settle with per-action budgets and same-site filtering, reported as settle_ms; add warnings[] with serde defaults; align the 50-target cap with the planner's 40; carry page events to the model.

**Dependencies and risks.** Defer set-of-marks and the image budget: no planner receives pixels, and ScreenshotAnnotated already means a changed-region overlay. Side bug: observation.rs:2140 slices a byte index and can panic on a multi-byte boundary.

<a id="F-t2-035"></a>
### F-t2-035 · Durable hosted browser sessions: idle and absolute TTLs, suspend/resume, view vs control tokens, optional recording, scrape-to-session replay
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Ingestion Plane; scope handling in the Frontend Plane gateway · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Extend Quarry's run durability with idle and absolute time-to-live (TTL) values and a reaper, a wider checkpoint state machine with suspend and resume, a working caller for resume_run_id, idempotent create, view-only scope on live-view routes, optional recording, and later a replay context on scrape results. Two minutes is short for real browsing and the roadmap's crash-restart rehydration test is still outstanding.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl's hosted sessions are the best source, 2 of 4 references with Apify for lifecycle and live view [SRC; Apify DOC]. A session row carries state, last activity, idle and absolute TTL and an idempotency key; a reaper transitions rows; suspend snapshots the profile and releases the browser; live view uses short-lived view and control URLs; recording is disabled under ZDR [SRC]. Replay from a scrape result is unique to Firecrawl, 1 of 4 [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The durable pieces exist: a Postgres AgentRunCheckpoint with status Active or Closed (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/step_receipts.rs:25-90), a resume_run_id path that refuses ZDR (quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:1308-1335), and Model Plane durable runs with pause and resume. Missing: RunEntry has no timestamps (agent_routes.rs:79-102), persistent_session.rs has no consumer, nothing sends resume_run_id (execution-core/src/quarry_agent.rs:182-192), and recording flags exist only for providers. Add created_at and last_activity with a reaper shared with [F-t2-006](#F-t2-006), widen checkpoint status, implement suspend as release plus persisted profile, wire resume into StartRunRequest with the rehydration test, and add view-only as a scope on the owner-checked routes rather than bearer URLs.

**Dependencies and risks.** No second run state machine beside session-core; cross-user sharing is an ACL decision for a separate ADR; resume needs a valid broker grant ([F-t2-001](#F-t2-001)).

<a id="F-t2-036"></a>
### F-t2-036 · Durable, tenant-scoped authority and one audit record for every permission and approval decision
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Model Plane (browser-broker, execution-core) + Control Plane audit-core + Ingestion Plane quarry-control · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Persist BrowserBroker grants with parent-child inheritance, scope quarry-control webhooks by org, and define one AuthzDecision audit event that approval decisions, grant validations and Quarry policy denials publish on the audit bus. This completes the evidence chain for "the Model proposes, Quarry executes or rejects" that compliance reviews ask for.

**Reference practice.** Least-privilege permissions appear in 3 of 4 references; a structured per-decision audit record is unique to Codex, 1 of 4 [SRC]. Codex emits one record per decision with decided_by, decision context, origin only and duration, recording only denials for per-navigation checks [SRC]. Apify issues scoped, expiring tokens inherited by runs and webhooks, and retries webhooks 11 times over about 32 hours with a dispatch-id header [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The grant store is an in-process map (apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/internal/grant/grant.go:62-64) with no production AcquireGrant caller; the Webhook table has no org_id (quarry-control/internal/store/store.go:376-383); the browser decided event leaves decided_by empty (execution-core/src/browser_events.rs:129-151) and session-core publishes no approval event (session-core/src/audit_publisher.rs:604-632). The durable approvals table already records decided_by and decision_reason, so the audit gap is publication and schema, not capture. Split: durable, issued grants merged into [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) on the existing parent_grant_id binding (L); webhook org scoping shared with [F-t2-031](#F-t2-031) (M); AuthzDecision as a new event name on the flat verevon.audit.v2 shape, first from session-core approvals, later from Quarry via a new publisher and subject grant (M). Evaluate and raw CSS are already refused by both facades, so that allowlist change is defense-in-depth and is tracked in [F-t2-002](#F-t2-002), not here.

**Dependencies and risks.** Audit-core rejects events whose subject tokens mismatch the payload; events carry origin only and ZDR tags; the webhook migration fails closed on unscoped rows.

<a id="F-t2-037"></a>
### F-t2-037 · Document parsing depth: PDF classification and Model Plane OCR routing, Office and e-book conversion, bounded parsing and a per-org parse cache
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Ingestion Plane + Model Plane (doc_intel) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Route PDF and Office bodies in the scrape and crawl pipeline to native text extraction, send only scanned pages to the Model Plane layout provider for optical character recognition (OCR), convert Office and EPUB files under byte and time budgets, and cache parses per org. Enterprise knowledge is mostly PDF; crawls that skip it leave the Data Plane incomplete, and unbounded parsing of untrusted binaries is an availability risk.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the best source: PDF classification is 1 of 4 references (Firecrawl), Office conversion 2 of 4 (Firecrawl, Apify). Firecrawl classifies pages as text or scanned, converts text pages natively and sends only OCR pages to a layout service; its document crate unzips under part and total budgets, keys a sha256 parse cache and limits parsers with a semaphore [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** pipeline.rs has no content-type router (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/pipeline.rs:444-545), so lopdf extraction runs only in the answer fetcher (quarry-runtime/src/answer.rs:835-839); docx.rs has no caller and reads unbounded (quarry-transform/src/docx.rs:15-45), although docs/GAP.md:85 claims both shipped. imports-core already ships budgeted PDF and Office parsers (apps/Ingestion Plane/imports-core/app/parsers.py:140-215). Sequence: shared router with native lopdf in spawn_blocking under budgets (S, with [F-t2-009](#F-t2-009)); an ADR between porting imports-core's budgets and calling its ephemeral extract endpoint; OCR via /v1/ai/documents/layout only after a manifest row, grant and ZDR exception exist; then an (org, sha256) cache skipped under Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR).

**Dependencies and risks.** Quarry holds no Model Plane call grant today and model_hop_allowed refuses every hop under ZDR; OCR cost needs per-org page caps.

<a id="F-t2-038"></a>
### F-t2-038 · Agent presence and run control UX: animated cursor and target highlight, real annotation markers, steer/queue follow-ups, needs-attention notifications
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Frontend Plane + Model Plane + Application Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Show users what the agent is about to do in the shared browser, let them mark elements for it, redirect a run at the next step boundary, and notify them when approval is needed. These are daily-use expectations once the step loop is live, and today the product advertises an annotation feature that is a placeholder.

**Reference practice.** Each mechanism is 1 of 4 references. Codex moves a host-drawn cursor with a sequence number before every pointer command and sends annotation payloads with role, name, selector, rect and nearby text [SRC]. Aside sends idempotent WebSocket commands with steer{interrupt, expectedTurnId}, queue and afterSeq resume [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** browser-session.ts:127-137 advertises 'annotate' and returns one hard-coded anchor rendered at BrowserChrome.tsx:1822-1857; the gateway advertises the same (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:2578). Quarry's action.started payload is {step, action} with no geometry (quarry-core/src/contracts.rs:360-460). Runs have pause, resume and stop only (execution-core/src/browser_agent.rs:972-1011); chat-turn queueing exists in model-gateway queued_input.rs but not in the browser loop; run_completed and run_failed notifications already flow to notification-core. Four slices: remove 'annotate' and the placeholder (S); add an optional rect to Quarry snapshots via the CDP box model and animate it, presentation-only (M-L); steer and queue consumed by execution-core at the existing pause boundary with session-core replay (M); a needs_approval notification type plus watch toggle (S-M).

**Dependencies and risks.** Steering never bypasses a pending approval; queued input stays refused for ZDR tokens; Quarry's SSE ?from_seq is a no-op, so replay is Model Plane work.

<a id="F-t2-039"></a>
### F-t2-039 · Governed downloads and uploads: quarantined, tenant-scoped artifacts, a downloads drawer and hand-off to the Data Plane
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane + Data Plane (ingest API) + Frontend Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Prove, gate and surface the download path that already exists in the Chromium driver: agent downloads behind a single-use sensitive grant, unsolicited downloads cancelled, files stored as org-scoped artifacts with a scan hook, listed in a drawer and handed to the Data Plane. Today any page that serves a file is a dead end for agents.

**Reference practice.** Codex is the only agent-aware gate (1 of 4 references): its will-download handler cancels unless the download is user-initiated or consumes a stored grant matched on URL chain, session and expiry, and pushes a status machine to the agent [SRC]. Plain download handling is 2 of 4 (Aside, Codex) [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** ChromiumoxideDriver creates a per-session quarantine directory and a frame-bound, 25 MiB-capped download_from_verified_target (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:433-456, 2211-2390); the runtime refuses under ZDR, checks magic bytes and stores an org-stamped artifact (quarry-runtime/src/observation.rs:475-512). All of it is gated off by downloads_to_artifacts=false (chromiumoxide.rs:1877). The artifact store is already org-scoped (quarry-runtime/src/artifact_store.rs:100-120), so that dependency is dropped. Order: grant issuance from [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) with allowed_actions=[download_ref]; tests and default-deny for unsolicited downloads; flip the flag; drawer and timeline events; Data Plane hand-off through the ingest client with a scan hook before widening admitted types. Uploads stay out of scope.

**Dependencies and risks.** Grant issuance is unbuilt (gateway browser.rs:1258-1262); admission refuses Office formats until a scanner exists; safety review before enabling.

<a id="F-t2-040"></a>
### F-t2-040 · Parallel batch scrape with shared options, idempotent creation and wait-or-resume
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Ingestion Plane (quarry-orchestrator, quarry-edge, quarry-control) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Fan out /v1/batch under a concurrency bound with shared scrape options, accept a client Idempotency-Key on job creation, and add a bounded wait that returns a resumable 408. Sequential batches are visibly slow for ingestion and agents, and retries from the gateway or Model Plane must not double-start crawls.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the best source. Batch with maxConcurrency and append is 2 of 4 references (Firecrawl; Codex declared only); wait-with-resume 2 of 4 (Apify, Firecrawl); a client idempotency key 1 of 4, and Firecrawl's guard is a negative example [SRC; Apify DOC]. Firecrawl keys heavy requests by (org, request hash) and returns 408 processing_continues with Retry-After so an identical retry re-attaches [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** /v1/batch forwards only urls, ingest, org and user (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:669-716) to a sequential BatchJobWF with no options or cancel handlers (quarry-orchestrator/internal/workflows/workflows.go:357-381). The handoff sends no Idempotency-Key (quarry-edge/src/handoff.rs:26-82), and control's unique index is on idempotency_key alone, so two orgs choosing the same key collide. No wait_ms or re-attach exists. Order: S quick win, accept and forward the key, namespace by org, index (org_id, idempotency_key), payload-hash 409; M, bounded-parallel BatchJobWF with [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005) options, appendToId, invalid-URL report and CrawlJobWF's pause and cancel handlers; M-L, per-job get and cancel routes ([F-t2-017](#F-t2-017)) then the 408 envelope.

**Dependencies and risks.** Durable jobs are refused under ZDR (routes.rs:563-583), so wait-or-resume is non-ZDR only; per-host politeness is already enforced in PageRunner.

<a id="F-t2-041"></a>
### F-t2-041 · Sitemap, robots and index-assisted crawl discovery, plus URL canonicalisation and redirect de-duplication
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Extend the existing sitemap and robots parsers into a discovery step that seeds live crawls (robots Sitemap lines, gzip, nested indexes) behind a sitemap include|only|skip option, and derive frontier identity from a canonical URL with a post-redirect check. Sitemap seeding makes crawls of docs and product sites complete and cheap; canonicalisation keeps duplicates out of the Data Plane.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the best source; sitemap discovery is 2 of 4 references (Firecrawl, Apify), canonicalisation 1 of 4 (Firecrawl). Firecrawl queues robots, scoped, root and registrable-domain sitemaps in parallel, filters through the scope filter, locks the canonical URL permutation before enqueue and the redirect target after fetch [SRC]. Apify probes common sitemap names [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** quarry-transform parses sitemaps and robots Sitemap lines (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-transform/src/robots.rs:80-81), and /v1/map reads /sitemap.xml plus five nested indexes but ignores robots lines and gzip (quarry-edge/src/map_routes.rs:132-250). Live crawl has no sitemap seeding (routes.rs:533-553) and dedupes on the exact link string (workflows.go:417). Do not add a unique_key column: the Postgres frontier already enforces (org_id, request_id) (quarry-runtime/src/postgres_queue.rs:243-260), so derive request_id from the canonical URL. Improve /v1/map first (S), then a discovery activity that enqueues into the durable frontier after the [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016) scope filter (M), then post-redirect reconciliation using final_url. Index seeding comes later.

**Dependencies and risks.** Discovered URLs are attacker-controlled and must pass the security preflight and robots filter; aggressive normalisation stays opt-in; never pass thousands of URLs through Temporal history.

<a id="F-t2-042"></a>
### F-t2-042 · Agent web toolset: content-hydrated search, next-step hints, citations, and Quarry jobs as approval-gated tools with cost estimates
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane + Ingestion Plane · Wave: W3 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Let one agent call return search hits with page content, add success-path hints from local signals, attach stable source ids to agent-path tool output, and expose crawl, map and batch as approval-gated tools with caps. Agents today can only search and fetch one URL, so the Model falls back to repeated single fetches.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl and Codex are the best sources. Search with content is 2 of 4 references (Firecrawl, Apify); hints and documentation gating 2 of 4 (Firecrawl, Codex); citations with per-URL permission 1 of 4 (Aside). Firecrawl scrapes the top N concurrently inside a time budget and emits rule-based hints that never include page text [SRC]. Apify confirms estimated cost before a paid run [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** /v1/search has no scrape options (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/search_routes.rs:50-120); execution-core web_search sends only query, limit and zdr (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/web_tools.rs:120-175); next_actions exists only on errors; no cap.web.* ids exist (execution-core/src/capability_policy.rs:775-835); the COST approval kind has no producer. The chat path already has citations, freshness guidance and untrusted-data labelling (model-gateway/src/tool_loop.rs:4304-4310, 4469). Split: (A) hydration via the /v1/answer fetch fan-out, hints on the retrieval_metadata.suggested_next_tools pattern, numbered source ids from the chat citation machinery (M); (B) crawl, map and batch as approval-gated tools with Quarry-enforced caps, cost-estimate approval once a cost source exists (L). Drop the docs-read gate; the MCP facade stays in [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064).

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005), [F-t2-040](#F-t2-040), [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008); hydration multiplies spend; org domain policy is a separate Control Plane item.

<a id="F-t2-043"></a>
### F-t2-043 · URL-aware planner guidance: site skills, integration-corev2 provider capability hints by domain, driver capability matrix
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Model Plane + Ingestion Plane (integration-corev2 catalog) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Give the planner routing guidance for web tools, let it see the driver capabilities Quarry already returns, inject operator site skills when the observed URL matches, and offer a governed provider action when the agent lands on a connected provider's site. Each change cuts rejected proposals, shrinks prompts and replaces brittle clicking with audited API calls.

**Reference practice.** Three mechanisms at 1 of 4 references each. Codex marks API members unsupported per backend and lets the agent list capabilities with one-line descriptions [REA, SRC]. Firecrawl ships per-endpoint skills, narrowest first, and matches provider tools to a URL host [SRC]. Aside's SKILL.md frontmatter carries autoInject.url, and matched site skills are injected bounded [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** web_search and web_fetch descriptions are one sentence with no web prompt snippet (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:4184-4192); AgentSkill has trigger_file_patterns but no URL pattern (apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/sessions.proto:876-893). Corrected premise: POST /v1/agent/runs already returns driver_capabilities and execution_tier (quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:1180-1260), but nothing consumes them. integration-corev2 operations are string switch arms with no domains or schemas (integration-corev2/internal/actions/service.go:727). Four slices: rewrite descriptions plus a conditional SNIPPET_* web snippet (S); forward driver_capabilities into what the Model sees (S); url_patterns on AgentSkill mirroring trigger_file_patterns, matched on browser.observe (M); a versioned integration-corev2 operation catalog with provider domains replacing execution-core's CATALOG (L).

**Dependencies and risks.** Hints inform only; writes keep the approval path. Cap hints to avoid tool-list bloat; site skills stay inside the 8000-character budget; URL matching stays in memory.

<a id="F-t2-044"></a>
### F-t2-044 · Hermetic browser and scrape evals in CI through the production tool contract, with injection and ZDR fixtures and per-release scores
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Ingestion Plane + Model Plane (eval harness) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Run the existing eval binaries in CI with the commit SHA, then make the real Model Plane to quarry-edge loop test hermetic and scored, with prompt-injection pages and ZDR no-write assertions. Parity and safety claims stay honest only if evals exercise the production contract; today no browser agent eval runs anywhere.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the only reference (1 of 4). It serves a fixture site in CI, runs end-to-end tests over a matrix of engines and backends, records driver exchanges for replay, and runs evals on a labelled PR and after deploy [SRC]. Codex reuses the same runtime archives in training and production [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** No workflow runs the lab/evals binaries (.github/workflows/quarry-v2-ci.yml:150-211), docs/SCOREBOARD.md:13-17 lists every suite as pending, and the browser benchmark is a mock-driver latency microbenchmark, not a task corpus (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/lab/evals/src/browser_benchmark.rs:1-12). TestSite has no consumer; canary.rs and experiments.rs are unwired scaffolding. execution-core has a real loop test with planner probe cases, ignored for needing a live stack (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/tests/browser_agent_e2e.rs:1-60), and ZDR rejection is covered by quarry-edge/tests/zdr_behavior.rs. Phase 0 (S): a CI job running quarry-eval, bakeoff and release_matrix with the SHA. Phase 1 (M): make browser_agent_e2e hermetic against TestSite with a stub grant validator and a deterministic or recorded planner, score success, steps and cost, add injection pages asserting on the Model Plane screening verdict, and ZDR no-write assertions. Drop the mirror-and-diff clause.

**Dependencies and risks.** Injection screening lives at the Model Plane render seam (execution-core/src/provenance.rs); Chromium jobs belong in a labelled or scheduled workflow given documented CI instability.

<a id="F-t2-045"></a>
### F-t2-045 · Memory pollution guard for runs that ingested web or browser content (verify first)
*Verevon today: Absent · Effort: M · Plane: Model Plane · Wave: W2 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Taint a run or thread window once a web, browser or MCP tool result enters context, and make save_memory and Dreaming extraction skip or quarantine it. Otherwise a prompt injection read from one page can become a persistent memory injected into every later session.

**Reference practice.** Codex is the only reference (1 of 4), with a no_memories_if_mcp_or_web_search setting and a polluted thread memory mode: a flag set when such a result is added, which the extraction job checks to skip or route candidates to review [SRC, medium confidence].

**Verevon today and approach.** No taint or memory_mode flag exists in any plane. execute_save_memory takes no taint (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/mod.rs:650-663) and save_memory is deliberately ungated (execution-core/src/permission/mod.rs:171-182); Dreaming upserts review_state 'accepted' unconditionally (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/session-core/src/dreaming.rs:1636-1690). Mitigations exist: the extractor treats conversation as untrusted and reads only user and assistant rows (session-core/src/dream_extractor.rs:616-640), but an assistant turn paraphrasing a poisoned page is still extracted. The proposed ZDR carrier does not fit, since MemoryRetention is credential-bound (session-core/src/grpc.rs:4096-4150) while taint appears mid-run. Instead: execution-core save_memory preflight refuses or marks the write when the run's tool history holds web, browser or mcp__ calls; Dreaming writes 'pending' or skips windows whose thread has such steps, derived from session_tool_audit_intents, and suppresses the Letta mirror; ship skip-only first (S, quick win). Drop the Data Plane clause; no agent-derived graph write path exists.

**Dependencies and risks.** Taint must be thread-window level; the pending path needs a new accept/reject RPC, and only the memory owner may promote.

<a id="F-t2-046"></a>
### F-t2-046 · Complete web-usage metering: browser metrics, outcome and surface attributes, price table and plan keys
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Control Plane (pricing, entitlements) + Ingestion Plane (metrics, concurrency) + Frontend Plane (rate-limit classes, usage UI) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Meter browser steps, minutes and agent runs, give extract and map their own metrics with outcome and surface attributes, add browser plan keys in billing-core, and put a per-org fair-share cap in front of the browser lease pool. The most expensive Quarry feature is invisible to billing, and one org can consume every lease.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the best source and metering is table-stakes at 3 of 4 references (Firecrawl, Apify, Aside). Firecrawl prices each job through one pure function with failures free, surcharges for LLM, PDF pages and ZDR, two limit units and a closed origin tag per request [SRC]. Apify meters compute units; Aside gates features per plan [DOC; Aside SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Quarry emits five idempotent metrics over NATS (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/usage.rs:50-94), but extract and map are billed as search queries and extract meters every attempted target (quarry-edge/src/extract_routes.rs:345-361); agent_routes.rs:700-761 emits no UsageEvent, leaving only a flat action_cost receipt for budgets. The scrape event puts the final URL into metadata even under ZDR (quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:490-505). No browser plan keys exist (apps/Control Plane/billing-core/internal/billing/defaults.go:5-80) and leases are two fixed process-wide pools (quarry-runtime/src/lease_pool.rs:30-45). Derive the surface tag from the verified principal, split extract and map and meter successes only, emit step and minute events with ids and counts only under ZDR, fix the metadata leak in the same change, put plan keys in billing-core behind the leads-style entitlement gate, and add a per-org semaphore before RuntimeLeasePool, the same per-org permits as [F-t2-025](#F-t2-025), built once.

**Dependencies and risks.** Prices never live in Quarry; each metric needs a Lago code first; confirm which key the gateway limiter uses before adding per-class buckets.

<a id="F-t2-047"></a>
### F-t2-047 · Fetch-path reliability: one deadline budget, wired retries and typed failure sub-codes
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane (quarry-runtime, quarry-core, orchestrator); Frontend Plane gateway for classification · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add typed failure sub-codes, a caller-visible timeout split across the driver waterfall with child cancellation, and in-process retries on the synchronous scrape path. Escalation through static, TLS and browser drivers should finish inside one deadline, and the Model and UI need to know why a fetch failed so they can switch source, wait or ask.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the best source for abort tiers and error vocabulary; retries are 2 of 4 references (Apify, Firecrawl), deadline budgeting and transportable codes 1 of 4 (Firecrawl). Firecrawl composes request, scrape-deadline and per-engine cancellation tokens and serialises one error union across queues and webhooks with a processing_continues detail [SRC]. Crawlee retries per request with error and failure hooks [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** execute_with_retry is called only by its own tests (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/lib.rs:164); live retries are Temporal's five attempts on job paths (quarry-orchestrator/internal/workflows/workflows.go:94-104). FallbackDriver walks drivers with no budget (quarry-runtime/src/fallback_driver.rs:57-120), ScrapeRequest has no timeout (quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:346-375) and FetchPolicy.timeout_s is read nowhere. DriverFailed is not retryable (quarry-core/src/error.rs:38-43), so wiring the helper alone does little. Reorder: sub-codes first (DriverFailed{dns|tls|connect|all_drivers_failed} with their own retryable flag; robots and policy denials non-retryable); then timeout_ms clamped by Quarry with per-attempt child tokens and a reserved slice for the last tier; then retries around the sync scrape path only, re-entering the per-host slot and reconciling the two retry config types.

**Dependencies and risks.** In-process retries on job paths would multiply Temporal attempts; model-gateway already bounds scrapes at 30 s; propagating sub-codes across planes raises effort to L and needs a versioned schema.

<a id="F-t2-048"></a>
### F-t2-048 · Org-scoped URL cache with explicit freshness, and a cache-only no-egress mode
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane; Data Plane for the artifact-backed no-egress path · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Replace the fingerprint-keyed page cache with an (org, normalised URL, variant) cache honouring maxAge, minAge and store flags, reporting cacheState and cachedAt and caching misses; later add a no_egress mode that serves only from cache and Data Plane artifacts. Repeated research gets cheaper, fewer requests hit target sites, and regulated workspaces get a hard no-outbound guarantee.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the only reference (1 of 4). Its key is a hash of the normalised URL plus a variant, with a metadata index and Redis hot path; reads resolve maxAge and return cacheState and cachedAt, negative windows skip the database, and lockdown rewrites the engine plan to index-only, forces ZDR and returns a typed miss without logging the URL [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** CachePolicy and should_read/should_write are live and bypassed under ZDR (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:56-76, 430-440), but the Redis PageCache is keyed by content fingerprint with no org segment and read only when the caller supplies prev_fingerprint (quarry-edge/src/cache.rs:1-30); the URL-plus-vary helper is dead scaffolding (cache.rs:84-105), the preset SWR field has no consumer, and the gateway keeps a URL-only-keyed preview cache (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/cache.rs:80-125). Simplify phase 1: keep Redis and the cached NormalizedOutput value, key through the existing helper with an org prefix mirroring SearchCache::key, add the freshness fields additively, implement or delete stale_while_revalidate_s, fix org scoping of the current cache in the same change (a caller with another tenant's fingerprint may read its output; verify) and retire the gateway copy. no_egress is phase 2 behind the [F-t2-022](#F-t2-022) policy floor.

**Dependencies and risks.** Never cache credentialed, cookie, profile or action requests; no_egress reaches Data Plane artifacts only through its API and returns a typed miss without logging the URL.

<a id="F-t2-049"></a>
### F-t2-049 · URL and domain reputation gate before fetch and navigation, then layered screening (reviewer model, Quarry-side injection labels)
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane + Model Plane (reviewer) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Automate and extend the host blocklist Quarry already loads so every fetch, redirect, click-driven navigation and subresource is checked, with a per-org mode and a decision source in egress receipts. Autonomous browsing can reach phishing or malware pages, and enterprise customers expect their own web-gateway policy to apply to agent traffic. Reviewer-model screening and Quarry-side prompt-injection labels are optional follow-ups.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the only reference with a reputation gate: 1 of 4 references [SRC]. Its checkUrl runs local allow and deny lists, optional customer secure-web-gateway categories and a hash-prefix or batched reputation provider before the first engine and on every redirect and discovered link, with an explicit fail-open or fail-closed policy [SRC]. Codex adds a tool-less reviewer with a per-origin cache and short expiry [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** An operator-run URLhaus/PhishTank sync (apps/Ingestion Plane/scripts/sync-security-feeds.py:1-12) writes a snapshot that quarry-edge loads once at boot (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/main.rs:345-372); SecurityEngine.preflight and validate_navigation consult it with exact-host matching (quarry-security/src/preflight.rs:22-60). Missing: scheduled sync, hot reload, subdomain matching, per-org modes, and enforcement on redirects, clicks and subresources, which use only the SSRF heuristic (quarry-browser/src/navigation.rs:1-35). Approach: scheduled sync with RwLock swap, suffix matching, org mode via the policy floor ([F-t2-022](#F-t2-022)), and a reputation callback in BrowserEgressPolicy so receipts carry a decision source. Add a hash-prefix provider only if feed coverage proves insufficient; never persist verdicts or send full URLs off-platform for Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) orgs.

**Dependencies and risks.** Layers 2 and 3 need Quarry-to-Model grants and an ADR and must never be the sole gate for irreversible tiers; false positives need an allow-list and a clear block message.

<a id="F-t2-050"></a>
### F-t2-050 · Fail-closed PII redaction stage for scraped and crawled content
*Verevon today: Planned · Effort: L · Plane: Ingestion Plane (stage) with Model Plane (PII model) and Control Plane (policy) · Wave: W3 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Add an optional stage that removes personally identifiable information (PII) from scraped and crawled content before anything is persisted, and that fails closed. Orgs could then ingest web content without storing personal data, which complements ZDR. Today only model input is filtered; what Quarry stores is not.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the only reference with this stage: 1 of 4 references [SRC]. After markdown derivation it sends markdown plus mode, entity list and replace style to a redaction service with several accuracy tiers; if the call fails or the size limit is exceeded it returns empty markdown with a warning [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** PageRunner builds markdown and metadata (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/pipeline.rs:505-516) and persists raw html, markdown and meta.json (pipeline.rs:604-660) without redaction. The existing redact_sensitive whitespace-tokenizes and would flatten markdown (quarry-runtime/src/agent_memory.rs:49-67); the Model Plane PII route takes no entity or replace-style parameters (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/http_routes.rs:1947-1957) and quarry-edge holds no model-gateway grant. SOVEREIGN_SCRAPING_RELIABILITY.md:97 and :218 already plan a PII Redactor stage. Approach: a deterministic redactor in quarry-transform (regex plus checksum validators for email, phone, IBAN, card Luhn and the Norwegian fodselsnummer) behind a per-request option and an org default from capability-core safety_policies, which owns PII policy rather than the Control Plane (model-gateway/src/moderation.rs:3-5). Fail closed at the persistence boundary: no html, markdown, meta, index or ingest write on error. Add the Model Plane PII model as a second tier after the grant lands.

**Dependencies and risks.** Model-gateway grant (shared with [F-t2-019](#F-t2-019)); the PII route is third-party processing under the PrivacyPolicy gate; over-redaction and non-English accuracy need tests.

<a id="F-t2-051"></a>
### F-t2-051 · Procedure store and run-as-procedure, with postconditions so real runs can compile
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Ingestion Plane · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Let a successful agent browsing session become a saved, versioned procedure that replays through the normal step path with grants, approvals and budgets re-applied. This is the step from "the agent browses" to "the product automates", and a replay is cheaper than re-planning. Quarry already has the hard half: compile, replay-compare, quality and impact checks.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the best source: 1 of 4 references [SRC]. Its agent route returns a skill, workflow and schema derived from a finished run, but the generator is closed upstream so only the route shape is known [SRC]. Apify adds immutable versioned builds with tags and records the resolved build on each run [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** compile_procedure, compare_replay, assess_quality and analyze_impact exist behind four stateless routes (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/browser_procedure.rs:61-200), but nothing stores the result, version is hard-coded to 1 and no execute route exists. compile_procedure rejects any receipt not marked Verified (browser_procedure.rs:85-96) while click, type, press, scroll, select and evaluate are Unknown (quarry-runtime/src/observation.rs:217-262), so a form-driving run cannot compile; ref-based actions embed snapshot_id and ref_id (quarry-core/src/contracts.rs:650-654) and cannot replay verbatim. Approach: add a step-level expect assertion Quarry evaluates to upgrade outcomes, canonicalise stored actions to SemanticLocator or ElementFingerprint, and fail closed on ambiguity. Put the store and runner in the Model Plane (capability-core or session-core), proposing each stored step to Quarry's step endpoint, because Quarry's own docs place rollout state outside Quarry (quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:2462-2466). Stage: postconditions (M), canonicalisation (M), store plus runner (L).

**Dependencies and risks.** Needs [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001), [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008), [F-t2-010](#F-t2-010); never reuse a stored sensitive approval; ZDR runs write no receipts and cannot be saved.

<a id="F-t2-052"></a>
### F-t2-052 · Per-run result sets with a dataset table view and server-side export
*Verevon today: Absent · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Give every crawl and batch run an inspectable result set with a table view in the Ingestions page and streamed CSV and JSONL export. Users extracting prices, listings or procurement notices need a table they can filter and export without forcing everything into the Data Plane; ingest stays an explicit, selective decision.

**Reference practice.** Apify is the only reference with per-run storages: 1 of 4 references [DOC]. Run creation allocates a dataset, a key-value store and a request queue; the dataset is append-only and exports server-side in csv, jsonl, xlsx, xml or rss with field projection, and unnamed storages expire after about seven days [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** The edge route table has artifact list and get, jobs, events, sources, snapshots and team aggregates only (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:130-293); ArtifactSummary has no run_id although keys are laid out org/run/page_hash/kind/id (quarry-core/src/resources.rs:83-97). The Ingestions page shows no result data (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/ingestions/components/VerevonIngestionsPage.tsx:67-114), and /v1/extract is synchronous and persists nothing. Approach, lighter than a new JSONL store: (1) add run_id to ArtifactSummary and a run filter on /v1/artifacts (S); (2) project per-page rows from the page_extracted events already stored per job (routes.rs:776-830) plus artifact refs as GET /v1/runs/{id}/items with streamed csv and jsonl (M); (3) a table view in run detail with an ingest action. Tie a durable structured dataset to durable extraction jobs, which do not exist yet.

**Dependencies and risks.** Crawl and batch are refused under ZDR (routes.rs:555-575); artifact reads are org-bound, not per actor; keep it a working set with retention, not a second Data Plane.

<a id="F-t2-053"></a>
### F-t2-053 · Saved job definitions: expose presets, then first-party Quarry packs with typed schemas
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: L · Plane: Ingestion Plane (with Frontend Plane for forms) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Give users and the Model Plane a menu of saved, typed job definitions instead of free-form crawls, with Quarry validating input and keeping execution authority. Phase 1 is mostly making dormant code work: stored scrape, crawl and batch schedules never fire, and presets are decorative at execution time.

**Reference practice.** Apify is the best source and Codex a second: 2 of 4 references [DOC, SRC]. Apify declares one manifest per unit with input and output JSON Schema whose UI hints render the form and validate API input identically; tasks are saved input plus run options that can be run, scheduled and shared [DOC]. Codex gates bundled plugins with an availability check before install [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** GET /v1/presets exists only on quarry-control (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-control/cmd/control/main.go:174), unproxied by edge; every PageRunner is built with RunPolicy::default() (quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:461) and the orchestrator never reads its Policy field. Schedules accept cron for any kind, but control maps only change_monitor to a workflow (quarry-control/internal/resources/schedule_wire.go:37-52) although Scrape, Crawl and Batch workflows are registered (quarry-orchestrator/cmd/orchestrator/main.go:94-118). A durable Sources registry already overlaps "save as preset" (quarry-edge/src/resource_routes.rs:410-426). Approach: (a) extend workflowFor so non-monitor schedules fire with a per-fire run_id; (b) define a saved definition as a Source plus request-level options, or drop the Go RunPolicy presets; do not expose /v1/presets until policy is enforced, since the fast preset ignores robots (presets.go:69); (c) rename the overloaded term. Demote the QuarryPack manifest and schema form until a second consumer exists.

**Dependencies and risks.** Saved crawl and batch jobs are refused under ZDR; Space-shared definitions must reference only sources the viewer already sees; keep packs declarative and first-party.

<a id="F-t2-055"></a>
### F-t2-055 · Crawl planning: validate/preview endpoint, Model Plane crawl-plan tool and a stronger /v1/map
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane (validate, map) with Model Plane (planner tool) and Frontend Plane (review UI) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Let a user scope a crawl in one sentence: a Model Plane tool maps the site, drafts crawl options from the goal, and the user approves the proposed scope before the gateway starts the crawl under the user token. This fits "Model proposes, Quarry validates and executes" and improves discovery on docs sites with weak sitemaps.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the only reference with natural-language crawl planning: 1 of 4 references; on site mapping Verevon is already at parity [SRC]. Firecrawl maps up to 50 URLs to ground the model, overlays only fields the caller did not set, re-runs the normal validator and returns a preview or starts the crawl; the ZDR path keeps the model step outside traces [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** /v1/map is shipped end to end with robots allow-filtering and sitemap.xml seeding (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/map_routes.rs:132-300) and CrawlPagePicker lets users curate pages. There is no /v1/crawl/validate, and CrawlRequest carries only url, max_pages, max_depth, ingest and zdr (quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:533-554), so there are no scope options to validate until [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016). Map ignores the Sitemap: lines that robots.rs:80-82 already parses, ranks lexically (map_routes.rs:266-270) and is closed to service principals (quarry-edge/src/auth.rs:504-535); execution-core web_tools declares only search:read and extract:read (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/web_tools.rs:39-40). Approach: map:read service scope and robots Sitemap seeding first (both S), then the planner tool (M); cut validate to a thin map-based preview; extend CrawlPagePicker to show the proposed scope instead of a new review UI; use the granted Data Plane web-rerank row only when its token is available, degrading to lexical order.

**Dependencies and risks.** Crawl is refused under ZDR, so a ZDR plan can be proposed but not run; always show drafted scope before running; a site: search pass spends paid-provider budget.

<a id="F-t2-056"></a>
### F-t2-056 · Provider APIs and export rewrites before rendering (Google Docs, GitHub, Notion, transcripts), after verifying current coverage
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Ingestion Plane (Quarry planner + integration-corev2) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** When a user or agent pastes a Google, Microsoft or GitHub document link, return the document through the user's connected provider account instead of a rendered login wall or a terms-of-service-risky scrape. integration-corev2 already owns the OAuth tokens and read actions, so the work is routing and agent-visible content, not new connectors.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the best source: 1 of 4 references [SRC]. Its URL normaliser rewrites Google document links to export URLs, and an engine resolver calls a provider API when the host matches a connected account, returning a normal scrape result [SRC]. Codex exports signed-in documents through the page session with strict URL validation [SRC, DOC]; Verevon should use OAuth provider actions instead, for credential isolation [INF].

**Verevon today and approach.** integration-corev2 ships Google drive.file.content with text or Office export (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/actions/drive_read.go:30-60), Microsoft drive.file.content, GitHub contents.get and readme.get (internal/actions/service.go:219, 348, 366) and list-only Notion pages (service.go:463-466). Model Plane agents reach integration-corev2, but the agent catalog omits every content op (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/integration_tools.rs:98-141), so such calls need approval as unknown-means-write (execution-core/src/permission/mod.rs:195-218) and results are cut at 2000 characters (integration_tools.rs:817-825). Quarry has no rewrite table, provider resolver, transcript format or /v1/parse. Approach: (1) a read-only document tool at the Model Plane or gateway that maps URL to provider and file id and decodes content to bounded text (S to M); (2) a small public-URL rewrite table in Quarry routed through the file router ([F-t2-009](#F-t2-009)) (S); (3) demote the caption transcript format; no Quarry /v1/parse without a quality comparison.

**Dependencies and risks.** Reads must use the requesting user's own connection; provider ACL and ZDR must travel with ingested content; Google and Microsoft content ops are not yet verified against live providers; Notion content needs a new action.

<a id="F-t2-058"></a>
### F-t2-058 · Scrape request context and egress tiers: locale/geo/mobile emulation, country egress with policy-gated auto-escalation, profile pinning and cache partitioning
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Ingestion Plane + Control Plane (tier cap, metering) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Let a scrape carry request context (locale, timezone, geolocation, device, an authenticated profile, a country egress tier) so a Norwegian-first product can fetch localized content, and so personalised pages never leak across tenants through a shared cache. Policy, never the Model, picks the proxy tier.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the best source: 1 of 4 references for profile routing and location, 2 of 4 (Firecrawl, Apify) for tiers and geo proxies [SRC, DOC]. Firecrawl restricts profile requests to profile-capable browser engines, treats headers, actions or profile as personalised with no cache use, sets Accept-Language and timezone from a validated country, and retries once on a stronger pool after a block signal [SRC]. Apify adds locale-constrained fingerprints and a scored SessionPool [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** ScrapeRequest has no profile_id, location, device or proxy field (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:346-375); profile_required arrives only through the client-supplied signals object (quarry-runtime/src/driver_plan.rs:36-48). The local driver sets no user-agent, locale, timezone or geolocation override (quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:1851-1860) and relaunches the shared Chromium when the viewport changes (chromiumoxide.rs:194-270). Egress is richer than assumed, a processor-gated pool with rotation on 401/403/407/429/503 (quarry-runtime/src/egress_broker.rs:102-163), but has no tier, country or request-level field, and the Chromium path has no upstream proxy chaining (quarry-runtime/src/browser_egress_proxy.rs:1-8). The cache is content-addressed, not option-keyed (quarry-edge/src/cache.rs:1-36), so the cache-key premise is wrong. Approach, in slices: (a) per-page emulation with session-scoped contexts, needs [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007) (M); (b) profile pinning for scrape with a hard no-cache rule and an org prefix on the cache key (M); (c) egress tiers and country behind the processor gate, browser chaining designed first (L); (d) defer SessionPool.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t2-020](#F-t2-020), [F-t2-022](#F-t2-022), [F-t2-027](#F-t2-027) to narrow block statuses before escalation; proxies are processors that resolve DNS server-side, so the SSRF boundary needs an explicit decision; residential tiers stay off by default.

<a id="F-t2-059"></a>
### F-t2-059 · Register governed remote browser backends (Kernel, Browserless, Browserbase) behind an egress-containment proof
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: XL · Plane: Ingestion Plane (quarry-browser, quarry-edge) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Give the in-app browser and agent runs a scale-out path beyond the single local Chromium by registering remote backends with declared capability sets, so the Model states requirements and Quarry selects or rejects a backend without the Model ever naming a provider. Capability flags and the isolated-egress gate already exist, so governance stays intact.

**Reference practice.** Codex is the only reference with a multi-backend list: 1 of 4 references [SRC]. Backends are registered from config at startup, the session API lists them with type, family, profile and metadata, and the planner chooses by required capabilities such as user login or isolated egress [SRC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Capability flags and gates exist (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/lib.rs:265-286; quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:1125-1217), but the agent path is hard-wired to one ChromiumoxideDriver (quarry-edge/src/main.rs:1283-1288) and the scrape path has a single Browser slot that Browserbase takes first (main.rs:322-339). Kernel and Browserless are one-shot REST shims built only in tests (quarry-browser/src/kernel.rs:1-40, browserless.rs:1-30) that cannot serve ref actions, AX snapshots or live frames, so the premise that they are ready to register is wrong. Approach in three parts: (1) scrape path only, key the registry beyond DriverKind::Browser and register Kernel or Browserless behind config with the managed_provider processor guard (S to M); (2) for agent sessions, a CDP-attached remote driver reusing ChromiumoxideDriver over Browser::connect, a backend registry in AppState and a real containment proof (XL); (3) GET /v1/agent/backends with selection by the driver_requirements field already accepted on start (S).

**Dependencies and risks.** The pinned egress proxy is loopback-only (lib.rs:331-360), so routing provider traffic through it contradicts that invariant; remote providers are processors refused under ZDR and need DPA review; per-provider redirect, iframe and subresource SSRF tests are a documented gate.

<a id="F-t2-060"></a>
### F-t2-060 · Isolate each browser session or tenant in its own container or VM instead of a shared Chromium
*Verevon today: Absent · Effort: XL · Plane: Ingestion Plane · Wave: W4 · Confidence: High*

**What and why.** Run each org's (later each session's) browser in its own container or microVM with its own Chromium, tmpfs profile, cgroup limits and egress only through the egress broker. A browser that users and agents drive on arbitrary sites is a tenant boundary; a renderer exploit in a shared Chromium crosses tenants. Two cheap hardening fixes should ship first.

**Reference practice.** Apify is the only reference, and documentation only: 1 of 4 references [DOC]. It runs each Actor in an isolated per-run container under SOC 2 Type II without disclosing the runtime technology [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** Isolation is one Chromium per driver (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:194-262) with a per-session CDP context inside it (chromiumoxide.rs:396-420). ensure_browser relaunches that shared Chromium, clearing every session's pages, when a lease asks for a different viewport (chromiumoxide.rs:194-223), so concurrent tenants can kill each other's runs. The compose default disables the Chromium sandbox when QUARRY_BROWSER_NO_SANDBOX is unset, contradicting its own comment (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:97-99); production manifests were not checked. sandbox-manager is only a lease registry that starts nothing (apps/Model Plane/go/services/sandbox-manager/internal/server/server.go:84-97) and is the wrong plane, so that option is dropped. Approach: step 1 (S to M, quick win), apply viewport per page or context through CDP emulation and flip the compose default; step 2 (L), a CDP-connect mode on ChromiumoxideDriver and a per-org pool of Chromium worker containers, each with its own pinned-proxy sidecar, owned by the Ingestion Plane; step 3 (XL, optional), microVM per session. The Kernel and Browserless shims are not a shortcut.

**Dependencies and risks.** The loopback-only proxy type needs a sidecar design or a reviewed relaxation; profile export stays blocked under ZDR; scrape-path sessions are keyed by host only (quarry-runtime/src/browser_driver.rs:139-148), so same-host scrapes from different orgs may share a session key [INF, not tested].

<a id="F-t2-062"></a>
### F-t2-062 · Budgeted web research job contract with run-context cost attribution
*Verevon today: Partial · Effort: M · Plane: Model Plane (run contract) with Ingestion Plane (run-context attribution) · Wave: W4 · Confidence: Medium*

**What and why.** Make web research an addressable run with a caller budget, partial results, a stop reason and one active run per thread, and let Quarry attach its cost events to the parent run so nothing is billed twice. One predictable contract serves other features better than stitching step-level routes, and per-run cost depends on Quarry knowing the parent run.

**Reference practice.** Firecrawl is the only reference with a research-job API: 1 of 4 references [SRC]. POST /agent takes prompt, optional urls, schema, a credit cap and effort; GET returns status, data, partial, schema validity, stop reason and credits used; a thread rejects a new turn with 409 while a run is active, and internal services pass a request id and a billing flag so tool calls attach to the parent run [SRC]. Apify caps Actor runs by maximum charge [DOC].

**Verevon today and approach.** A research pipeline already exists as a chat feature: model-gateway deep_research plans, searches, reads and synthesizes under the turn's session run with spend caps and a halt_code stop reason (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/deep_research.rs:58-140, 600-660), but it dies with the stream (deep_research.rs:1-24), takes no caller budget or output schema, and no thread-busy guard exists. execution-core web tools send only a bearer and x-quarry-org (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/web_tools.rs:95-96), and Quarry usage events carry no run id (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/usage.rs:47-83); a USD ceiling exists only for browser runs. Approach: promote deep_research to an addressable run (status, partial results, stopReason from halt_code, 409 with the running run id), map the caller budget onto the existing caps plus a credit or USD ceiling enforced in the Model Plane, and add output-schema validation after [F-t2-019](#F-t2-019). Separately, send a signed requestId and parentRunId from web_tools and the gateway Quarry client, written into UsageEvent.metadata as advisory attribution, never authority.

**Dependencies and risks.** [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001); a durable job record must retain no content for ZDR callers; cost-core ingestion of Quarry cost was not traced; crawl starts remain approval-gated.

## 6. Nice to have

Nice-to-have items are improvements that none of the priority or recommended work depends on. Some are polish that the reference browsers ship and users will eventually expect; others are bets that the code verification demoted from "recommended" because a premise did not hold, a prerequisite is unbuilt, or an owner ruling parks them. Status, effort and approach on every entry come from the verified data for Verevon at commit afb83928 (2026-10-10); where a note says "demoted", the verification pass changed the tier. Acronyms used below: ZDR is Zero-Data-Retention, UA is user agent, CDP is the Chrome DevTools Protocol, MCP is the Model Context Protocol, ADR is an Architecture Decision Record, SIEM is Security Information and Event Management.

### 6.1 Identity and network posture

- <a id="F-t2-066"></a>**F-t2-066 · Policy-gated browsing preset limited to consent-banner handling and ad/tracker filtering (fingerprint masking deferred to an ADR)** (Stub · M · Ingestion Plane · W5). Wire consent-banner dismissal and optional ad/tracker filtering behind an org policy flag; leave fingerprint masking to an ADR because Verevon's contract forbids challenge bypass and the honest posture fits it. Reference: Apify fingerprint-suite and Camoufox [DOC]; Firecrawl blockAds through uBlock Origin Lite [SRC]; Codex ships no stealth because it drives real user browsers [SRC]. Verevon gap: stealth.rs scripts have no production caller (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/lib.rs:164 only exports the module); inject CONSENT_DISMISS_SCRIPT alone, reuse install_network_guard as the filter hook, log every Quarry-initiated click in receipts, and note that the current script accepts cookies instead of rejecting them [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-067"></a>**F-t2-067 · One honest product identity: unified user-agent and optional signed agent traffic (Web Bot Auth style)** (Partial · M · Ingestion Plane, Control Plane for key custody · W5). One product UA and bot-information page, then optional request signing so sites that admit verified agents let governed traffic through. Nice to have because self-identification can also increase blocks and the standard is still emerging. Reference: Codex gates agent-originated requests on an identifying agent header [SRC]; Web Bot Auth is an industry analogue, not a studied source [INF]. Verevon gap: five unaligned UA strings plus a FetchPolicy.user_agent field that is hashed but never applied (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/config.rs:301-303 still names an older domain); route one quarry-core constant everywhere, and settle a per-tier identity stance before signing, since the driver ladder presents browser identities on its upper tiers [SRC].

### 6.2 Browser panel polish and browsing data

- <a id="F-t2-068"></a>**F-t2-068 · Browsing data lifecycle: server-side history with agent/user source, bookmarks, clear-site-data, permission-deny events, support export** (Partial · L · Ingestion Plane with Frontend Plane · W5). Let users see and undo what the agent did in their profile. Table-stakes in both reference browsers, but not needed for the agent loop itself. Reference: Codex keeps a history with an Agent badge and source filter, a clear-data dialog that shows counts first, and per-page permission prompts; Aside has bookmarks, a history panel and a privacy page [SRC]. Verevon gap: the shell keeps a browser-local list of 10 entries (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/web-history.ts:32), while a durable per-run owner timeline with a human/agent/system initiator and ZDR 409 handling already exists; split into clear-data on the stored SessionSnapshot through ProfileStore (S-M) and a derived cross-run visit log that never stores URLs for ZDR runs [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-069"></a>**F-t2-069 · Quarry-backed page utilities: find, zoom, print-to-PDF, full-page and area screenshot, viewport emulation, colour scheme** (Partial · M · Ingestion Plane with Frontend Plane UI · W5).
