Expose capture the driver already does, then add find, viewport and colour-scheme actions. Reference: Codex has find-in-page, per-tab zoom, device-metrics override with reset, and emulated colour scheme re-synced on theme change; Aside has an area-capture overlay and copy to clipboard [SRC]. Verevon gap: the driver already produces full-page screenshots and PDFs (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:2392-2444), but the gateway allowlist rejects pdf and coerces its media type to image/png, so step 1 is gateway and frontend only (S); viewport changes relaunch the shared Chromium and clear every page, so emulation needs a per-lease design [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-070"></a>**F-t2-070 · Browser chrome polish in the SPA: tab menu, new-tab page, typed error cards, split view, keyboard commands, activity strip, reader view** (Partial · M · Frontend Plane · W5). Small daily frictions once the browser is usable, and understandable denials instead of silent failures. Reference: Codex has tab context-menu commands, local error pages keyed on net error codes, a central keymap and a browser activity preview; Aside has a search-or-ask new-tab page, split view and reader mode [SRC]. Verevon gap: the tab strip has no context menu (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/BrowserChrome.tsx:1075-1138), though open-in-system-browser and a reader-style markdown render exist; do typed navigation errors once with [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032), route every tab operation through gateway-to-Quarry routes, and defer split view, activity strip and reader view [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-071"></a>**F-t2-071 · Render MCP App (ui://) resources in an opaque-origin sandbox, and retire the permissive web-view iframe** (Partial · L · Frontend Plane, data path owned by Model Plane · W5). Show interactive UI returned by MCP servers inline and safely. Emerging, 1 of 4 references, not a parity blocker. Reference: Codex renders MCP App resources in a guest view on a dedicated sandbox origin, allows only about, blob, data, https and wss, denies permission requests and limits the agent to locator actions [SRC]. Verevon gap: no ui://, resources/read or postMessage bridge exists anywhere, but the opaque-origin srcdoc sandbox primitive is shipped (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/chat/components/ChatArtifactPanel.tsx:497-525); delete the unused WebPageViewer now (S) and build the MCP App frame later with resource reads proxied through model-gateway and widget tool calls under Model Plane approvals [SRC].

### 6.3 Collaboration and governance

- <a id="F-t2-072"></a>**F-t2-072 · Share an agent run and its evidence with Space members through the existing resource-grant authority** (Absent · L · Application Plane projection with Ingestion Plane artifact serving, grant decision in Control Plane · W5). Read-only sharing of a finished run's timeline, receipts and screenshots with named users or a Space; natural for a workspace product but not needed for single-user flows. Reference: Aside publishes an immutable snapshot of transcript and tool outputs with capped images [SRC]; Apify adds time-limited pre-signed URLs and scoped grants [DOC]. Verevon gap: the ShareDialog backend is document-only and user-core's taxonomy classes quarry_run as team_shared, so per-user grants are rejected (apps/Control Plane/user-core/internal/authztaxonomy/taxonomy.go:91-101); enforce the viewer grant at the gateway, which already gates artifacts, rather than in Quarry, which has no user-core client, and never widen an owner-plane ACL through a Space [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-073"></a>**F-t2-073 · Control Plane governance extras: SIEM export, saved approval preferences and privacy-safe usage events** (Absent · L · Control Plane · W5). Enterprise checkboxes and feature-usage insight, all invisible to day-one users and dependent on the unified decision audit of [F-t2-036](#F-t2-036). Reference: Firecrawl exports per-org batches with a bounded queue, retry ladder, dead-letter queue and destination allowlist; Codex keeps a revisioned preferences document and typed usage events without URLs [SRC]; Aside has opt-out telemetry [SRC]. Verevon gap: audit-core consumes audit and usage subjects into append-only tables but exposes only a pull API (apps/Control Plane/audit-core/internal/api/api.go:106-115); split the bundle, let customers pull GET /v1/audit with a service token as an S interim, fold approval preferences into [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032), and keep usage events off under ZDR [SRC]. Confidence low.

### 6.4 Engineering hygiene

- <a id="F-t2-074"></a>**F-t2-074 · Quarry failure-mode and provider hardening: fail-open policy review, opaque provider errors, Postgres maintenance (verify first)** (Absent · S · Ingestion Plane · W5). Small, confirmed, low-urgency hardening. Reference: Firecrawl caches entitlements with a fail-open flag and 60 second TTL, maps hosted-browser errors to fixed messages with the body dropped, and runs staggered pg_cron reindex jobs [SRC]. Verevon gap: the Browserbase, Kernel and Browserless drivers put raw upstream bodies into error details (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/browserbase.rs:95-130) and the ingestion Postgres has no autovacuum tuning; the fail-open premise has no counterpart in Quarry, whose real outage exposure is JWKS, so add a bounded stale-if-error window to auth.rs, a ProviderError type that never echoes bodies, and a RUNBOOKS entry that grants, ZDR and billing stay fail-closed [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-075"></a>**F-t2-075 · Contract and signal hygiene: deprecation registry, signed browser and sandbox images, web-result usefulness signal** (Absent · L · Ingestion Plane with Support Plane and Model Plane · W5). Cheap insurance; verification narrowed it (its tier was already nice-to-have) because the code contradicts its premises. Reference: Firecrawl drives Deprecation, Sunset, Link and Warning headers from a typed registry and takes job-keyed feedback; Codex signs bundle receipts in CI [SRC]. Verevon gap: quarry-edge serves /v1 routes with no deprecation handling (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:131-275), no workflow references cosign or provenance, and no usefulness event exists; Quarry's only callers are the gateway and Model Plane, so fold deprecation flags into the OpenAPI-generation work, which now sits in [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005) (moved there from [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064)), defer signing until a registry publish pipeline exists, and keep any usefulness signal to ids and domains [SRC].

### 6.5 Agent scaling bets and parked items

- <a id="F-t2-076"></a>**F-t2-076 · Agent scaling bets: child browser subagents and WebMCP page tools (site playbooks and MCP exposure are covered by F-t2-043 and F-t2-064)** (Partial · L · Model Plane · W5). Parallel browser research by child runs and typed page tools; later-stage bets that depend on grant wiring and the live planner loop. Reference: Aside spawns subagent profiles with isolated browser state; Codex enables WebMCP per frame with toolsAdded and toolsRemoved events [SRC]. Verevon gap: delegated subagents are shipped with depth 1 and a per-run ceiling, but the leaf role refuses browser_agent and browser.act as risky (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/permission/mod.rs:259-272); add a read-only browser action class to BrowserBroker grants as a strict subset of the parent grant, reuse run_delegated_child, add a per-org live-run cap and a page-scoped viewport first, and keep WebMCP a research spike (the CDP WebMCP domain is experimental [INF]) until real sites publish tools, after which a descriptor would run as a fenced, one-shot-approved Quarry action [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-077"></a>**F-t2-077 · Design spike only: drive the user's own signed-in Chrome through an attested extension backend, behind per-tab consent** (Absent · XL · Ingestion Plane backend abstraction with Frontend extension distribution · W5). The only route to SSO-protected apps without importing credentials, but parked: the product owner ruled on 2026-09-28 that the user's own Chrome and its logins are not driven, and it conflicts with governed egress. Reference: Codex pairs a Manifest V3 extension using chrome.debugger with a native messaging host that relays CDP commands and enforces per-tab consent [SRC]. Verevon gap: no extension, native host or user-browser executor exists (ruling at apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/verevon-web/plans/system-audits/CHAT_PAGE_GAP_PLAN_2026-09-28.md:250-264); record it as a decision note listing what reopens it: an owner decision, the multi-backend abstraction of [F-t2-059](#F-t2-059) and a redesigned fail-closed gate [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-054"></a>**F-t2-054 · Run- and turn-scoped tabs: deterministic cleanup, persisted tab sets and cold restore under a live-page budget** (Partial · L · Ingestion Plane with Model Plane run and turn ids · W4) *Moved down from Recommended by verification.*. Demoted: the premise that agent-opened tabs are left behind does not match the code. Reference: Codex marks tabs as deliverable or handoff and runs a once-only turn-ended cleanup that closes unmarked tabs, clears downloads and detaches the debugger, with thread-keyed tabs and a live-page budget [SRC]. Verevon gap: AgentAction has no tab verbs, window.open is rewritten into the same tab, execution-core closes the run at loop end, and the checkpoint holds a single current_url (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/step_receipts.rs:25-71); first decide whether a chat thread keeps one long-lived Quarry run across turns, then add agent tab verbs before any cleanup logic [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-061"></a>**F-t2-061 · Agent routines: scheduled, heartbeat and event-triggered agent runs with per-fire browser grants** (Partial · XL · Model Plane · W4) *Moved down from Recommended by verification.*. A proactive agent that watches a page or reacts to a webhook; demoted because the distinctive parts are blocked by the Wave-3 draft-only ruling and unbuilt grant issuance. Reference: Aside exposes one routine_update tool over a routines table with cron, heartbeat and event kinds [SRC]; 1 of 4 references. Verevon gap: cron is shipped only as a user-authored, Space-bound, draft-only lane whose fires run one inference-only step with per-fire Control reauthorization (apps/Model Plane/go/services/capability-core/internal/cron/sweeper.go:16-69); stage 1 is an agent-proposed routine that the user confirms through the existing user-bound create path, and event triggers come from Space Watch adapters, never a service insert [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-063"></a>**F-t2-063 · Code-mode browser control: sandboxed script runtime over typed Quarry bindings, with approval-input hardening** (Planned · L · Model Plane · W4) *Moved down from Recommended by verification.*. Fewer round-trips for multi-step page work; demoted because code-mode is already planned generically and the savings are unmeasured. Reference: Aside keeps one persistent QuickJS scope per session with a small host surface and non-marshalable handles; Codex freezes approval inputs to close a TOCTOU gap; Firecrawl has an interact tool [SRC]; 3 of 4 references. Verevon gap: browser.act is a single closed-schema action with snapshot refs only (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/runtime_loop/agent.rs:3479-3508); fold a browser binding set into the planned code-mode, run the engine as a bwrap'd child with network disabled rather than an in-process isolate, and keep one approval per Quarry step, never per script [SRC].

### 6.6 Engine and external contract

- <a id="F-t2-057"></a>**F-t2-057 · Engine planner v2: declared capability matrix, typed re-plan signals with budgets, learned per-domain mode and a domain-intelligence registry** (Partial · M · Ingestion Plane · W4) *Moved down from Recommended by verification.*. Mostly an internal efficiency refactor; demoted because per-request escalation already works and only three drivers exist. Reference: Firecrawl builds feature flags per request, picks engines from a declared matrix and hedges a waterfall with typed re-plan [SRC]; Apify's AdaptivePlaywrightCrawler learns a per-domain mode [DOC]. Verevon gap: plan_from_signals maps five booleans to a fixed static, TLS, browser order with no per-domain memory (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/driver_plan.rs:36-50); narrow to a per-domain render-mode memory in quarry-control fed by non-ZDR traffic only, and defer the matrix until [F-t2-059](#F-t2-059) adds a fourth driver [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-064"></a>**F-t2-064 · External contract: full OpenAPI, then an audience-bound MCP server for Quarry and read-only connectors, SDK call-and-wait and a first no-code connector** (Partial · XL · Ingestion Plane with Control Plane auth-core · W4) *Moved down from Recommended by verification.*. The distribution channel for external agents; demoted because it reverses a documented deferral and needs customer authentication that does not exist. Reference: Apify hosts a Streamable-HTTP MCP server behind OAuth with on-demand tool groups and a full OpenAPI [DOC]; Firecrawl's hosted MCP uses OAuth introspection with audience binding and a per-call tool log [SRC]; MCP exposure is 3 of 4 references. Verevon gap: docs/openapi.yaml is hand-maintained with 46 paths and omits registered routes such as profiles, answer and search verticals (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:135-171); keep OpenAPI generation with a route-coverage check inside [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005), and treat the MCP server, connector and CLI as a conditional later item that must not become a third audit producer [SRC].
- <a id="F-t2-065"></a>**F-t2-065 · Cached declarative extraction profiles generated by the Model, with bounded browser-agent escalation** (Absent · XL · Ingestion Plane with Model Plane · W4) *Moved down from Recommended by verification.*. Cuts per-page model cost on recurring templates and fits "Model proposes, Quarry executes"; demoted because it is not buildable as described. Reference: Firecrawl caches a codegen extractor keyed by model, url, schema and prompt, regenerates on validation failure, and escalates to SmartScrape when data sits behind interaction [SRC]; 1 of 4 references. Verevon gap: the ExtractionProfile executor is a string scanner that supports only id, data-testid or a bare tag name (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/observation.rs:2255-2380), and the Quarry-to-Model inference grant is refused; first make the executor a usable generation target on scraper selectors with versioned schema, then add the per-org, non-ZDR cache, and hold Phase B until cumulative cost enforcement exists [SRC].

## 7. What each reference does uniquely well, and how that helps Verevon

Provenance tags: [SRC] read in source or shipped files, [REA] reverse-engineered from the shipped app, [DOC] documentation only, [INF] inference. Codex and Aside facts come from static analysis of the installed apps, Firecrawl facts from its open-source repo, and Apify facts from public docs only.

### 7.1 Codex desktop app (in-app browser, computer use), version 26.1007.2314.0

Codex is best at making agent browsing safe by construction: a typed, fail-closed authorisation pipeline, credential delivery the model never sees, and engine-level enforcement of navigation and downloads. [SRC] It has no crawl, queue, schedule, stealth or proxy layer, so it is a reference for the browser half only. [SRC] Its Electron shell and the user's-own-Chrome extension do not fit Verevon's remote, leased Chromium; the extension is a design spike at most ([F-t2-077](#F-t2-077)) because the product owner ruled it out on 2026-09-28 (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/verevon-web/plans/system-audits/CHAT_PAGE_GAP_PLAN_2026-09-28.md:250-264). [SRC]

- **Credential handoff with an observation gate (Codex Cloud Browser).** `browserAuth` proceeds only if the origin is https, the frame origin equals the top origin and exactly one required input exists; values go from the host into an isolated world, the model receives only a status, and accessibility, DOM and screenshot reads of that document are denied until the next navigation; an email one-time password (OTP) variant raises a user elicitation the reviewer cannot auto-approve. [SRC] This is wired to the hosted Cloud Browser, not the shipped desktop browser. [SRC] Verevon has no secret-reference action; [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024) copies the shape with integration-corev2 as custodian.
- **One policy choke point with typed denials and per-origin consent.** Every command passes an ordered gate chain; a denial carries a reason, a decision source, a retryable flag and text telling the model not to work around it; consent is stored at turn, conversation or global scope and resolved deny-first; an org origin policy merges per field with deny winning. [SRC] Verevon's grant check is a choke point that returns generic codes; [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032) adds consent and typed denials, [F-t2-036](#F-t2-036) adds Codex's one audit record per decision.
- **Hit-tested trusted input.** Before a ref-targeted click Codex probes `elementFromPoint` per frame hop, scrolls into view only if the target is not hittable, polls every 25 ms for two stable samples under a 500 ms deadline, then dispatches trusted `Input` events; failures are typed as covered-by, did-not-settle or could-not-be-clicked. [SRC] Quarry's ref actions call `element.click()` in page script with no actionability check; [F-t2-015](#F-t2-015) carries this.
- **Structurally safe evaluate, gated raw CDP.** `evaluate` runs in an isolated world against a read-only proxy of `document`, masks credential fields, blocks `import()` and caps results; raw Chrome DevTools Protocol (CDP) access needs a preference that defaults to false, an enterprise requirement and per-origin consent. [SRC] Quarry runs `page.evaluate` in the main world with no gate; [F-t2-002](#F-t2-002) adds both.
- **Turn-scoped agent tabs with handoff marks.** Agent tabs open hidden; `markTab handoff|deliverable` decides which survive the turn; a once-only turn-ended tracker closes the rest and the Interrupt, Stop and SubagentStop hooks all call it. [SRC] Codex has no code-enforced "user took over" state, so that part of [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) is a Verevon design. [SRC] The lifecycle is carried by [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) and [F-t2-054](#F-t2-054).
- **Settle heuristics and a frame-aware accessibility snapshot.** After an action Codex awaits navigation commit and first paint, then waits for accessibility and DOM mutation events to go quiet (capped at 750 ms); the snapshot stitches `Accessibility.getFullAXTree` per auto-attached frame session under per-frame timeouts inside a global deadline and lists per-frame failures as warnings. [SRC] Quarry captures immediately and walks one root frame; [F-t2-034](#F-t2-034) carries both.
- **Honest bot-block handling and per-command deadlines.** A classifier reads site-served evidence, names explicit non-signals (bare 403/429, timeouts, sign-in pages, paywalls), allows one recovery and reports a closed reason set with the hostname only; no stealth code exists in the app. [SRC] Every CDP call carries a deadline from a constants table and a destroyed execution context is retried once. [SRC] [F-t2-027](#F-t2-027) and [F-t2-026](#F-t2-026) are both W1 quick wins.
- **Operational hygiene and a multi-backend protocol.** Log calls split fields into safe and sensitive maps app-wide (sink behaviour not found); a setting suppresses memory extraction after web or MCP (Model Context Protocol) tool use; the browser API marks each member as unsupported in named backends and the agent lists capabilities and reads docs on demand. [SRC] These feed [F-t2-004](#F-t2-004), [F-t2-045](#F-t2-045), [F-t2-059](#F-t2-059) and [F-t2-043](#F-t2-043). WebMCP page tools are a long-tail bet only ([F-t2-076](#F-t2-076)).

### 7.2 Aside browser, version 1.0.928.2

Aside is best at agent ergonomics for a background-first local agent: authenticated local control surfaces, credential indirection, steering and recovery, and tab hygiene. [SRC] It is a single-user desktop browser with no fleet, proxy pool or crawl scale, and its defaults are open (browser and network rules allow by default, dialogs auto-accept, a cookie-based site API and a CAPTCHA solver), so its topology is not adoptable and those features should be avoided. [SRC]

- **Signed-challenge control channel.** A client fetches a challenge, signs it with an installation key (hardware-backed on Windows) and receives a short-lived bearer; the command bridge is routed per account, profile and mode and rejects stale connections. [SRC] This is a single-user local trust check; no reference implements a durable org-scoped grant issuer, so [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) is a Verevon requirement that borrows only the short-lived token idea. [INF]
- **Opaque credential references.** Vault fills go through the Vault extension with agent-session, policy and URL-match checks; the tool API returns summaries and opaque refs, never values. [SRC] Verevon persists typed text verbatim in receipts; [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024) takes the reference model and [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021) the attribution and scrubbing.
- **Capture-time redaction.** For durable text and ARIA snapshot capture Aside drops password and tel values and sensitive autocomplete tokens, applies label regexes (cvv, otp, pin, ssn) and value patterns (JWT, cloud keys, private-key blocks) with a Luhn check, and redacts tool arguments and error bodies in logs. [SRC] Its interactive agent snapshot redacts only non-empty password inputs. [SRC] Quarry copies AX values verbatim and its redactor has no caller; [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021) carries this and extends it to screenshots and receipts, which no reference does.
- **Steering protocol and durable suspensions.** Commands carry an idempotency id, an `afterSeq` resume cursor and an expected turn id; runs can be steered, queued or interrupted; human decisions persist on the run record, survive restarts and support "deny with instruction" and "remember this scope". [SRC] [F-t2-038](#F-t2-038) carries steering and [F-t2-033](#F-t2-033) the approval card with redirect.
- **Tab hygiene and session caps.** Tabs are owned or borrowed; a sweeper closes agent-owned tabs of idle, interrupted or aborted sessions past an idle threshold; a global run semaphore (200), a sub-agent cap (8) and a five-tab warning bound the agent. [SRC] Aside has no absolute run TTL and does not reap running runs. [SRC] [F-t2-006](#F-t2-006) adds a reaper and caps to Quarry, which has neither; [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007) takes the page hygiene.
- **Side-panel chat bound to the active tab.** `sidePanel.setOptions` is called per tab with a session id, the target session is resolved on tab activation, and a "sidecar to this page" framing is injected on the first user message. [SRC] Verevon's chat split is read-only and fed by screenshots; [F-t2-012](#F-t2-012) carries the binding.
- **Settle defaults and ref re-resolution.** After an action Aside waits for a 350 ms quiet DOM window, tracks same-site requests up to 1.2 s, dwells on SPA URL changes and caps at 8 s; stale refs are re-resolved by role, name and ordinal when unique. [SRC] [F-t2-034](#F-t2-034) carries the settle logic and notes re-resolution as an option.
- **Routines, site playbooks and code mode.** One `routine_update` tool manages cron, heartbeat and event-triggered runs; sixteen SKILL.md playbooks auto-inject by URL or keyword; one persistent QuickJS scope per session exposes a small page, tabs, snapshot and fetch surface. [SRC] These map to [F-t2-061](#F-t2-061), [F-t2-043](#F-t2-043) and [F-t2-063](#F-t2-063); the first and last are nice-to-have and need ADRs, because a script runtime fits only if every binding call stays one governed Quarry step.

### 7.3 Firecrawl, commit 11e9dc5e2 (2026-10-12)

Firecrawl is best at the acquisition contract: format breadth behind one strict options object, a mature async job lifecycle, crawl scoping with typed denials, and a governed-web-access policy model for regulated customers. [SRC] Its anti-bot, OCR, actions and browser sessions run in closed hosted services (fire-engine, Hangar), so claims about them rest on client contracts only. [SRC] Its webhook delivery, crawl idempotency, path-secret admin routes and lexical map ranking are weaker than Verevon's and should not be copied. [SRC]

- **One strict options schema with inline formats.** Every endpoint validates against a shared strict object; `formats[]` is a tagged union with per-format options; the document returns inline with effective options echoed; a per-team permit waits a bounded fraction of the timeout. [SRC] It silently defaults `skipTlsVerification` to true unless headers or actions are present, which Verevon must not copy. [SRC] [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005) carries this and makes inline return the Zero-Data-Retention (ZDR) path.
- **Crawl scoping with typed denials and discovery.** A native Rust `filter_links` returns typed denial codes for depth, scope, domain, patterns, file type and robots; TypeScript maps each code to a message naming the option to change; robots is on by default and overriding it is an enterprise entitlement; sitemaps are seeded in parallel and URL permutations are locked before enqueue with redirect-race handling. [SRC] Quarry's live crawl has no scoping and its `CrawlFrontier` has no caller; [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016) and [F-t2-041](#F-t2-041) wire the existing code.
- **Async job and long-request contracts.** `GET /crawl/:id` pages results by cursor with per-URL errors and a robots-blocked list; cancel is a signal; a synchronous request that outlives its timeout returns a `processing_continues` 408 with a job id and `Retry-After` so an identical retry re-attaches. [SRC] Firecrawl crawls have no pause or resume and its WebSocket has no resume cursor, so those parts of [F-t2-017](#F-t2-017) come from Quarry's own Temporal signals. [F-t2-040](#F-t2-040) takes the batch and wait-or-resume shape.
- **Safe Mode and tri-state team flags.** An org policy is merged in the API layer, which rejects forbidden options, and recomputed in the worker for every URL including crawl children as a backstop that downgrades instead of trusting the request; team flags hold disabled, allowed or forced per capability. [SRC] An admin can set `allowBypassSafeMode`, so it is not strictly tighten-only. [SRC] [F-t2-022](#F-t2-022) adopts the two-layer enforcement; signed claims are Verevon's own design.
- **ZDR discipline across telemetry and caches.** A sampler returns NOT_RECORD for ZDR spans, a bounded FIFO registry catches traces flagged ZDR late, a log filter drops ZDR metadata, an inline cache-admission check excludes custom headers, actions, profiles and low-fidelity engine output, and a per-feature matrix states what degrades or refuses under ZDR. [SRC] [F-t2-004](#F-t2-004), [F-t2-003](#F-t2-003) and [F-t2-048](#F-t2-048) carry these; Verevon's gateway search cache has no org or ZDR key today.
- **Engine matrix, hedged waterfall and file handoff.** Engines declare feature flags and a quality score; candidates race under per-engine timers and the first acceptable result wins; `NeedsFeature` and `DropFeature` errors re-plan under a retry budget; files are detected by a stored reference, then Content-Type, then magic bytes, and the downloaded bytes are handed to the parser without a refetch. [SRC] [F-t2-057](#F-t2-057), [F-t2-047](#F-t2-047) and [F-t2-009](#F-t2-009) carry these; the file router is a W1 quick win.
- **Monitors and structured extraction.** Change tracking normalises content before compare, emits unified and JSON field diffs and runs a goal-aware judge; the monitor scheduler adds deterministic jitter and records `skipped_overlap` and `skipped_no_credits`; json extraction normalises the schema, uses strict `json_schema` output, runs a repair pass and picks a larger model for schemas with `$ref` or `$defs`. [SRC] The scheduler drives monitors, not generic jobs. [SRC] [F-t2-030](#F-t2-030), [F-t2-018](#F-t2-018) and [F-t2-019](#F-t2-019) carry these.
- **Tenant fairness, metering and quality loops.** A per-tenant backlog with expiry and an in-flight priority penalty returns 429 only on overflow; one pure pricing function makes failures free; a URL reputation gate runs before the first engine and on every redirect; a hermetic test site, record-and-replay fixtures and a CI matrix gate releases; a finished agent run exports as a skill. [SRC] These map to [F-t2-025](#F-t2-025), [F-t2-046](#F-t2-046), [F-t2-049](#F-t2-049), [F-t2-044](#F-t2-044) and [F-t2-051](#F-t2-051). Cached generated extractors ([F-t2-065](#F-t2-065)) were demoted to nice-to-have because Verevon should generate declarative profiles, not code.

### 7.4 Apify platform, public docs, October 2026

Apify is best as a platform substrate: least-privilege by construction, schema-driven job units, per-run storages with export, and cooperative budgets between platform and workload. [DOC] All evidence is documentation, so these are shapes to adopt, not observed code. [DOC] Its marketplace, link-by-id default sharing, unsigned webhooks and retention-only data controls conflict with Verevon's rules and are not recommended. [DOC]

- **Least-privilege tokens with scope propagation.** Tools default to limited scope, broad scopes need explicit human approval, agents are excluded from high-privilege tools, and scope propagates from caller to run to webhook. [DOC] [F-t2-036](#F-t2-036) adopts this for durable, tenant-scoped grants.
- **Charge-within-cap budgets.** The caller sets a maximum total charge; before each billable event the executor asks how many units remain chargeable; at the cap the platform aborts the run. [DOC] Quarry enforces only `max_steps`; [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008) combines this with Firecrawl's settle-once-under-row-lock for browser time.
- **Browser pool lifecycle.** A new page goes to the first active browser below `maxOpenPagesPerBrowser`; a browser retires after N pages and closes after 300 s of inactivity. [DOC] Quarry relaunches its single Chromium on any viewport change; [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007) carries this (W1 quick win).
- **Learned per-site rendering mode.** The adaptive crawler runs HTTP first, samples a fraction of requests in both modes, compares results and feeds a per-site predictor. [DOC] [F-t2-057](#F-t2-057) adds the learned mode to Quarry's planner.
- **Schema-driven job units and per-run storages.** One manifest declares input and output schemas; the same schema renders the form, validates API input and becomes the MCP tool definition; tasks are saved inputs that can be run, scheduled and shared; each run gets a dataset, key-value store and request queue with server-side export. [DOC] [F-t2-053](#F-t2-053) exposes Quarry's inert presets this way and [F-t2-052](#F-t2-052) adds result sets.
- **Hosted MCP with OAuth.** A Streamable-HTTP MCP endpoint behind OAuth loads tool groups on demand and maps every call to the same governed path as in-product calls, beside a full OpenAPI. [DOC] Verevon's OpenAPI covers 46 paths and nothing serves MCP; [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064) carries this but needs an ADR because the repo documents a deferral.
- **Secret inputs encrypted per user and tool.** A random AES-256-GCM key encrypts the value and is wrapped with an RSA key unique to the user and Actor; only ciphertext reaches logs. [DOC] This covers input fields, not browser profiles, so [F-t2-020](#F-t2-020) borrows it as a security baseline rather than parity.
- **Operations and isolation.** Webhooks carry a rich event vocabulary, payload templates and ad hoc per-run hooks; alerts fire on run status and dataset field statistics; each run gets an isolated container (technology undisclosed). [DOC] These feed [F-t2-031](#F-t2-031), [F-t2-029](#F-t2-029) and [F-t2-060](#F-t2-060); the last is an XL bet gated on the substrate ADR.

## 8. What Verevon already does well (protect it)

Verevon is behind on the acquisition contract and the shared browser shell, but ahead of every reference on egress containment, governed execution, evidence and ZDR strictness, and it is the only system in this audit that combines a crawl engine with an interactive agent browser. [SRC]

- **Search.** The multi-provider router (Tantivy, Stract, SearXNG, Brave, Serper and Data Plane vectors) with hybrid rerank and ZDR gating beats every reference; paid providers are never used under ZDR (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/search_routes.rs:50-160; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/smart_router.rs:39-60). [SRC] Do not regress: keep the org-keyed `SearchCache` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/cache.rs:108-125) and fix only the gateway cache in front of it ([F-t2-003](#F-t2-003)).
- **SSRF and egress containment.** DNS is resolved once and pinned, redirects are re-validated per hop, each browser session gets a loopback DNS-pinned egress proxy with QUIC disabled, and a CDP Fetch guard denies by default (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/dns_guard.rs:104; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/fetch.rs:193-217; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:186-243, 706-775). [SRC] Do not regress: extend it to tenant webhooks ([F-t2-031](#F-t2-031)) and require the same proof from remote providers ([F-t2-059](#F-t2-059)).
- **Governed execution.** One action per `/step`; the broker grant is validated before every action, broker domains replace any client list, domains are checked before and after each action, and agent runs are refused unless the driver proves isolated egress (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:2257-2266, 1397-1412, 2294-2400, 1131-1141). [SRC] Do not regress: the grant redesign across [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001), [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011), [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) and [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032) must keep "the Model proposes, Quarry executes or rejects".
- **One-shot sensitive approvals.** Dialog, upload and download actions need a fresh single-use grant scoped to the exact frame, dialog or artifact, and dialogs are never auto-answered (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:135-312; apps/Model Plane/go/services/browser-broker/internal/grant/grant.go:32-36, 104-125). [SRC] Stricter than Aside's auto-accept. Do not regress: [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024) and [F-t2-039](#F-t2-039) must reuse this machinery.
- **Risk gates enforced in code.** Login, checkout, posting, destructive, cross-domain and persistent-cookie risks combine model self-report with a deterministic backstop and fail closed; web tool results are classified and screened unconditionally; the gateway denies `evaluate` for UI sessions (apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/browser_agent/browser_risk.rs; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/execution-core/src/provenance.rs:1-60; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:3751-3755). [SRC] Do not regress: keep enforcement in code when adding per-origin consent ([F-t2-032](#F-t2-032)).
- **Evidence.** Immutable actor-scoped step receipts, checkpoints, proof bundles, a cursor timeline, an event stream with `from_seq` replay, and procedure compile with replay, quality and impact checks (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/step_receipts.rs:183-560; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/browser_procedure.rs:61; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:2466-2538, 2658). [SRC] No reference matches this. Do not regress: surface it ([F-t2-014](#F-t2-014)) and add a store ([F-t2-051](#F-t2-051)) rather than rebuild it.
- **ZDR strictness.** Durable crawl and batch are refused, persistent profiles and resume are refused, events reach live subscribers only, artifacts are withheld, and model-gateway refuses browser runs under issuer-enforced ZDR (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:575-588; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:527-534, 1347-1353; apps/Model Plane/rust/services/model-gateway/src/browser_run.rs:307-315; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:3846-3870). [SRC] Stricter than any reference's incognito mode. Do not regress: check every new cache, monitor, inline payload or telemetry path against the ZDR matrix ([F-t2-003](#F-t2-003)).
- **Observation and locators.** Refs are bound to native CDP backend node ids, actions verify and act atomically, stale or ambiguous targets fail closed with `TARGET_REPAIR_REQUIRED`, semantic locators and frame refs cover cross-process frames, and console, network and lifecycle collectors run at CDP level so pages cannot tamper with them (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:1086-1200, 1232, 1446-1610; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/observation.rs:1019; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-core/src/contracts.rs:640-827). [SRC] Do not regress: [F-t2-015](#F-t2-015) and [F-t2-034](#F-t2-034) add checks on this contract, not a second vocabulary.
- **Politeness and durable orchestration.** A per-host AIMD scheduler and a global autoscaled pool are wired into every page fetch; a Postgres SKIP LOCKED queue plus Temporal workflows give durability with deterministic job idempotency (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/pipeline.rs:330-400; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/postgres_queue.rs:74). [SRC] Do not regress: no second queue store; feed robots Crawl-delay into the existing scheduler ([F-t2-016](#F-t2-016)).
- **Webhook delivery and error envelope.** Delivery is HMAC-signed with backoff from 1 s to 1 h, five attempts and a dead-letter queue; the error envelope carries retryable codes, `retry_after_seconds` and `next_actions` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-control/internal/dispatcher/dispatcher.go:25-34; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/api_error.rs:11-26; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-core/src/error.rs:10-50). [SRC] Firecrawl's in-process webhook path shows no retry. [SRC] Do not regress: [F-t2-031](#F-t2-031) wraps the dispatcher with org scoping instead of replacing it.
- **Production defaults.** Production refuses to start with ephemeral stores or a default control key, Chromium is pinned, and internal control calls use HMAC rather than path secrets (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/main.rs:690-704; apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/services/quarry-control/cmd/control/main.go:63-117). [SRC] Firecrawl's admin surface uses a secret in the URL path. [SRC] Do not regress: never adopt a secret-in-path gate.
- **Integration Plane.** integration-corev2 holds an AES-GCM token vault, a short-lived internal token broker, about 142 named provider operations with signed write attestations and mandatory idempotency keys, and a raw provider proxy that returns 410 (apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/crypto/vault.go:16-20; apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/actions/service.go:164; apps/Ingestion Plane/integration-corev2/internal/api/legacy_routes.go:69-74). [SRC] This is the shape Apify documents for its MCP connectors. [DOC] Do not regress: [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024) keeps secrets in the vault and lets Quarry resolve references only.
- **MCP client hardening and provisioning.** Outbound MCP connections use no redirects, DNS pinning per dial, SSRF re-validation of discovery and token hops and a mandatory exact tool allowlist; provisioning is standards-based SCIM through Control Plane principals. [SRC] Do not regress: an MCP server ([F-t2-064](#F-t2-064)) must reuse the same allowlist discipline.
- **Browser profiles with governance.** Profiles have scopes, a restore probe and an always-visible ZDR chip, and ZDR forbids a persistent profile at run creation on both the gateway and Quarry sides (agent_routes.rs:527-533), although Quarry's `/v1/profiles` routes themselves check org id only, with no ZDR or owner check, and `GET` returns raw cookie values ([F-t2-020](#F-t2-020)) (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/profile_routes.rs:102-432; apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:147-185). [SRC] Do not regress: keep scope separation and never widen an `org_shared` profile through a Space; [F-t2-020](#F-t2-020) adds encryption under the same store.
- **Bulk plus interactive.** Crawl, map, batch and schedules sit beside the interactive agent browser, which neither Codex nor Aside has. [SRC] Do not regress: keep bulk acquisition out of the agent's tab loop and offer it to the Model as approval-gated jobs ([F-t2-042](#F-t2-042)).
- **Takeover authority, with one correction.** Human input switches the session to takeover and agent actions get 409, but that check lives in the gateway (apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/browser.rs:663-759); Quarry's `/step` never reads `control_mode` (apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:2227-2294). [SRC] Protect the gateway check until phase 1 of [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) moves enforcement into Quarry, then keep it there.

## 9. Roadmap and decisions

Tiers and efforts below are the verified values. Six items moved from Recommended to Nice-to-have during verification (F-t2-054, 057, 061, 063, 064, 065) but keep their wave; 33 efforts were revised, mostly upward. ZDR means Zero-Data-Retention. ADR means Architecture Decision Record.

### 9.1 Implementation waves

**W1: unblock and make safe.** Goal: make every browser path work in production mode, close the ZDR and tenant leaks, gate raw script, stop resource leaks and relaunches, and ship the inline scrape contract and file routing. Phase 1 of [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) (takeover enforced inside Quarry, S) also lands here.

### 9.2 Quick wins

These items are quick wins because they are wiring or deletion inside one plane with no unbuilt cross-plane dependency. Where the verified effort is M, the first slice is S.

- [F-t2-002](#F-t2-002) (S): a default-deny gate on `/step` for `AgentAction::Evaluate`; the isolated-world evaluator moves to [F-t2-023](#F-t2-023).
- [F-t2-003](#F-t2-003) (phase 1 is S): one gateway file, `apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/search.rs:195-231`, plus a test, reusing the private `CacheAdmission` in `apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/apps/gateway/src/domains/knowledge/quarry.rs:213-280`.
- [F-t2-004](#F-t2-004) (phase 1 is S): remove the URL field logged at `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/change_routes.rs:632-637`, sweep the tracing call sites, add a lint.
- [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005) (M for `/v1/scrape` only): formats, main-content stage and inline return reuse the existing pipeline.
- [F-t2-006](#F-t2-006) (M): a sweeper and caps at `start_run`; the fleet console proxy is a separate S.
- [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007) (part A is S): per-page viewport emulation removes the cross-session kill at `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/chromiumoxide.rs:194-223`.
- [F-t2-009](#F-t2-009) (S): the sniffer and both parsers exist; only PDF and DOCX routing needs wiring, since there is no image parser to route to.
- [F-t2-026](#F-t2-026) (S): one `cdp_call` wrapper inside quarry-browser.
- [F-t2-027](#F-t2-027) (deletion is S; full item M): `stealth.rs` has no caller, so removing it is safe.
- [F-t2-045](#F-t2-045) (skip-only slice is S): a `save_memory` preflight in execution-core plus a Dreaming window filter.
- Phase 1 of [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) (S): reject agent-principal steps while `control_mode` is HumanTakeover in `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:2222-2310`.
- Two fixes surfaced by verification: the compose default at `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/deploy/compose/docker-compose.yml:97-99` disables the Chromium sandbox when `QUARRY_BROWSER_NO_SANDBOX` is unset ([F-t2-060](#F-t2-060) step 1), and `WebPageViewer` at `apps/Frontend Plane/verevonv3/src/features/dashboard/home/DashboardSearchResults.tsx:147-192` is dead code that loads third-party pages outside Quarry's egress controls ([F-t2-071](#F-t2-071) slice a).

### 9.3 Decisions needed before building (ADR candidates)

1. **Web policy of record and tighten-only claims.** Question: does org web policy live in Control Plane org-core (delivered as signed claims) or in Model Plane capability-core (delivered as grant fields)? Items: [F-t2-022](#F-t2-022), [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032), [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008), [F-t2-025](#F-t2-025). Options: org-core claims, which already carry the ZDR floor but reach only user-token callers; or capability-core grants, which already have tenant-bound org-scope grants. Recommendation: service-token callers ignore the zdr claim by design (`apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/auth.rs:56-140`), so policy must travel by claim for user paths and by grant field for Model-driven runs whichever owner wins; ship the owner-neutral Quarry-only `ResolvedPolicy` slice first.
2. **Secret custody for browser credential use.** Question: who holds user secrets used inside pages, and does every email-OTP use need approval? Items: [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024), [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021). Options: extend integration-corev2 (today an at-rest token vault, not a user-secret store, with a shared internal API key) or create a Control Plane secret service. Recommendation: ship OTP first through the existing sensitive-approval path and defer password references until the ADR, as the verification suggests.
3. **Shared human+agent sessions.** Question: how does a grant bind a session that both a human and a Model run use, and what navigation policy governs free human browsing? Items: [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001), [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011), [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013), [F-t2-054](#F-t2-054). Options: a broker human-browse grant kind with renewal; a delegation claim so a service principal can attach to a user-owned run; or end and resume with a new grant. No reference implements takeover enforcement in the executor, so this is a Verevon design [INF]. Recommendation: design the three together; the data supports nothing more specific.
4. **ZDR semantics per feature.** Question: for monitors, URL caches, schedules, durable crawls and telemetry, does ZDR mean refused, inline-only or hash-only? Items: [F-t2-003](#F-t2-003), [F-t2-004](#F-t2-004), [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016), [F-t2-018](#F-t2-018), [F-t2-030](#F-t2-030), [F-t2-048](#F-t2-048). Today crawl, batch and schedules are refused under ZDR (`apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/routes.rs:563-588`). Recommendation: publish the matrix generated from route attributes rather than hand-written JSON; do not loosen existing refusals.
5. **Anti-bot posture.** Question: is honest identity official policy, and which policy-gated escalation (proxy rotation, TLS impersonation tiers) is allowed? Items: [F-t2-027](#F-t2-027), [F-t2-066](#F-t2-066), [F-t2-067](#F-t2-067), [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016), [F-t2-058](#F-t2-058). The live `block_rotation` already rotates proxies on bare 401/403/407/503, so the stance must cover shipped behaviour, not only the dead `stealth.rs`. Recommendation: confirm the honest posture and narrow rotation triggers with [F-t2-027](#F-t2-027).
6. **Script execution model.** Question: may the Model submit programs (evaluate, scrape action lists, code mode, generated extractors) if Quarry still validates every binding call? Items: [F-t2-002](#F-t2-002), [F-t2-023](#F-t2-023), [F-t2-063](#F-t2-063), [F-t2-065](#F-t2-065). Options: keep one governed action per call; or allow declarative capped action lists and a sandboxed script runtime whose every binding is a separate Quarry step. Recommendation: declarative lists and profiles fit the rule; code mode should be a slice of the generic code-mode already planned in Model Plane docs [DOC].
7. **External MCP exposure.** Question: will Verevon expose Quarry and read-only connectors to third-party agents, reversing the documented deferral in `apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/docs/OSS_PARITY_BACKLOG.md:30`? Items: [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064), [F-t2-042](#F-t2-042). Options: stay REST-only; or add an audience-bound MCP front outside Quarry with customer API keys and audit routed through existing producers. Recommendation: do the OpenAPI-from-types slice now and gate the rest on an owner decision.
8. **Run trace envelope and sharing.** Question: which envelope is canonical, and how do Application Plane projections and Space grants avoid widening the owning plane's ACL? Items: [F-t2-014](#F-t2-014), [F-t2-038](#F-t2-038), [F-t2-072](#F-t2-072). Recommendation: extend the existing `model_plane.v1.Event` (reserved fields 14-20 in `apps/Model Plane/proto/model_plane/v1/events.proto:8-60`) rather than add a second envelope; run sharing needs a resource-taxonomy decision because `quarry_run` is org-team-shared today.
9. **Automation owner.** Question: do completion-triggered follow-ups and agent routines belong to Application Plane or Model Plane routines, and how are unattended browser runs authorized per fire? Items: [F-t2-031](#F-t2-031), [F-t2-061](#F-t2-061). Constraint: unattended runs are draft-only through Wave 2 by owner ruling, and the scheduled lane cannot borrow a human's delegated bearer. Recommendation: keep acquisition-only automation on Quarry schedules; agent routines wait for the pause/resume design.
10. **Browser execution substrate.** Question: shared Chromium pool, per-session containers or microVMs, remote providers, or a user-browser extension? Items: [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007), [F-t2-059](#F-t2-059), [F-t2-060](#F-t2-060), [F-t2-077](#F-t2-077). Constraint: the pinned egress proxy type is loopback-only (`apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-browser/src/lib.rs:331-360`), so remote or containerised Chromium needs either a sidecar proxy or a reviewed relaxation. Recommendation: per-page emulation and the sandbox default now; CDP-connect worker pool next; the extension stays parked by the 2026-09-28 owner ruling.

### 9.4 Dependency notes

1. [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001) gates every browser item: production Quarry rejects runs without a grant (`apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/main.rs:182-200`) and nothing issues one, so [F-t2-010](#F-t2-010), [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011), [F-t2-012](#F-t2-012), [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) phase 2, [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024), [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032) and [F-t2-063](#F-t2-063) cannot be tested end to end before it.
2. [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005) is the second foundation: [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016), [F-t2-019](#F-t2-019), [F-t2-023](#F-t2-023), [F-t2-030](#F-t2-030), [F-t2-037](#F-t2-037), [F-t2-040](#F-t2-040) and [F-t2-050](#F-t2-050) extend its options or formats.
3. The policy ADR (9.3 item 1) must precede [F-t2-022](#F-t2-022), which in turn supplies the claim channel for [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008), [F-t2-025](#F-t2-025), [F-t2-046](#F-t2-046), [F-t2-048](#F-t2-048) and [F-t2-058](#F-t2-058).
4. [F-t2-010](#F-t2-010) (planner on by default) must wait for budgets ([F-t2-008](#F-t2-008)) and capture-time redaction ([F-t2-021](#F-t2-021)), because the planner prompt receives raw observation text today.
5. [F-t2-016](#F-t2-016) scope must land before [F-t2-018](#F-t2-018) switches schedules on, or recurring crawls repeat the off-site walk; [F-t2-018](#F-t2-018) also needs an explicit re-enable so existing rows do not all start firing.
6. [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021) redaction precedes any `fill_secret` in [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024), since non-ZDR receipts store typed text verbatim (`apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-runtime/src/step_receipts.rs:185-200`).
7. [F-t2-019](#F-t2-019), [F-t2-030](#F-t2-030) phase 2, [F-t2-037](#F-t2-037) and [F-t2-065](#F-t2-065) all wait for the model-gateway grant to quarry-edge, which is `granted:false` in `apps/Control Plane/auth-core/src/auth/fixtures/cross-plane-token-requests.json:40-46` and has no owner.
8. [F-t2-004](#F-t2-004) phase 2 and [F-t2-029](#F-t2-029) are mutually ordered: the sampler needs spans, and spans must not be exported for ZDR runs before the sampler exists; ship the log-field sweep first.
9. [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007) part A precedes [F-t2-020](#F-t2-020) restore hooks, [F-t2-058](#F-t2-058) emulation and both [F-t2-059](#F-t2-059) and [F-t2-060](#F-t2-060).
10. [F-t2-035](#F-t2-035) and [F-t2-054](#F-t2-054) must share one session state machine; decide whether a chat thread keeps one long-lived Quarry run across turns before building either.

## 10. Risks and open questions

### 10.1 Main risks

1. **Grant redesign blast radius (authority).** [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001), [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011), [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) and [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032) all change what a grant means; single-action child grants and the per-step domain-equality check (`apps/Ingestion Plane/Quarry-v2/crates/quarry-edge/src/agent_routes.rs:314-339`) do not support the per-origin extension the items assume.
2. **Cross-tenant exposure (security).** The gateway search cache is keyed without org and feeds org-scoped private-corpus providers ([F-t2-003](#F-t2-003)); webhooks have no `org_id` and dial without address vetting ([F-t2-031](#F-t2-031)); all sessions share one Chromium and scrape sessions are keyed by host only ([F-t2-060](#F-t2-060), INF); the fingerprint-keyed PageCache is not org-scoped ([F-t2-048](#F-t2-048), to verify).
3. **ZDR regressions (data governance).** Every new cache, diff, inline payload, span or trace projection is a new leak path: [F-t2-003](#F-t2-003), [F-t2-004](#F-t2-004), [F-t2-014](#F-t2-014), [F-t2-023](#F-t2-023), [F-t2-030](#F-t2-030), [F-t2-048](#F-t2-048). Telemetry already logs a source URL inside a ZDR branch.
4. **Evidence captures secrets (security).** AX snapshots, receipts, screenshots and the planner prompt carry field values verbatim until [F-t2-021](#F-t2-021) lands; [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024) and [F-t2-010](#F-t2-010) widen the exposure if sequenced first.
5. **Plane-ownership drift (architecture).** Policy ([F-t2-022](#F-t2-022)), capacity ([F-t2-025](#F-t2-025)), automation ([F-t2-031](#F-t2-031), [F-t2-061](#F-t2-061)), trace projection ([F-t2-014](#F-t2-014), [F-t2-072](#F-t2-072)) and isolation ([F-t2-060](#F-t2-060)) each had a proposed option that would add cross-plane DB access or let Spaces widen access; verification dropped them, but they will resurface.
6. **Unbounded cost (commercial).** Only `max_steps` is enforced ([F-t2-008](#F-t2-008)); planner-on ([F-t2-010](#F-t2-010)), per-page extraction ([F-t2-019](#F-t2-019)), hydration ([F-t2-042](#F-t2-042)) and profile escalation ([F-t2-065](#F-t2-065)) multiply spend before enforcement exists.
7. **Compliance and posture (data governance).** Crawl follows external links with no robots check ([F-t2-016](#F-t2-016)); the dead consent script clicks the affirmative accept button, granting tracking consent on the user's behalf ([F-t2-066](#F-t2-066)); proxy rotation on bare 403 sits in tension with the no-bypass rule ([F-t2-027](#F-t2-027), [F-t2-058](#F-t2-058)).
8. **Human-browsing regressions (delivery).** Enforcing runtime and cost budgets in `/step` would cap manual sessions, which the gateway creates with `max_steps 12` and `max_runtime_s 120` ([F-t2-008](#F-t2-008), [F-t2-011](#F-t2-011)); budgets must key on the initiator from [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) phase 1.
9. **Stale docs steer planning (delivery).** Docs mark grant issuance COMPLETE, formats done, budgets enforced, DOCX shipped and an adblock task fixed; none holds in code ([F-t2-001](#F-t2-001), [F-t2-005](#F-t2-005), [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008), [F-t2-037](#F-t2-037), [F-t2-066](#F-t2-066)).
10. **Effort growth (delivery).** 33 efforts were revised (29 up, 4 down), six items were demoted, and the XL items [F-t2-032](#F-t2-032), [F-t2-036](#F-t2-036), [F-t2-058](#F-t2-058), [F-t2-059](#F-t2-059) and [F-t2-060](#F-t2-060) each depend on an ADR; without the ADRs they stall.

### 10.2 Open questions

1. Which plane owns web and browser capability policy, and how is a policy claim revoked mid-run when grants live 15 minutes?
2. For a human browsing session, is navigation governed by a broker policy kind, a renewed grant, or both, and what idle timeout replaces the 120 s run TTL?
3. Can smart-router search results include org-private corpus hits? The verification says yes for Quarry's `/v1/search` providers, which makes the gateway cache a cross-tenant incident rather than only a ZDR gap; confirm the hit rate and whether any such result was ever served across orgs.
4. What does ZDR mean for monitors, schedules, durable crawls and URL caches: refused, inline-only or hash-only?
5. Is the honest anti-bot posture official? If so, delete `stealth.rs`, retire the fingerprint-rotation backlog item, and narrow `block_rotation` triggers.
6. Will Verevon expose Quarry and connectors over MCP to third-party agents, and under which customer authentication model?
7. Who owns secrets used inside pages, and must every email-OTP read be approved and shown with origin and connection?
8. Do enterprise tenants need a container or VM per browser session, and is a sidecar proxy an acceptable relaxation of the loopback-only egress type?
9. Are code-mode control and generated extraction profiles acceptable when Quarry validates every call, and should Python-over-RPC be the first engine?
10. Which claims need runtime confirmation first: the AI-path 401/403 failures, how often the viewport relaunch fires in production, whether Chromium already masks password values in the accessibility tree, and whether same-host scrape sessions from different orgs share a session key?
11. Should manual browsing be exempt from robots.txt, and who may hold the audited robots-override entitlement?
12. Does a chat thread keep one long-lived Quarry run across turns, or one run per Model Plane run as today? This decides [F-t2-054](#F-t2-054) and [F-t2-035](#F-t2-035).
13. Should `GET /v1/profiles/:id` ever return raw cookie values, and who may call it once an owner check exists?
14. Who takes ownership of the org-bound metered Model Plane invoke path (DEF-QB-T17-PART2) that four items wait on?
15. Should stale docs be retired wave by wave and added to `apps/STALE_DOC_DELETION_REGISTER.md`?

### 10.3 Evidence limits that matter for decisions

- All Apify evidence is public documentation [DOC]. Browser-pool lifecycle, per-run containers, charge-within-cap budgets and hosted MCP are described, not inspected, so [F-t2-007](#F-t2-007), [F-t2-060](#F-t2-060), [F-t2-008](#F-t2-008) and [F-t2-064](#F-t2-064) borrow shapes, not implementations.
- Firecrawl's hosted parts (fire-engine, Hangar sessions, the run-trace producer) are seen only through client contracts in the open-source repo [SRC for contracts, INF for behaviour]. [F-t2-001](#F-t2-001), [F-t2-014](#F-t2-014), [F-t2-020](#F-t2-020) and [F-t2-035](#F-t2-035) cite contracts observed, implementation unknown.
- Codex credential handoff is wired to the hosted Codex Cloud Browser, not the shipped desktop in-app browser, and the connector-side OTP fetch is inferred from elicitation text [SRC, INF]. [F-t2-024](#F-t2-024) rests on a cloud-only variant plus Aside's vault fill.
- Several Codex and Aside behaviours are plugin or prompt text, not code: connector-first routing and fail-closed tab-mention resolution [DOC]; Aside takeover handling is an absence claim from static analysis [INF]. [F-t2-010](#F-t2-010), [F-t2-013](#F-t2-013) and [F-t2-028](#F-t2-028) must not cite these as enforced mechanisms.
- Nothing in Verevon was run. Every "fails in production" statement, including the AI-path 401/403, the relaunch frequency and same-host session sharing, is inference from code reading [INF] and should be reproduced before fixes are scoped.
- Prevalence counts were wrong for 14 items before correction; where a correction gave no new count, this document states none.

## Appendix A. Method, sources and limits

### A.1 What was analysed

| Source | Version or commit | Kind | How it was analysed | Feature records |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Codex desktop app (in-app browser, computer use) | 26.1007.2314.0 | Installed app | REA on the app.asar and native binaries; the pre-extracted bundle and plugin scripts were beautified and read directly | 261 |
| Aside browser | 1.0.928.2 | Installed app | REA native, PE and string analysis; no JavaScript bundle for the daemon, so the embedded payload and extension sources were read directly | 187 |
| Firecrawl | commit 11e9dc5e2 (2026-10-12) | Open-source repository at C:/dev/firecrawl | Source and in-repo docs | 226 |
| Apify platform | public docs, October 2026 | Public documentation | Public web docs only; every Apify claim is DOC | 112 |
| Verevon (CoresSystem) | commit afb83928 (2026-10-10) | Git monorepo at C:/dev/CoresSystem | Code read as ground truth; repo docs used only for intent | 260 baseline capabilities |

The four references together yielded 786 feature records. Prevalence statements use N = 4, except the section 2 tables for the two browser categories, which use N = 3 because Apify has no browser shell to compare.

### A.2 Process

The work ran in seven steps.

1. **Inventory.** Each source was mapped first: installed files, bundles, plugins, manifests and locale strings for the two apps; the repository layout for Firecrawl; the documentation tree for Apify. The inventories were later corrected by the deep dives where the code disagreed with them.
2. **Deep dives.** Each source was split into two areas (Codex: in-app browser, automation and safety; Aside: browser product, agent-control architecture; Firecrawl: API surface, engine and infrastructure; Apify: platform) and turned into structured feature records, each with a mechanism, an evidence location (file and line, byte offset, or URL), a provenance tag and a confidence level.
3. **Code-verified baseline.** Two baselines described what Verevon actually has, one for Quarry v2 and integration-corev2 and one for the browser application path across the Model and Frontend planes. Each capability was tagged shipped, partial, stub or absent from the code, not the docs.
4. **Cross-source comparison.** The 786 records were grouped into 322 clusters across ten categories (acquisition API surface; output formats and content processing; rendering and browser engines; anti-bot, proxies and reliability; orchestration and scale; platform and ecosystem; observability and operations; in-app browser shell; AI-browser control; integration-plane overlap). Each cluster records which references have the capability, the best implementation, and Verevon's baseline status.
5. **Consolidation.** Per-category candidate recommendations were merged across categories (for example, production browser grants, crawl scoping and budget enforcement were each raised in three categories) into 77 items. Two candidates were dropped: design extraction (niche) and a Slack entry point (pre-empts the future Channel Plane).
6. **Tier calibration.** The category consolidators had proposed 121 candidates, 44 of them in Priority; the tiering pass cut Priority to 24 by merging duplicates and demoting items that do not block the vision, assigned waves, and listed the ADR topics.
7. **Two independent verification passes.** A Verevon-side pass read the code behind every one of the 77 items and recorded the exact gap, evidence paths, reusable parts, constraints, an adjusted approach and a revised effort. A reference-side pass re-read the evidence behind 24 items to refute or correct the stated reference behaviour and prevalence counts. The verified data supersedes the originals throughout this document.

Model tiers by job: inventories ran on Claude Haiku 5.5 at maximum effort; deep dives on Claude Sonnet 5.5 at extra-high effort; baselines and comparison on Claude Opus 5.5 at high effort; web research and writing on Claude Fable 5.1 at medium effort.

### A.3 How REA was used, and its limits

REA (reverse-engineering analysis) was run only on the two installed apps. Its contribution was smaller than planned, and the provenance tags reflect that honestly.

- **JavaScript analysis was partial.** On Codex, `analyze_javascript_application` parsed the plugin scripts and the computer-use modules without parse failures, but the semantic analysis of the two large bundles (`browser-service.mjs` and `browser-client.mjs`) exhausted the worker heap at the 2048 MB limit, so the dependency graph is partial and Electron surfaces reported zero [REA]. On Aside, the same tool hit the heap limit on the extension's `background.js` and `content.js` [REA].
- **Feature tracing returned no matches on strings that exist.** `trace_application_feature` seeds for literals and APIs present in the sources returned no-match on both apps because the partial graph does not index string literals in these minified bundles; absence of a match was never treated as evidence of absence [REA].
- **Native sessions failed on the MSIX install path.** Opening `codex-computer-use.exe` directly under `WindowsApps` was refused by the binary loader (Win32 access denied); a scratch copy opened, but `search_strings` timed out twice at 330 s [REA]. On Aside, `search_strings` on `aside-native.node` also timed out at 330 s [REA]. `inspect_pe_resources` worked on both apps and contributed manifest and resource facts, for example the `asInvoker` execution level of the Codex sandbox setup executable [REA].
- **Consequence.** Most app evidence is tagged SRC: beautified copies of the shipped bundles (names preserved, line numbers refer to the copies), byte-offset reads of the Aside daemon payload, parsed locale paks, extension sources and ASCII string tables of the native executables. No code was executed and no disassembly was done; native facts are string-table facts only [SRC]. Where a record rests on strings alone, its confidence is medium and the record says so.

### A.4 Limits that affect confidence

- **Server-side behaviour is invisible.** Codex's reviewer prompts, site-status reputation service, cloud browser, cloud broker and Chrome extension live outside the package; only their client contracts were seen, so their behaviour is DOC or INF [SRC]. Aside's remote-control trust rests on a cloud relay that is not visible locally [INF]. Firecrawl's hosted parts (fire-engine, FirePDF, Hangar, smart-scrape, the privacy service and the index database) are closed; what they do is known only through the open-source client code, so any "Firecrawl can do X" claim that is not in the repo is hosted-only [SRC].
- **Apify is public-docs-only.** No Apify code was read, so every Apify claim is DOC, and the docs themselves carry inconsistencies (for example two different free-plan concurrency figures) [DOC].
- **Presence of code is not proof of enablement.** Several Codex features are flag-gated (accessibility-tree mode in the in-app browser, raw CDP access, history, WebMCP), and client defaults stay off until server values load, so parity targets may not be fully shipped in the audited build [SRC]. Feature flags and rollout state in all four references are invisible.
- **Nothing was executed or launched.** No reference app, installer, test, build or dev server was run, and nothing in Verevon was run. Every "fails in production" statement about Verevon is an inference from reading the code and is tagged INF where it is not a direct code fact. Items that still need runtime confirmation are listed in the open questions.
- **Reference code evolves.** Firecrawl is pinned to one commit and the apps to one build; the in-repo Firecrawl OpenAPI is already stale against its own code, and SDKs drift from the API [SRC].
- **Licence caution.** The Firecrawl API is AGPL-3.0 and its Python SDK is MIT, so this document recommends re-implementing observed designs, never copying code; this is an engineering caution, not legal advice [SRC].
- **No user data was read.** Per-user data directories, cookies, local storage, tokens and real secrets were excluded for all apps; a few shipped files that carry key material were noted and not recorded.
- **Repo docs are stale.** Verevon docs claim robots handling, format parity, DOCX support and budget enforcement are done and mark the browser workspace plan complete; the code contradicts each claim. Code was treated as ground truth throughout.
